## Commands & Controls Reference

*Living section — update this whenever a command or keybind is added, removed, or rebound.*

This is now also shown in-game (Escape → Controls & Commands, added 2026-09-17,
Session 45) as a read-only new-player reference — keybind remapping is planned but
not built yet, so `pause_menu.gd`'s `KEYBIND_GROUPS`/`CHAT_COMMANDS` tables are a
second copy of this list rather than reading it from here. Keep both in sync when
either changes until remapping unifies them.

### Chat Commands (type in the chat box at the bottom of the log window, or the NPC dialogue box)
Commands work Linux-shell-style: type any unambiguous prefix and it resolves to the
full command (added 2026-09-14, Session 42) — e.g. `/fol` or `/foll` both run `/follow`,
`/loc` runs `/location`, since no other command starts with those letters. If a typed
prefix matches more than one command it prints "Ambiguous command" and lists the
candidates instead of guessing; a typo that matches nothing prints "Unknown command".
- `/location` (or any unique prefix, e.g. `/loc`) — prints your current X/Y/Z position
  to the General log tab. Renamed from the old bare `/loc` command name (2026-09-14) —
  `/loc` still works, it's now just an abbreviation rather than the literal command.
- `/time` — prints the in-game date/day-night phase and real-world time.
- `/hail` — hails the nearest guard NPC in range (same as pressing H).
- `/appraise` — Insight Check on your current target (same as pressing I).
- `/follow [name]` (added 2026-09-14, Session 42) — follow an NPC or player around the
  zone, e.g. `/follow Sergeant Bryn` to tag along on his patrol. With no name, follows
  your current target (Tab/left-click) instead. Typing `/follow` again on whoever you're
  already following stops following them (toggle). Stops automatically if you press any
  movement key (W/S/A/D/Q/E/turn/jump — taking the wheel manually always overrides it),
  or if the target becomes invalid (despawns, etc). Steers using the same self-managed
  `NavigationServer3D.query_path()` approach guard_npc.gd's patrol system uses (Session
  39/41) rather than `NavigationAgent3D`, for the same long-distance-reliability reasons.
  Stops 3m short of the target rather than walking into them. See `player3d.gd`:
  `try_follow()`, `start_following()`, `stop_following()`, `_handle_follow_movement()`.

- `/who` (added 2026-09-19) — lists every player in the world with level, class and zone (every zone since 2026-09-25).
- `/pet` (added 2026-09-19) — pet your targeted cat, or the nearest one within 4m (Kenji, Oni).
  Note: `/p` is now ambiguous between /party and /pet (use /pa or /pe, or the full names).
- `/weather rain|clear` (added 2026-09-19, host / single-player only) — start or stop rain; it
  otherwise rains on its own, randomly and rarely.
- `/raid [bandits|goblins]` (added 2026-09-21, host / single-player only) — start a gate raid now (they otherwise come by themselves, every 25-45 minutes
  while someone is near the town gate). Listed in Escape -> Controls & Commands too.
- Chat channels (added 2026-09-19, Session 50). A dropdown left of the chat box picks the channel plain text goes to;
  it is sticky — it only changes when you pick another one or type a channel command. Each has its own color:
  `/say [text]` (yellow) heard by players within 10 m; `/party [text]` (blue) your group; `/zone [text]` (orange) a shout the
  whole zone hears; `/tell <name> [text]` (purple) one player — `/tell Name` makes later plain text keep going to them, and the
  dropdown reads "Tell: Name". A channel command with text sends it AND switches to that channel; without text it only
  switches. In single-player /say and /zone just echo to yourself. Typed "[" is escaped and messages are capped at 300 chars.
- `/played` (added 2026-09-19) — the character's birthday and time in game: the in-world birth date with its clock time,
  the real-world date/time (12-hour) with how many days ago, and total time played plus this session.
  Playtime is a per-character running total banked into the save on every save; it starts counting when you enter the world
  (not menus/loading). Existing characters start from 0 — it never persisted before.
- `/language [name]` (added 2026-09-23) — no name: lists your languages and skills; a name: speak that language (same as the
  language dropdown next to the chat channel dropdown). Names match loosely ("khuzdul", "kry'thuun", "old thallian").
- Game master commands (only after `/gm enable <password>`; listed on the Controls & Commands page only for a GM):
  `/gm enable <password> | disable`, `/weather rain|clear`, `/raid [bandits|goblins]`, `/announce <text>`,
  `/maintenance [minutes|cancel]`, `/ban <ip | name>` (bans the address and disconnects everyone on it), `/unban <ip>`,
  `/bans` (added 2026-09-23), `/surname <player> <Name|clear>` (added 2026-09-24: set or remove anyone's surname; a GM can
  also /surname Name their own at any level, and /surname clear).
  `/kill` (your target: a monster dies without experience or loot, a player on their own machine; `/kill me`), `/give <item>
  [count]` (into your bags; name or its start), `/teleport <zone>` (to that zone's arrival point) — added 2026-09-25.
- `/surname [Name]` (added 2026-09-24) — from level 10, choose your last name, once (letters, an apostrophe or hyphen inside
  is fine); with no name, shows yours. Shown on nameplates, the target frame and /who. `/s` still means /say.
- `/stuck` (added 2026-09-24, test 33) — moves you 5 m in a random direction to a spot with floor and room to stand
  (tries 16 directions); not in a fight, once every 30 s.
- Macros (added 2026-09-24): `/macro` opens the abilities book's Macros tab — 24 macros per character plus 24 shared
  by every character on the account; each is a name, an icon (a spell's) and up to 5 chat lines, dragged to the action
  bar (badge "M"). `/cast <spell>` casts a known spell by name or unique prefix (works in chat too), `/target <name>`
  targets the nearest living match, `/pause <seconds>` (macro only, max 10) waits. %t = target's name, %T = name, level
  and health, %s = your name. A failed /cast stops the macro; pressing it while it runs does nothing.
  `/g` and `/gsay` now mean /party (before, /g ran /gm); `/pa` stays /party and `/ca` stays /camp.

### Movement
- `W` / `Up` — move forward
- `S` / `Down` — move backward (also cancels Autorun if active)
- `A` / `Left` — turn left
- `D` / `Right` — turn right
- `Q` — strafe left
- `E` — strafe right
- `Shift` — toggle run/walk
- `R` — toggle Autorun (keeps moving forward until toggled off or you press back)
- `Ctrl` — crouch (hold)
- `Space` — jump
- `X` — sit/stand (rest to regen HP/Mana/Stamina faster)

### Camera
- `F12` — toggle free-look mouselook on/off (captures/releases the cursor). Uncapped
  yaw (spins fully around), pitch capped only to avoid flipping past straight up/down.
  Changed 2026-09-13 — was clamped to 180°/90° like a "head turn"; see below.
- Hold Right Mouse Button — "head turn" look, independent of mouselook: capped to a
  realistic 180° left/right, 90° up/down range, doesn't move past that cap, stays where
  you left it on release. Added 2026-09-13. Hides the cursor only while held (changed
  2026-09-14, Session 42, from `MOUSE_MODE_CAPTURED` to `MOUSE_MODE_HIDDEN` — `CAPTURED`
  locks the OS cursor to the window center every frame, so releasing it — including on a
  quick right-click to loot/shop that never even dragged — made the cursor visibly jump
  to screen center. Head-turn's ±90°/±45° cap never needed `CAPTURED`'s unlimited
  relative motion, so `HIDDEN` fixes it with no functional loss).
- Mouse wheel — zoom in/out
- `Home` — cycle camera mode (behind / angled / first-person)
- Both look modes respect the "Invert Look Y" option (Escape → Options), added 2026-09-14.

### Combat / Targeting
- `Tab` — target nearest enemy
- `Ctrl+Tab` — `target_closest` action (bound, not yet wired to a function — known gap)
- `Ctrl+Escape` — clear current target and turn off autoattack
- `` ` `` (Quoteleft/tilde) — toggle autoattack on current target
- Left-click (cursor visible) — target via raycast; also swings if in range
- `melee_attack` action — defined but currently **unbound to any key** (known gap)
- `1`–`9`, `0`, `-`, `=` — action bar slots (cast spell / use skill in that slot)
- Taking damage from a monster automatically switches your target to it (added 2026-09-14) —
  no more losing track of who's actually hitting you mid-fight.

### Interface
- `C` — toggle character sheet
- `B` — toggle backpack
- `K` — toggle abilities book
- `T` — toggle tracking window (added 2026-09-17, Session 44) — only if you have the
  tracking skill (Elf/Half-Elf by race, Woodstalker/Wildspeaker/Troubadour by class);
  otherwise just logs that you haven't learned to track.
- `J` — toggle the quest journal (added 2026-09-22): every quest you've been given, Pending / Completed, with its lore, objective progress and rewards
- `L` — toggle the recipe book (added 2026-09-22): every tradeskill recipe you know, with its ingredients, skill level and where to make it
- `H` — hail nearby guard or vendor NPC. Hailing a guard gives a random flavor line;
  hailing a vendor (e.g. Aldric the Provisioner) now opens their shop window directly
  (changed 2026-09-14, Session 42 — used to just print a greeting line, so you still had
  to right-click precisely on the vendor's model to actually shop).
- `I` — Insight Check (appraise) your current target
- `Escape` — pause menu (also cancels chat typing without sending, if the chat box has focus)
- `Enter` — opens the chat box for typing when nothing else has focus (added 2026-09-13);
  sends the message and returns focus/movement to the game if the chat box is already focused
- Escape → **Options** (added 2026-09-14) — Music/Sound volume sliders, Invert Look Y toggle,
  and a Loot Preferences list (reset an accidentally-marked item's Loot/Ignore/Sell choice
  without needing to re-encounter it). Settings live in `user://settings.json`, separate from
  character saves.
- Every HUD/UI window is now draggable and remembers its position across sessions
  (added 2026-09-14) — previously only the chat window did; player frame and target frame
  had no drag handler at all.

### Looting
- Right-click a corpse (cursor visible) — opens the loot window, or auto-resolves and
  skips the window entirely if every item on the corpse already has a saved preference
- Loot window has Loot / Ignore / Sell toggles per item (added 2026-09-13, rendering fixed
  2026-09-14 — Godot's CheckBox wasn't drawing its check glyph at all, so these are now
  self-styled toggle buttons instead) — toggling one sets a permanent preference for that
  item id (applies immediately to the current drop too) so future drops of it are handled
  automatically without opening the window. Currency always auto-collects, no toggle needed,
  and now logs "You receive X Copper." same as any other pickup (was silent).
- The window auto-resizes to fit its contents (added 2026-09-14).
- `G` — loot every corpse within 10 m at once (added 2026-09-23; the same as each loot window's Loot All).

### Food & Water
- Auto-eat/auto-drink (added 2026-09-14, Session 42) — the moment satiety or thirst
  drops below 25, the player automatically eats/drinks the first matching item found
  in their inventory (basic slots checked first, then bags) — no more manually opening
  the backpack to eat. Falls back to the old "Your stomach growls"/"Your throat is dry"
  warnings if nothing suitable is carried. Manual Eat/Drink from the inventory
  right-click menu still works exactly as before.
- Both manual and automatic eating/drinking now print a random flavor line from
  `Data/food_drink_flavor.json` (e.g. "You consume Iron Rations. Yumm! That was
  tasty!", "You quench your thirst with A Flask of Water. Ahhh! That hit the spot.")
  instead of the old plain "(Hunger: X/100)" message.

### Saving
- Your position is now saved every time the game saves (Save, or Save & Exit from the
  pause menu) and restored on next login, so you log back in exactly where you logged
  out (added 2026-09-14, Session 42) — previously you always woke up at the zone's fixed
  spawn point regardless of where you'd actually been. Separate from your bind point
  (death-respawn location), which is unaffected.

---

## 2026-09-17 — Session 46: Charm command relay + kill-credit/DoT/Shadow-Aura/campfire relay fixes, full items.json icon sweep, spell level-gating, bard song toggle system

### Multiplayer — closed out the charm gap flagged at the end of Session 45
- Charm's control-window commands (Follow/Attack/Sit/Guard/Assist/Dismiss) now relay to
  whichever machine is actually authoritative for the monster, not just the initial
  charm itself. Each `cmd_*()` applies locally first (`command`/`charm_attack_target`
  aren't replicated fields, so this is what keeps the caster's own control window
  responsive) and relays via a new `apply_networked_charm_command` RPC when the caster
  isn't the real authority. `cmd_attack`'s target crosses the wire as a NodePath string
  (a Node reference means nothing cross-machine — same rule as `apply_charm`'s owner
  param) and resolves back to a real node with `get_node_or_null()` on the server.
- Found and fixed while closing that out: a charmed monster's kill of another monster
  didn't pass `credited_peer_id`, so it would have wrongly credited the host instead of
  whoever actually cast the charm; the `dot` effect type was missed in the original
  relay pass (copy-paste gap) and still mutated a monster's `combat_node` directly for
  non-host casters; Shadow Aura's continuous per-tick nearby-enemy debuff had the same
  unrelayed mutation.
- Separate but related bug in `campfire.gd`: its warmth-buff Area3D is a static,
  hand-placed node that exists identically on every peer, so it was detecting ANY
  replicated body (including a remote player's puppet) entering its trigger on every
  single peer's own local physics — a non-owning peer could apply the warmth buff
  directly to someone else's `combat_node` and print "you feel invigorated" on the
  wrong screen entirely. Fixed with an `is_multiplayer_authority()` gate on the body
  itself rather than a relay — a player is always authoritative over themselves
  (unlike a monster), so no RPC was needed, just the right check.

### Items.json — full icon sweep (not just Lumora Outskirts drops this time)
- User reported items still showing as invisible/iconless in the inventory. The
  earlier pass only fixed the 21 items that actually drop in Lumora Outskirts;
  39 more broken icon paths existed elsewhere in the file (quest/lore items, crafting
  reagents, a few weapons/armor from zones not live yet, plus `scroll_case` which had
  no icon path at all) — all now use the placeholder icon. Zero broken icons remain
  anywhere in `items.json`, verified by checking every entry's icon path against the
  actual filesystem.

### Spell casting — real level-gating (found via a fresh level-1 Troubadour able to
### cast a level-2 spell)
- `cast_spell()` previously had no level check of any kind. Now checks
  `class_level_requirements[player_class]` against `combat_node.level` — deliberately
  NOT the flat top-level `level` field, which only reflects one class's requirement
  when a spell is shared across several at different levels (e.g. Improved Block is
  Voidknight:3 but Blademaster:7).

### Status Effects window — descriptions on hover only
- Per user feedback, the always-visible description text under each buff/debuff row
  is gone; the same `player_spells.json` description now shows as a tooltip on hover
  instead, so the window reads as a compact list rather than a wall of text.

### Bard songs — real toggle-and-auto-recast system (the actual mechanic, replacing
### an earlier documented design that was never built)
- Casting a Troubadour spell toggles it on/off instead of one-shot casting
  (`player3d.gd`'s new `_active_songs` dict, gated on `player_class == "Troubadour"` —
  applies to any of their spells, not a per-spell flag). While "on," `_tick_cooldowns()`'s
  new `_tick_active_songs()` auto-recasts the spell the instant its own cooldown clears,
  checked every frame rather than edge-triggered so a transient block (still animating
  the previous cast) naturally retries next frame instead of getting stuck. A genuine
  failure (dead target, out of mana) removes it from `_active_songs` and logs why the
  song stopped, rather than retrying forever.
- Multiple songs stack independently and correctly: turning one off only stops ITS OWN
  auto-recast loop — a buff it already applied keeps counting down and showing in the
  Status Effects window on its own schedule, completely unaffected by starting or
  stopping any other song. Verified end-to-end with a real test (cast A, let its buff
  land, cast B without touching A, confirm both show simultaneously, stop A, confirm
  its buff persists until its own duration naturally elapses while B keeps playing).
- Charm Melody (a song) additionally gets a periodic break-early roll while charmed
  (`monster3d.gd`'s `state_charmed()`: every 5s, 15% chance — placeholder values, not
  balanced) — since the song keeps recasting itself, a broken charm gets automatically
  re-attempted the moment the bard's own cooldown allows, with no extra code needed;
  this falls straight out of the toggle/auto-recast system.
- Most songs are intentionally mana-free (the user's own design: "it's a song they're
  singing... only a few will cost mana") — `song_of_courage`'s `mana_cost: 0` is
  correct, not a bug, confirmed with the user directly after a test caught it.

### Multiplayer loot testing round — personal loot, auto-close, cross-player combat log
- **Personal/instanced loot.** Root cause was worse than "shared loot pool": `monster3d.gd`'s
  `pending_loot`/`is_lootable` were non-replicated fields only ever set inside `die()`,
  which itself only ever runs on the authoritative/host machine — so a non-host player
  could not loot anything at all, not just "the same drops as everyone else." Fixed by
  rolling loot lazily, per-peer, inside `open_loot_window()` itself (`_personal_loot:
  Dictionary`, keyed by peer id) instead of once in `die()` — since a script field not
  listed in a node's `SceneReplicationConfig` is already implicitly per-peer, this one
  redesign fixes both problems at once with no networking code needed.
- The corpse no longer frees itself the instant one player finishes looting — it waits
  out its normal 60s despawn timer so every player gets a fair loot window. An empty
  loot window (nothing left for that peer) now auto-closes 5s after the last item is
  taken, and `_try_loot_corpse()` no longer gates on a shared `is_lootable` flag that
  doesn't exist anymore — `open_loot_window()` handles "nothing here" itself.
- **Wild Surge fix + a much bigger bug found while chasing it.** The user's own data
  edit (target: enemy → group) had only landed in the description text, not the actual
  `target`/`spell_type` fields — fixed directly. While verifying, found the real reason
  it "didn't work" at all: 7 pairs of spells across `player_spells.json` shared identical
  `spell_name` values, which silently shadows one spell entirely via `_spell_by_name`'s
  last-write-wins dictionary construction (Gravecaller/Voidknight, Aetherfist/Lightsworn,
  Wildspeaker/Chaosborn, Runecaster/Troubadour pairs). Renamed the shadowed copy in each
  pair, keeping whichever name was already hardcoded or already had a scroll item.
  Verified zero duplicate spell names remain anywhere in the file.
- **Cross-player combat log visibility.** `GameLog` (game_log.gd) is a purely local
  signal bus with zero networking — a second player never saw the first player's
  attacks, spells, or buffs land at all, even standing right next to them. Added a
  combat-message relay to `net.gd` (`broadcast_combat_message()` /
  `_rpc_receive_combat_message` RPC, same pattern as the existing `/tell` and `/party`
  relays) that every other connected peer receives and feeds into their own
  `GameLog.log_combat()` — tagged with the acting player's position so each receiver's
  existing 10m `COMBAT_VISIBILITY_RANGE` filter (already used to hide distant NPC combat
  noise in `game_log_window.gd`) applies automatically, no separate proximity system
  needed. `player3d.gd`'s new `_broadcast_combat()` helper fires this alongside every
  local combat/spell/buff message: melee hits and death (`CombatLogFormatter`'s new
  third-person `player_attack_broadcast()`), spell damage and death (reusing the
  existing caster-parameterized `spell_damage()`/`death()`), and every buff/debuff/heal/
  CC message from `_apply_generic_spell_effect()` — those were already written
  target-referential ("Target is weakened.") with no "You" in them, so they broadcast
  verbatim except when the target is the caster themself (a "yourself" placeholder,
  swapped for the caster's real name so it doesn't read as nonsense on someone else's
  screen). Self-cast spells (buffs, wards, summons) get one generic "PlayerName casts
  Spell Name." broadcast rather than a bespoke third-person line for every flavor text
  variant.
- Also found and fixed a genuine, unrelated data bug while doing the headless compile
  check for the above: `Data/player_skills.json` was missing a comma after
  `musicianship`, which failed to parse at all and would have silently dropped the
  entire skills list on load.

### Multiplayer core bug: monsters only ever noticed/attacked the HOST
- Root cause of "remote player isn't losing any life when hit, monsters run right to
  the host's player": `monster3d.gd`'s AI ran entirely off a single `player` reference
  set once via `TargetFrame.local_player()` — correct for a HUD script (which wants
  "my own player"), but monster AI only ever *runs* on the server, where "the local
  player" means the HOST's own character specifically. A monster could never so much
  as glance at any other connected player. Fixed with a new `_nearest_player()` that
  scans every real player in the "player" group and picks whichever is physically
  closest, re-resolved every frame (once real aggro exists, `get_current_target()`
  already correctly overrides this with the highest-threat attacker — this only
  governs who gets noticed/chased before that).
- Second, compounding bug found while fixing the above: even once a monster *did*
  fight a non-host player, `add_threat()` was never actually reaching the real
  (server-side) monster for their damage — player3d.gd's own `add_threat()` call only
  ever mutates the ATTACKING player's own non-authoritative puppet copy of the
  monster when they're not the host, silently discarded. Fixed by registering threat
  directly inside `apply_networked_damage()` (the RPC that already runs
  authoritatively on the server for every non-host attack, including pet attacks),
  resolving the attacker's peer id back to their real player node.

### Multiplayer testing round 2 fixes (per 2026-09-17 test notes)
- Target level: targeting or tracking another PLAYER always showed "Lv 1" — `target_frame.gd`/
  `tracking_window.gd`/the Appraisal check all read a flat `"level"` property that only
  exists on Monster (a Player3D node has none, only `combat_node.level`). New shared
  `TargetFrame.entity_level()` helper prefers `combat_node.level` (correct for both
  monsters and players) and fixes all three call sites at once.
- Tracking window now includes other real players in its list (candidates previously
  covered monsters/guards/vendors only).
- Personal loot MLW auto-close and the wild_surge/duplicate-spell-name fixes (Session
  46, first half) are unrelated pre-existing completions — the "both people are seeing
  loot now" test report describes the pre-fix build.
- Inventory fill order: `Inventory.add_item()` filled bags first and only fell back to
  free basic-inventory (non-bag) slots as a last resort — the opposite of what's
  expected. Now free basic slots fill first, then bags in slot order. Also fixed a
  latent stacking gap this reorder would have made worse: stackable items sitting
  directly in a basic slot were never checked for an existing stack to merge into
  (only bags were), so pickups would create duplicate stacks there instead of merging.
- Bleed-out revival: another player healing (spell) or bandaging (right-click a downed
  ally in the 3D world) can now bring them back before their bleed-out timer expires —
  previously nothing checked for this at all. Logs/broadcasts "PlayerName comes back to
  life!" to nearby players. Bandaging required actually wiring up bandage items for the
  first time: Cloth/Coarse Bandage had a pure-flavor-text "effect" field and no Use
  button anywhere, completely inert — added a real `heal_amount` field and a "Use"
  button in the inventory item popup for self-use, plus the new right-click-a-downed-
  ally flow for bandaging someone else.
- Ultravision: added a per-race `"ultravision": true` trait (everyone except Human,
  per the user's own call — "most elves, trolls, etc have it") and a per-viewer
  brightness boost on that player's own camera at night (`Camera3D.environment`
  override with `adjustment_brightness`), since day_night_cycle.gd's darkness is one
  shared WorldEnvironment for the whole zone — there's no such thing as "this one
  player's world is brighter" at that level in multiplayer. Not yet visually verified
  in-game; brightness/adjustment values are a first guess to tune.
- Buffs/debuffs are now right-click-cancellable from the Status Effects window
  (stances excluded — they toggle through stance_bar.gd's own state, not a plain
  removable active_effects entry).
- Mobs now show a nameplate above their head, same as players (`monster_template.tscn`
  gained a `NameLabel` Label3D, reusing `TargetFrame.nameplate_name()`).
- Desert Scavenger's mesh was clipping through the floor (pivot sits above its feet) —
  raised 0.4m; Juvenile Spider's pivot was already correct.
- Troubadour songs: fixed one stray mis-tagged self-buff (Improved Stealth had
  `target: enemy`/`spell_type: detrimental`/a leftover nonzero `damage` despite being
  a pure self movement-speed buff) — every actual song (359-394) already matched the
  user's own stated defaults (duration 8, mana 0, cast 1.5, recast 5, damage 0 unless
  a real damaging song) with no further stray damage values found. Added a real
  `musicianship` skill: all 36 Troubadour-exclusive songs now tick it via the existing
  `skill_category` mechanism (was pointing at generic arcane schools like evocation/
  enchantment instead) — the 5 stealth/pickpocket abilities Troubadour shares with
  Woodstalker/Shadowblade were left alone since they're not musical performances.
- Could not reproduce "Coarse/Ancient Bandage, Rotten Linen, Torch have no inventory
  icon" — all four already point to a valid, loadable icon file (the same placeholder
  ~235 other items use) via every icon-rendering code path checked (backpack,
  character sheet equipment slots, vendor sell list). Needs a screenshot to
  investigate further.

### Human Female re-rig, "Version 2" (new outfit/mesh/texture/animations)
- User dropped a full new Mixamo/Meshy asset set into `models/Human Female/Version 2/`
  (new outfit mesh + texture, and a fresh animation set including — for the first time
  for this model — a proper "-buff"-suffixed cast clip). Rebuilt `female_animations.res`
  from these source files following the established pipeline (pick each source FBX's
  longest clip to dodge Mixamo's ~1s throwaway bind-pose clip, same bug that recurs
  every re-rig): idle/walk/run/jump/sit/attack_horizontal/attack_downward/death/
  cast_beneficial/cast_detrimental, both attack slots sharing the one available melee
  clip (Standing Torch Melee Attack). `player3d.gd`'s `CHARACTER_MODELS["human_female"]`
  now points entirely at the new Version 2 folder (scene/library/texture_override).
  Verified headless: Character node builds, AnimationPlayer has all 10 expected keys,
  texture override loads without a "not found" warning. Not yet seen in-game with a
  real screenshot.

### Arcanist/Chaosborn multi-bolt spells — real bug, real fix
- Magic Missile (and the same authoring mistake on Arcane Barrage, Unstable Missile,
  Chaos Barrage) had "damage" set to the BOLT COUNT from its own description ("fires 3
  bolts") instead of the actual per-bolt damage, and two of them were tagged `target:
  "cone"` (hits every enemy in an area) instead of `"enemy"` (single target, multiple
  hits). Fixed all four properly rather than just bumping Magic Missile's number: added
  a data-driven `bolt_count` field (+ optional `extra_bolt_chance` for the two spells
  with a "chance for extra bolt" line) and a new `_cast_multi_bolt_spell()` engine
  function that fires that many separate damage instances at one target, each with its
  own resist roll/combat log line/threat, stopping the instant the target dies. Any
  future multi-hit spell just needs the two fields, no new code.

### Troubadour: message editing, real AoE, single-song exclusivity
- Song of Courage (and every self-cast buff generally) was showing "Yourself is
  empowered." — the generic effect engine's message, which was never meant to be
  user-facing wording. Added a `cast_message` spell-data field (2nd person, e.g. "You
  feel courageous.") the user can edit/add per spell directly in player_spells.json;
  falls back to the old generic text when absent, so nothing else needed updating.
- Confirmed and fixed the deeper report: Troubadour's "group"-targeted songs were only
  ever landing on ONE ally (defaulting to self) despite every song's own description
  saying "allies in Xm"/"grants party". `target: "group"` is shared by 150+ spells
  across every healer/support class in the game (Lightmender's whole kit, Lightsworn,
  Spiritweaver, etc.) where it genuinely does mean "your one chosen ally" — so this is
  gated specifically on `player_class == "Troubadour"`, not a blanket engine change:
  their songs now hit the caster plus everyone non-Enemy within 8m (tunable per-spell
  via a new `aoe_radius` field), reusing Earth Totem's existing radius-scan pattern.
- Reversed the "multiple songs stack independently" design from Session 45 per direct
  user correction — that was explicitly wrong, not a feature. Starting a new song now
  stops every other one from auto-recasting (only the most recent stays running); the
  one it replaces isn't ripped away, its already-applied buff just runs its course.

### Tradeskills — cooking/alchemy now actually raise their skill
- A successful craft never ticked anything in the skill book for either station. Added
  the same use-it-to-level-it `_tick_skill()` call every combat/spell skill already
  gets, mapped per station_id (`campfire` → cooking, `basic_alchemy_kit` → alchemy).

### Loot window auto-close
- Was waiting 5 real seconds after the last item before closing (my own addition, not
  requested) — per feedback this just read as "it doesn't auto-close at all" since the
  player had already moved on by the time it fired. Now closes the instant nothing's
  left.

---

## 2026-09-18 — Session 47: Pet dialogue by class, Shadowblade Backstab/Shadowstep/Stealth stance

### Spiritweaver/Wildspeaker pets no longer talk like a Voidknight's undead thrall
- `phantasmal_echo_pet.gd` (Spiritweaver) and `wildspeaker_pet.gd` (Wildspeaker) both
  `extends PetMinion`, so they inherited every "dark lord"/"master"/"m'lord" flavor
  line from the Voidknight's skeleton pet verbatim — thematically wrong for a
  benevolent spirit ally. Refactored `pet_minion.gd`'s `cmd_*()` methods to call a new
  set of overridable `_phrase_*()` functions (same pattern as the existing
  `_pet_title()`/`_pick_random_name()` hooks) instead of hardcoding the line inline, so
  a subclass only needs to override the text, not duplicate the state-management
  logic. Both pets now have their own appropriately-toned lines.

### Shadowblade: Backstab, Shadowstep, and a real Stealth mechanic
- **Backstab** did its full 100 damage from any angle — no positional check existed at
  all. Now requires actually being behind the target (dot product of the target's
  facing vs. the direction to the caster, threshold ~120° rear arc) OR being
  stealthed/invisible, matching the spell's own description ("from stealth/behind
  target" — either condition, not both).
- **Shadowstep** did nothing at all before this — `target: "none"` isn't a case the
  spell-resolution match statement has ever handled (same true of 4 other classes'
  unbuilt teleport spells: Aetherfist's swift_step/wind_dash, Arcanist's blink,
  Chaosborn's chaos_rift — casting any of them just spends mana/cooldown silently;
  only Shadowstep is built now, per this specific request). Now teleports the caster
  2m behind the target, faces them afterward, and applies the same "invisibility"
  effect (`{"invisible": 1.0}`) the existing Invisibility spell uses — which means the
  nameplate change onto "(a shadowy figure)" the user asked about needed zero extra
  code, since `TargetFrame.nameplate_name()` already handles that generically for any
  invisible entity, player or monster.
- Found and fixed a sign error of my own while building Shadowstep: Godot's forward
  convention is -Z, so "behind" a node is its raw +Z basis vector, not the negated one
  — caught by a headless test before it shipped (teleported IN FRONT of the target
  instead of behind).
- **Stealth is now a real Stance**, per user request ("similar to the stances that the
  tanking classes have") — added a `Shadowblade` entry to `class_stances.json`
  (previously Voidknight-only) with a `stealthed: 1.0` modifier, using the exact same
  toggle-on-the-stance-bar mechanism the tank stances already have. This is also what
  makes Backstab's "from stealth" condition actually reachable for the first time —
  nothing previously ever set the `stealthed` modifier at all.

### Guard height + five more Version 2 model re-rigs
- **Guard height.** Guards measured a genuine 1.7m (same standard baseline every
  correctly-exported model in this game uses — verified via a headless AABB
  measurement, cross-checked against Human Male's identical raw height), visibly
  shorter than intended. Scaled `guard_npc.tscn`'s Character node + collision capsule +
  nameplate height by 1.2x, landing at 2.04m — "approximately 2m or a bit taller" as
  requested. Verified the final in-world height directly rather than guessing at a
  scale multiplier.
- **Elf Female, Half-Elf Female, Dark Elf Female** all got a second re-rig ("Version
  2" — new mesh/texture/animation set, same pipeline as Human Female's Version 2 from
  earlier this session). All three measure the same standard 1.7m baseline, so the old
  Elf Female's `"scale": 98.0` hack (needed only because THAT specific 2026-09-16 export
  was badly undersized) is gone — don't carry it forward if this model is ever re-rigged
  again without re-measuring first.
- **Skeleton Pet (Voidknight's Morthan's Call)** also got a Version 2 re-rig. No new
  death clip was provided this time, so its rebuilt animation library mixes the new
  idle/walk/attack clips with the original folder's existing death animation.
- **Spirit Pet is now Spiritweaver's Phantasmal Echo**, with a real animated Mixamo
  model for the first time (previously a translucent placeholder sphere — see this
  session's earlier pet-dialogue work). **Wildspeaker's Spirit of the Woods pet now
  has its own real model too**, from a newly-provided `models/Wildspeaker Pet/`
  folder — these are two SEPARATE dedicated folders for two different pets, and an
  initial pass wired them backwards (Wildspeaker → Spirit Pet folder) before catching
  and correcting the mix-up.
- **Found and fixed a separate, unrelated, real bug while re-measuring these models
  for scale correction:** `player_race` is saved/loaded as character_options.json's
  raw lowercase key (`"half_elf"`, `"dark_elf"`, `"elf"` — `character_creation.gd`
  never `.capitalize()`s it the way it explicitly does for `player_class`), but both
  `TRACKING_RACES` (pre-existing) and this session's own new `ULTRAVISION_RACES` were
  written with the capitalized display names (`"Elf"`, `"Half-Elf"`). Neither ever
  matched anything — race-granted tracking has silently never worked for Elf/Half-Elf,
  and ultravision never worked for ANY race at all. Both lists now use the correct
  lowercase-key form.

### Lumora Outskirts background music
- `global_background_music.gd` only ever supported one shared menu-screen music loop,
  hard-stopping for every other scene (including live zones). Generalized it to a
  `SCENE_MUSIC` dict keyed by scene path — Lumora Outskirts now plays "Sands of
  Lumora.ogg" (new asset) while the menu scenes keep using the original `.tscn`-assigned
  stream. Adding another zone's ambient track going forward is a one-line dict entry,
  no script changes.

### Character creation — the last 5 races have real portraits
- Dwarf, Gnome, Halfling, Ogre, Half-Orc, and Lizardkin were all still showing
  `angry_cat.png` (a placeholder) for both sexes in the character creation screen.
  New portrait art dropped into `Assets/character creation/` for all 6 races — wired
  up in `character_options.json`, verified every one of all 22 race/sex portraits in
  the game now loads as a real texture (no more placeholders anywhere).

### Racial traits now actually do something (core combat stats)
- Every race's `traits`/`penalties` in `character_options.json` were pure flavor text —
  only Lizardkin's AC bonus and Troll's flat regen bonus ever did anything mechanically.
  Added a parallel `race_*` field family to `combatnode.gd` (mirroring the existing
  `class_*` bonus pattern exactly: `race_hp_mult`, `race_mana_mult`,
  `race_melee_damage_mult`, `race_spell_damage_mult`, `race_physical_resist`,
  `race_dodge_bonus`, `race_parry_bonus`, `race_crit_bonus`,
  `race_movement_speed_mult`, `race_all_stats_mult`, `race_negative_effect_resist`,
  `race_immune_to_root`, `race_immune_to_blind`) and wired all 11 races' `apply_racial_modifiers()`
  entries in `player3d.gd` to set them: Dwarf (+15% HP, +5% physical resist, -10%
  move speed, -10% mana), Elf (+10% mana, +5% spell damage, root immunity, -10% HP),
  Gnome (+10 dodge, +5 crit, -15% melee damage), Halfling (10% negative-effect resist,
  -10% HP, -5% melee damage), Half-Elf (+5% all stats), Ogre (+20% melee damage, +10%
  HP, +5% physical resist, -15% move speed, -10 dodge, -20% mana), Troll (+10 crit,
  -10 dodge, -10 parry, keeps its existing flat regen bonus), Dark Elf (blind
  immunity), Half-Orc (+15% melee damage, +5% HP, -10% mana, -5% spell damage),
  Lizardkin (existing AC bonus, plus -2 charisma).
- Scoped to "core combat stats" only, per discussion — explicitly NOT covered this
  pass: skill-specific bonuses (blacksmithing, hide, tracking, and a dozen+ other named
  skills — no single clean hook), faction standing offsets (a separate system),
  environmental/conditional traits (Dark Elf's daylight regen penalty, Lizardkin's cold
  penalties, Troll's swamp bonus, Elf's "outdoors"-only movement bonus — all need
  zone/time-of-day awareness that doesn't exist yet), XP-gain-rate bonuses (a leveling
  hook, not combat), and Lizardkin's unarmed bite/amphibious (full abilities, not
  passive stats). See `project_racial_traits` in Claude's memory for the complete
  breakdown of what's done vs. deferred.

## 2026-09-19 — Session 48: Asset folder standardization, texture resize (load-lag fix), pet follow/idle-facing fixes, character creation Back button, Gravecaller pet, full spell_school/skill_category reclassification + 10-type resistance system

### Asset consolidation & naming standardization
- Consolidated all 6 "Version 2" re-rig folders (Dark Elf/Elf/Half-Elf Female, Human
  Female, Skeleton Pet, Spirit Pet) — deleted superseded originals, moved Version 2's
  contents up to be the folder's only content, deleted the now-empty Version 2
  subfolders, updated every code reference in player3d.gd/pet_minion.gd/
  phantasmal_echo_pet.gd. Fixed a mislabeled file caught along the way (Half-Elf
  Female's Version 2 had a "Female Elf Sitting Idle.fbx" — wrong race in the name).
- Standardized animation filenames to `<Race> <Sex> <Animation>.fbx` (character
  models) / lowercase `<name> <animation>.fbx` (pets/mobs) across those 6 folders,
  the 12 new race folders, 3 newly-wired animated mobs, and 3 previously-inconsistent
  male race folders (Elf/Half-Elf/Dark Elf Male were "Male Race X", now "Race Male X").
  Deleted genuinely-unused duplicate Meshy "Animation_Running/Walking_withSkin"
  byproduct clips wherever a dedicated named clip already covered the same motion.
  Deleted `models/guard/` entirely — a confirmed-dead placeholder folder, replaced by
  Lumora Guardsman and no longer referenced anywhere in code.
- Wired 12 new player races into CHARACTER_MODELS (Dwarf/Gnome/Halfling/Half-Orc/
  Lizardkin/Ogre × Female/Male) — built fresh animation libraries for each, all
  measure the standard 1.7m height baseline, no scale corrections needed.
- Wired 8 new mob visuals to their correct existing monster entries, confirmed against
  the user's Zone Spawn Sheet (exact 1:1 match, 13 mob folders to 13 Lumora Outskirts
  spawn-sheet rows, zero leftover assets): bandit→Sand Brigand, ghost→Tormented Spirit,
  goblin_warrior→new Desert Goblin Warrior model (replacing the old shared "desert
  goblin" placeholder), plus slime/bat/spider/snake/dune_scarab as static critter
  meshes. Fixed a bug found while wiring these: `_apply_critter_material()` called
  `load("")` for critters with no normal map, throwing a harmless-but-noisy engine
  error every spawn — now guards empty texture paths before calling load().

### Texture resize — root cause of the reported 5-7s load-in lag + music stutter
- Every Meshy-exported model (226 files project-wide, old and new alike) shipped a
  full 4096×4096 texture (~20-24MB each), each taking ~450-500ms to synchronously
  load the first time it's needed — this session roughly doubled how many distinct
  mob types with these textures are active in Lumora Outskirts (6→13), pushing the
  cumulative first-load cost into multiple seconds of blocked main thread (which is
  also why the music stuttered — audio can't process while the thread is blocked).
  Resized all 226 to 2048×2048 (LANCZOS resample) — models/ dropped from 4.8GB to
  3.2GB, and the same 9-texture timing sample dropped from ~4.0s to ~1.1s (3.6x).
- Checked "Sands of Lumora.ogg" itself for a baked-in defect per a follow-up report
  that it stutters even played standalone — ffprobe/ffmpeg found a clean Vorbis
  stream with zero decode warnings, and a sample-level scan (clicks, mid-track
  silence dropouts) found nothing wrong with the file's actual audio data. Points to
  the export/render process rather than something fixable in the current file —
  user re-exporting from their DAW.

### Pet AI fixes
- Fixed pet stutter: `MIN_OWNER_DISTANCE` (the "too close, back off" threshold) was
  set to the exact same value as `FOLLOW_DISTANCE` (the pet's normal resting spot
  behind the owner), so ordinary navmesh-snap slop made the pet flicker between
  "arrived" and "too close" every few frames. Increased `FOLLOW_DISTANCE` 3.0→4.5
  (pets trail further back, as requested) and dropped `MIN_OWNER_DISTANCE` to 2.0,
  giving real breathing room between the two so they stop fighting each other.
- Added `_face_nearest_enemy_if_idle()` — a pet standing in Follow/Assist/Guard (not
  moving, not already fighting) now turns to face the nearest living monster within
  15m, so it looks alert/reactive instead of frozen facing its last movement
  direction. Never overrides Attack (already faces its real target) or Sit.

### Character creation Back button + multiplayer "Create New Character" flow
- Added a Back button to character creation, returning to wherever it was reached
  from (main menu, or the multiplayer screen if that's where the player came from).
- Added a "+ Create New Character" button to the multiplayer host/join menu. Sets
  `Global.return_to_multiplayer_menu`, sends the player through the normal character
  creation flow, then automatically reopens the multiplayer overlay (dropdown
  refreshed, new character selectable) once they land back on the main menu — the
  multiplayer menu is an overlay added as a child of main_menu.tscn, not its own
  scene, so a plain scene change would otherwise strand the player on a bare main menu.

### Gravecaller's raise_skeleton
- Found already defined in player_spells.json at exactly 80% caster health (matching
  the user's own guess) but completely unwired, and carrying two data bugs
  (`target: "group"` and `duration: 5` instead of `"self"`/`"unlimited"` — copied from
  a template row, not intentional). Fixed both, wired the spell to summon the exact
  same skeleton model/pet as Voidknight's spectral_minion via a new
  `hp_percent_of_caster` field on pet_minion.gd (default 0.4, overridden to 0.8 for
  Gravecaller's variant via `PET_HP_PERCENT_OVERRIDES` in player3d.gd). Verified:
  177 HP caster → 141 HP Gravecaller pet vs. 70 HP Voidknight pet, same visual model.

### Spell classification overhaul — skill_category synced to spreadsheet, spell_school fully reclassified, unified 10-type resistance system
- `skill_category` (which skill levels up when a spell is cast — confirmed via
  `_tick_skill(skill_category)`, `player3d.gd` ~line 2859, feeding the K-book) synced
  against the user's Player Spells Classifications.xlsx: 41 mismatches found and
  fixed (mostly Troubadour's placeholder "musicianship" replaced with real schools,
  plus a few trap/defense mismatches). ~560 of 561 spells covered by the spreadsheet;
  the gap is content added after its snapshot (mainly Chaosborn's kit and Voidknight's
  levels 4-8).
- `spell_school` (what damage type a spell deals, for resistance — confirmed via
  `_resolve_spell_cast()`'s damage branch, `player3d.gd` ~line 2997) was essentially
  never properly set: 334-457 of 561 spells were still on the default "physical"
  regardless of class. Finalized a 10-value vocabulary through discussion (fire, cold,
  acid, lightning, poison, disease, magic, divine, psychic, spirit — psychic and
  spirit deliberately kept separate despite similar surface flavor, since they protect
  two different class identities: Runecaster's mind-affecting kit vs. Gravecaller/
  Voidknight's life-force/death-drain kit) and reclassified all 561 spells: keyword-
  detected damage type in the description takes priority (so a physically-delivered
  attack with an elemental/status effect — Aetherfist's fire_strike, Shadowblade's
  venom_strike — correctly gets its real damage type instead of "physical", per
  direct discussion of this exact case), with a cure-context guard so a spell that
  merely mentions curing/removing an ailment isn't misread as dealing it (Lightsworn's
  purify no longer reads as "poison"), then skill_category-based defaults, then
  class-wide overrides for Lightsworn/Lightmender→divine, Gravecaller/Voidknight→
  spirit, Runecaster→psychic where nothing more specific was detected. Final spread:
  magic 172, physical 104, psychic 104, spirit 82, divine 62, fire 12, lightning 8,
  poison 7, cold 6, disease 4, acid 0 (no acid-flavored spell exists yet at the
  current level-1-20 content).
- Found and fixed a real architecture gap while designing this: two disconnected
  resistance systems existed — CombatNode computed 8 resist stats dynamically from
  CON/WIS/gear but ALL used the identical formula (pure duplicates), and damage calc
  only ever consulted arcane-or-divine via a crude is_arcane bool; the character sheet
  UI's Resistances badges only showed 5 of the 10 types with two silently-wrong key
  mappings ("acid" was reading poison_resist, "magic" was reading arcane_resist).
  Unified into one system: CombatNode now has real fields for all 10 types (added
  acid_resist/lightning_resist, renamed arcane_resist→magic_resist),
  `calculate_spell_damage()`/`calculate_resist_chance()` take the spell's own
  spell_school as a resist_type String directly instead of an is_arcane bool, and the
  character sheet shows all 10 with correct mappings (now an HFlowContainer since 10
  badges don't reliably fit one row).
- Also found and wired in `Data/racial_stats.json`'s per-race elemental resistance
  data (e.g. Dwarf: acid/cold/magic +5, psychic -5; Troll: acid +10, cold/fire -5) —
  real authored content that was never read anywhere before this pass. Added
  `race_fire_resist`/`race_cold_resist`/`race_acid_resist`/`race_magic_resist`/
  `race_psychic_resist` to CombatNode, applied per-race in `apply_racial_modifiers()`.
  Verified: Dwarf shows +5 acid/cold/magic and -5 psychic vs. Human's flat baseline.
- Full design documented in game_flow.txt's new "Spell Classification" section for
  reference — not duplicated here.
- Not done this pass: monster-side magic resistances (monsters.json's existing
  "resistances" field is a different concept — physical weapon-type tags for
  appraisal, not magic damage-type resistance), and no acid-flavored spell exists yet
  to verify that resist type end-to-end in real gameplay.

## 2026-09-19 — Session 49: Critter ground offsets, per-mob level ranges, monster death_text, pet "Protect" rename, items.json icon re-path + new icon assets, spell icons + tooltips on the action bar/K book/buff bar

(Reconstructed after the working session's window was closed accidentally — written
from the git working-tree diff, not from the original conversation. Everything below
was verified against the actual files; anything discussed in that session but never
saved to disk isn't captured here.)

### Critter meshes sunk into the ground — real per-model offsets
- The 2026-09-18 static critter meshes (slime/bat/spider/snake/dune_scarab, plus rat)
  each have their pivot sitting a different distance above their lowest geometry, so
  they clipped through the floor at identity transform. The old code only special-cased
  the rat (+0.4m). Replaced with a `GROUND_OFFSET` table in `_setup_critter_visual()`
  (monster3d.gd), measured precisely via world-space AABB (mesh bottom vs. character
  root): rat 0.4, snake 0.22, spider 0.38, bat 0.9, slime 0.95, dune_scarab 0.46.
  Juvenile Spider was already correct at 0 and has no entry.

### Per-mob level ranges (from Zone Spawn Sheet.xlsx)
- monsters.json now has `level_min`/`level_max` on the Lumora Outskirts mobs: slime
  2-4, spiderling 1-2, rat 1-3, bandit 2-4, spider 2-4, snake 2-4, dune_scarab 2-5,
  mummy 3-6, skeleton 1-3, ghost 2-5, goblin 1-4, goblin_scout 1-4, goblin_warrior
  3-6, bat 1-3. `Monster._ready()` rolls a random level in that range on each spawn;
  monsters without a range (everything outside Lumora Outskirts, not live yet) keep
  the flat `level`.
- Deliberately does NOT scale health/damage/armor_class by the rolled level — those
  stay the flat values tuned per monster. Level's existing effect on to-hit/resist
  (`calculate_hit_chance()`'s level_modifier) is what differentiates a level-4 spawn
  from a level-2 one.
- Also in monsters.json: mummy's description changed from "an ancient tomb guardian"
  to "a reanimated sand mummy"; bat's `special_ability` changed from "Life Tap" to
  "life_siphon" (the spell key). Note `special_ability` is only ever loaded into a
  field and shown by appraisal — no monster ability is actually executed in code yet,
  so this is a data tag, not a behavior change.

### Monster death_text
- New `death_text` field on every monster in monsters.json (all 28 entries, all blank
  for now — user-authored flavor). `Monster.die()` prints it to the combat log
  (`GameLog.log_combat(death_text, global_position)`, so distant deaths are filtered
  like other mob combat lines) when non-empty. The field/loader existed but nothing
  ever printed it until this pass — the hook in die() was the missing piece.

### Pet command "Follow" → "Protect" (UI only)
- pet_frame.gd's button relabeled Protect; still calls `cmd_follow` and the FOLLOW
  state (pet already followed the owner AND defended them from whatever hit them —
  see `_process_auto_engage()`'s "defend" branch; the old label didn't say so). Pet
  acknowledgment line changed to "I will stay close and guard you, dark lord."

### Item/spell icon assets (user-added) — verified + imported
- items.json's `icon` paths were re-pointed from `Assets/789_Lorc_RPG_icons/...` to a
  new `Assets/icons/items/` set (61 PNGs). Verified: all 273 items parse, every icon
  path resolves to a real file, no old-style refs remain, and no non-icon field
  differs from `Data/items-old.json` (the pre-change backup, kept alongside).
  197 items (spell scrolls) currently share the single generic `scroll.png`.
- `Assets/icons/spells/` (49 PNGs) and six new `aldenexia-*-sheet.png` sprite sheets
  were also added. The spell icons are NOT referenced by any code or data yet
  (player_spells.json has no icon field) — assets only, wiring still to do.
- Ran a headless Godot import pass so the new PNGs get `.import` files (110 created);
  also imported the new `Tomb of the Lost.ogg` and the updated `Sands of Lumora.ogg`.
- Known/unrelated: import logs a pre-existing parse error in the old 2D
  `Scripts/player.gd` (line 162, "Cannot infer the type of initial_offset") — file is
  untouched and predates this session; the live 3D game isn't affected.

### Spell icons on every spell + icons/tooltips on the action bar, K book and buff bar
- Added an `icon` field (res:// path into Assets/icons/spells/) to 514 of the 561
  spells in player_spells.json. Nothing else in the file changed (verified: zero
  non-icon differences vs. the previous version). The other 47 have none: 40 are
  physical attacks (no physical icon art exists yet) and 7 are upgrades of one.
- Icons are assigned by the new, re-runnable `tools/assign_spell_icons.py` (dry run by
  default, `--write` to apply). Icon family follows the user's targeting design:
  self* = on yourself, target* = single target, group* = player group (beneficial) or
  all enemies around the cast target (detrimental), aoe* = pbaoe/cone/line radiating
  from the caster. The picture comes from effect/damage type (spell_school → fire/ice/
  lightning/poison/divine/arcane/necromancy). improved_/enhanced_/greater_/master_
  upgrades reuse their base spell's icon. Full rules in game_flow.txt's new "Spell
  Icons & Tooltips" section.
- Found while matching: ~64 spells are tagged `target: "enemy"` but are plainly self
  buffs/forms/shields (cat_form, mana_shield, lights_bastion, sacred_oath...). They got
  self* icons by description. Data left as-is — `target` drives casting, so retagging
  is a separate decision. Side effect: buff_bar.gd draws those red (debuff) because it
  keys off target == "enemy".
- New `Scripts/spell_info.gd` (class_name SpellInfo): cached icon loading, word-wrapping
  for tooltips (Godot's tooltips never wrap on their own), and the shared tooltip text
  (name, the file's description, "N mp · Ns recast"). First version named the wrap
  helper `wrap()`, which collided with GDScript's global wrap() and failed to compile —
  renamed `wrap_text()`.
- action_bar.gd: spell slots show the icon (name label hidden) with a hover tooltip;
  key number/badge labels got an outline so they read over artwork. Skill slots and
  icon-less spells keep the text label.

  drag preview is the icon.

### Reanimated Tomb Mummy wired in + Kenji/Oni cat models slimmed (bit 1 of the town-cats/weather plan)
- Mummy: resized all 7 textures 4096²→2048² (folder 204MB→103MB), renamed the Mixamo clips
  to the `reanimated tomb mummy <animation>.fbx` convention, deleted the redundant Meshy

  Blender units (Meshy default, same as the existing 1.9m rat) — will be scaled to cat size
  when their scenes are built.
- Originals of everything touched were backed up outside the repo to
  ~/Documents/aldenexia-model-backups/2026-09-19/ (Kenji, Oni, reanimated tomb mummy).
- Correction to an earlier note in this session: `.gitattributes` sends `*.fbx` through Git
  LFS, so the 100MB GitHub file limit does NOT apply to FBX — the size was still worth
  fixing (rendering/load cost, LFS quota) but was never a push blocker. For reference the
  existing critter meshes are just as dense (rat 2.7M tris / 120MB, juvenile spider
  142MB) — left untouched, could be slimmed the same way.

  need looping when weather is built).

### World announcements + /who (multiplayer)
- Joining a world now announces itself to everyone else, and leaving does too — lines in
  `Data/world_announcements.json` (7 join / 5 leave, edit freely), e.g. "Zozuur, the 10th
  season voidknight, enters the world!" Placeholders {name} {level} (ordinal: 10th) {class}
  (lowercase). The sender picks the variant so every viewer sees the same line.
- A joining client announces itself once its character is loaded (player3d.gd
  `_announce_world_entry()` -> `Net.broadcast_world_announce()`); the HOST announces a
  departure from `Net._on_peer_disconnected()` (it still holds the leaver's puppet), and
  skips it while its own connection is tearing down so quitting doesn't spam "leaves".

  (client-to-client relay works), leave is announced by the host and relayed identically.
- Not done: /who and the announcements don't hide invisible players or tag game masters.

### Kenji + Oni (town cats), Give window, NPC respawn
- Kenji (`Scenes/kenji_npc.tscn`, `kenji_npc.gd`, built on VendorNPC for targeting/hail/faction) sits
  by the Town Gate at (6, 0, 9.5); cats don't speak, so the "quest" is EverQuest-style: guards'
  hail lines now mention him (3 new lines in guard_flavor_text.json), hailing him prints your
  emote text ("Meoww! Kenji meows at you while pawing at a rat's tail..."), `/pet` ("You pet Kenji.
  He begins to purr happily!" — Oni: "She begins...", both editable `pet_text`), right-click opens a
  small "Give to Kenji" window (`give_window.gd`) — drag an item into the slot, press Give.
  10 rat_tail -> 40 XP + "Kenji's Blessing" (+2 HP/Mana/Stamina regen, +3 to hit, 15 min,
  buff-bar entry added). He REMEMBERS partial hand-ins per character (save-file key
  `kenji_rat_tails_given`, so it survives logging out): give 3 now and 7 later and it completes at 10
  total, then resets (repeatable). He only takes what's still owed (carrying 5 with 3 owed -> keeps
  2); the Give window shows "Rat tails you've given Kenji: 3 / 10" and stays open after a partial
  hand-in. Wrong item -> an emote, nothing consumed; nothing to give -> an emote. Tested 16/16 incl.
  the save file, two characters with separate counts, and lowering the requirement under stored progress.
  All texts, counts, XP, buff duration and model_scale are @exports on the scene.
- Oni (`oni_npc.tscn`, `oni_npc.gd`, built on GuardNPC): patrols a 6-point loop through town
  (new OniPatrol1-6 markers), hunts rats within 60m of home, registers threat on every rat she hits
  so the rat's normal aggro table makes it fight back (same mechanism pets use) — rats CAN hurt
  her. Her kills give no loot/XP. Procedural bob + lunge instead of animations (unrigged mesh);
  emote-style chatter in Data/cat_flavor_text.json. Stats are @exports (lvl 3, 200 HP, 10 dmg);
  Kenji's `max_health` export is also 200 (vendors default to 100).
- New shared helpers: `cat_model.gd` (static cat mesh + PBR material, auto-grounds and floats
  the nameplate), `npc_respawner.gd`. ALL town NPCs (guards, vendors, Kenji, Oni) now have
  `die()` and respawn at their original spot after `respawn_seconds` (default 120): hidden,
  untargetable, dropped from every monster's aggro table meanwhile. Only Oni can actually die
  today — nothing else is targeted by monsters yet.
- Hooks: player3d.gd `_open_shop()` routes VendorNPCs that define `open_interaction()`
  (Kenji) to it, hail does the same; monster3d.gd names an NPC target by `npc_name` in the
  combat log; GuardNPC's animation lookup is now optional (unrigged NPCs).
- Verified in the real zone: both placed on flat ground, Oni walks her loop, engages a rat,
  the rat's threat table locks onto her and her HP drops, death hides/untargets and respawns at
  home, Kenji hand-in with real inventory (wrong item, 6/10, 14 -> 10 taken, blessing on the buff
  list), Give-window drag/drop. Model renders confirm both face forward and stand on the ground.

### Rain / weather (Lumora Outskirts)
- New `weather_manager.gd` node in the zone: random, infrequent rain — a 10-25 min quiet spell
  then a 2-5 min shower (host decides, RPC-synced; a peer joining mid-rain is told silently).
  Chat: "It begins to rain..." on start and "The clear sky begins to break from behind the
  clouds..." when it stops (both @exports; verified on host + clients, late joiners get no start message).
  Falling-rain particle box follows the local player, haze (fog) scaled by intensity, darker/greyer
  sky and light via new `DayNightCycle.set_weather_dim()`, and the looping rain sound
  (`Assets/boons_freak-rain-sound-188158.mp3`, SFX bus, loop set at runtime). All fade in/out.

  10-25 minute wait — remove it from game_log_window.gd/pause_menu.gd if unwanted.
- Tested: 11/11 zone checks + a 3-process multiplayer sync test; screenshots clear day, rainy day
  (overcast + haze + streaks) and rainy night.

### Ultravision (racial trait, now functional) — RENAMED Dark Sight / Improved Dark Sight, see below
- 10 of 11 races have `traits.ultravision: true` in character_options.json (all but Human).
  `DayNightCycle` reads the LOCAL player's race and, outdoors at night, lifts the moonlit sky,
  ambient and moon energy toward "bright" (viewer-local — each client renders its own lighting).
  Daytime is unchanged; a scene can set `outdoors = false` (Deep Darkness) for no benefit;
  works under rain (dimmer). Measured screen luminance at night: 0.086 (Human) vs 0.252 (Elf),
  noon identical (0.547); Dark Elf and "Half-Elf" resolve correctly. Tunable via the
  `ultravision_*` exports.
- OPEN DESIGN QUESTION (raised by the user): 10/11 races having it may be too many / leaves Human
  night-blind — nothing changed yet; proposed tiers (full: Dark Elf/Dwarf/Gnome/Troll, half: the rest)
  plus a carriable torch for everyone, awaiting the user's decision.

### Dark Sight tiers, the Light slot + torches, and the modern equipment tab (2026-09-19, later)
- Renamed the racial trait: "Improved Dark Sight" (full strength: Dwarf, Gnome, Troll, Dark Elf) and
  "Dark Sight" (half: Elf, Half-Elf, Halfling, Ogre, Half-Orc, Lizardkin); Human has neither. Data keys
  `improved_dark_sight` / `dark_sight` replace `ultravision`; `dark_sight_*` exports on DayNightCycle
  (`dark_sight_basic_strength` = 0.5). Measured night luminance: Human 0.086, Dark Sight 0.164, Improved
  0.252 (noon identical). Character creation now prints readable trait names ("• Dark Sight — ...") with
  descriptions from a new top-level `trait_descriptions` block (edit the wording there).
- FOUND AND REMOVED an older, separate ultravision from Session 46: a per-camera Environment override in
  camera_controller.gd fed by a hardcoded race list in player3d.gd. It could not do tiers, ignored the
  data, and would have stacked on top of the new system; its "one shared world" premise doesn't hold
  because each client renders its own lighting.
- New equipment slot "light" (Light Source) and working torches. `torch` is now a `light` item with a
  `light_source` block (radius 9, warm colour, 10 min burn, snuffed_by_rain, breaks_stealth). Use a torch
  from a bag (item popup -> Light) to equip ONE and light it; a "Lit Torch" effect on the buff bar (with
  the torch icon) is the burn timer; right-click it there or use Snuff Out on the slot to put it out
  (time kept, no free refills via unequip). Rain snuffs fire lights and stops them lighting; a lit fire
  light breaks the Shadowblade stealth stance and blocks entering it. Magic lights later just set those
  two flags false. Human torches burn 15 min (trait torch_burn_bonus 0.5). Carried light is a plain warm
  point light on the player, visible to everyone (light_item_id replicated); no held model/flames (the
  wall-torch particles were dropped — in-hand animation is a later step).
- Equipment tab redesigned to match the Stats tab: styled sections (ARMOR & JEWELRY, WEAPONS,
  ACCESSORIES, LIGHT SOURCE with a live "Torch — lit, 14:59 left" status), rounded tiles with the slot name
  inside when empty, gold frame when filled, warm frame when a light is lit, item hover tooltips (name,
  slot, stats, description, burn time), stack counts drawn on tiles (everywhere), Unequip/Light/Snuff
  buttons in the item popup for equipped gear.
- Fixed: item icons in slots could be covered by the tile background (drawn after the icon), and every
  slot/pet-gear/tradeskill/backpack icon loader used FileAccess.file_exists(), which fails for imported
  textures in EXPORTED builds — all now use ItemIcon.texture(). Unequip no longer snuffs a lit torch when
  the inventory is full (it fails and the torch stays lit).
- Tests: 25/25 torch logic checks in the real zone, Dark Sight tier luminance check, and earlier Kenji /
  pet suites re-run clean. NOT yet verified across two machines: another player seeing your carried light.

### Versioning + multiplayer join check
- There was NO version anywhere before (no config/version, empty export-preset version fields, no
  git tags), so numbering starts now at **0.1.0** (pre-release). Single source of truth:
  Project Settings -> Application -> Config -> Version (`config/version` in project.godot). New
  `Scripts/game_version.gd`: version(), build_id(), display() ("v0.1.0 (1ce742b)" / "(dev)" in the
  editor), is_compatible().
- Shown at the bottom right of the main menu (main_menu.gd) and printed in the log at startup.
- `tools/stamp_build.sh` writes the exact git commit (+ "-dirty" if uncommitted changes) to
  `Data/build_info.json` (git-ignored) — run it right before exporting so a build carries its commit.
- Multiplayer join check (net.gd): a client sends its version/build to the host on connect; the host
  accepts only if the version numbers match (and the build ids too when BOTH are stamped — unstamped
  editor runs only need the numbers to match). Mismatch -> the joiner sees "Version mismatch: the host
  is running X and you are running Y..." on the multiplayer screen; a build that never sends a version
  (older than this change) is dropped after 5s. Nothing spawns for the newcomer until they pass.
  IMPORTANT: every participant needs a build from this change onward — older exports can't join.
- Tested with 6 real processes (host + same build, unstamped editor build, wrong version, wrong build
  stamp, old build without the handshake): first two join, the next two get the message, the last is dropped.
- Bumping: raise the version in Project Settings when a change affects multiplayer compatibility
  (netcode/RPCs/replicated data/anything peers must agree on), and for each tagged test build.
- v0.1.0 -> **v0.2.0** (2026-09-19): the Light slot / Dark Sight batch added a replicated player value
  (`light_item_id`) and changed race trait data, so builds from before it must not mix with newer ones.
