## 2026-09-19 — Session 50: Multiplayer join/sync overhaul (joiners saw "Default Hero", monsters never hurt remote players, unsynced clock/NPCs), stance + Well Fed icons

Groundwork for a headless dedicated server (see Claude's memory `project_dedicated_server_exploration`):
a feasibility probe ran the real zone headless as an ENet server with no player — 27 monsters spawned
and ran their AI with zero errors — and turned up the bugs below.

### Multiplayer — joiners lost their character, monsters and the host's real name ("Default Hero")
- Root cause, reproduced with a real two-process test: the joiner connected from the MENU and only then
  loaded the zone. The host starts replicating the instant a peer connects (a spawn for every monster and
  player already in the world), and Godot silently drops a spawn whose spawner node doesn't exist yet on
  the receiving side (`on_spawn_receive: Parameter "spawner" is null`) — never resending it. So a joiner
  got no character of their own, no initial monsters, and the host's pre-placed node never received its
  name/race/model (hence "Default Hero"; the host saw the joiner fine because the host's zone existed).
  Synchronizer visibility filters do NOT hold spawn packets back (tested in isolation), so the fix is
  ordering: `Net.begin_join()` loads the zone FIRST, and the zone's `multiplayer_player_spawner.gd`
  connects from its `_ready` (`Net.complete_pending_join()`), by which point every spawner exists.
- The zone's pre-placed player is now single-player only. In a multiplayer game EVERY player, the host
  included, is spawned through the `PlayerSpawner`; `Net.omit_preplaced_player` makes `player3d.gd`
  free the pre-placed copy in `_enter_tree()` (and `camera_controller.gd` stay inert for it). A dedicated
  server (`Net.is_dedicated_server`) simply doesn't spawn peer 1. Verified: a joiner on a player-less server
  sees only themselves — no phantom "host".
- Spawned players now appear at the zone's real start (`spawn_position` export, the old pre-placed
  transform) instead of the world origin — which also stops a NEW character's bind point being saved as (0,0,0).
- A join that fails (bad address, version mismatch) or a server that drops mid-game now returns to the
  main menu with the reason shown on the multiplayer screen (previously a lost server left the joiner in a
  zone quietly running its own spawn logic). The joiner sees a "Connecting to ..." cover until their own
  character exists.

### Multiplayer — monsters never actually hurt remote players
- Reproduced: an aggressive skeleton stood on the client for 20 s; the SERVER's copy of the client's HP fell
  172 -> 153 -> 105 while the client's own HP stayed 172. `resolve_attack()` mutated the server's stat-less
  puppet, and HP replicates owner -> everyone, never back. (Session 46's fix only made monsters TARGET remote
  players.) Now `monster3d.gd::perform_attack()` sends the target's OWNER a `_rpc_resolve_attack_on_owner`; the owner
  rolls dodge/parry/block/damage against their real character and HP, which then replicates out. Works for
  pets too (owner-authoritative). Any riposte damage is undone on the owner's puppet copy and relayed to the
  server via `apply_networked_damage`. Re-verified: client HP now falls 172 -> 154 -> 144 -> 134.
- The shared swing logic moved into `_resolve_attack_on(target, relayed)`.

### Combat text — "You" on the wrong screen
- The server's monster `player` reference is only the NEAREST player, so a hit on the remote player was
  logged on the HOST's screen as "hits **you**" (and "attacks you!" on aggro). Both now go to the actual
  target's owner; everyone else nearby gets a named version ("A Desert Scavenger hits Zozuur for 7 damage!")
  via the existing `Net.broadcast_combat_message`. Player melee/spell broadcasts were already third-person
  named — verified with a two-player fight (each reads "You ..." for themselves, the other's name for the other).

### Multiplayer — world clock and town NPCs
- Time of day: every peer used to start its own clock at 12:00. The server is now the clock: `Global.send_time_to()`
  pushes it to a joiner right after the version check, then every 10 s (`_rpc_sync_game_time`); peers keep ticking
  locally in between. Verified: host at 20:xx, joiner adopts it.
- Guards and Oni (GuardNPC): AI now runs only on the server (`_is_puppet()` gate in `_physics_process`); other peers
  replay position/rotation/velocity/state/attack_seq/visible/HP from a `MultiplayerSynchronizer` added to
  `guard_npc.tscn` and `oni_npc.tscn`. Unprompted speech (banter, engage callouts, the day/night scripted guard
  conversation) is decided on the server and relayed (`say_to_all`), each peer applying its own hearing range;
  a hail reply stays local to whoever hailed. Guard fight lines are broadcast too. Death/respawn hides and
  un-targets the puppet via `NPCRespawner.mirror_hidden()`. Verified: host vs joiner NPC positions within
  ~0.3 m, same patrol/engage states. Kenji and the vendors are static and unchanged.

### UI
- Stances now have icons: `class_stances.json` gained an `"icon"` field (same convention as spells); the stance bar
  shows it (name moved to a wrapped tooltip) and the buff bar shows it for an active stance. PLACEHOLDER art from
  existing icons (Shadow Ascendant = sword, Necrotic Bastion = shield, Dusk Walker = self-buff, Stealth = self-ward) —
  edit the JSON path when real art exists.
- "Well Fed" buff icon: mapped to the food item icon in `buff_bar.gd`'s `ENVIRONMENTAL_EFFECT_ITEM_ICONS`
  (generic `food.png` — swap when dedicated art exists). Campfire Warmth and Kenji's Blessing still have no icon.

### Version
- v0.2.0 -> **v0.3.0** (2026-09-19): new RPCs (monster attack/aggro relay, world-clock sync, NPC speech) and a new join
  order, so 0.2.0 builds must not mix with 0.3.0. Stamp a fresh build (commit -> tools/stamp_build.sh -> export).

### Session 50 follow-ups (after the first 0.3.0 test build)
- Old characters' inventory/equipment icons: a saved item is a full copy of its definition, including the icon path from the
  day it was created, so every pre-Session-49 character kept dead paths (`Assets/789_Lorc_RPG_icons/...` = flat white
  silhouettes, `Assets/Items/torch.png` = blank slot; the ragged starter set all showed one white shield). `Inventory.load_inventory_data()`
  now calls `refresh_item_icons()` (bags, bank, equipped; pet gear via `player3d.gd`), re-reading the icon from items.json. Presentational
  only; nothing else on the saved item changes. Checked against Zozuur's real save: 21 items, 0 missing icons.
- Tab: target cycling also left Godot's built-in Tab focus navigation enabled, so each press shifted keyboard focus around the HUD
  (proved: with a HUD button focused, Tab hopped Invite <-> Disband). Movement is skipped whenever the chat `LineEdit` has focus, which
  matches "Tab targets but I can't move". `player3d.gd::_input()` now consumes the Tab press (unless a LineEdit is focused, so typing is
  unaffected). Verified focus stays put and targeting still cycles. Not reproduced end-to-end in the exact "stuck" state.
- Rats: Oni (home ~(22,20), hunt_range 60 + leash 15) reached both original rat spawns, and her kills use die(false, false) so nothing she kills
  drops loot — no tails ever reached the player. Added four rat spawn entries ~100 m out (95,45) (-80,60) (85,-65) (-95,-35),
  all on the navmesh and beyond her reach, `max_active` 12. NOTE: mob_spawner3d.gd counts active monsters PER TYPE across all entries, so
  a new entry's max_active is really the type-wide cap. Probe after 50 s: 11 rats alive beyond her reach, 1 near town.
- Oni's `hunt_range` 60 -> 25 m (user's call), so with the 15 m leash she now stops at 40 m from her home (~(22,20)). Of the original rat spawns only
  the one near the gate is still inside it; the other and the four new far ones are out of reach.
- Nameplates through walls: every NameLabel/TitleLabel (monsters, guards, Oni, Kenji, vendors, pets, players) had `no_depth_test = true`,
  which draws a Label3D over all geometry. Now depth-tested with `alpha_cut = 2` (opaque prepass, crisp edges). Screenshot-verified: Kenji's
  and a guard's names no longer show through the town wall.
- Clock: re-tested with same-instant sampling — host and joiner identical to the minute over 20 samples. Cause of the reported mismatch not found yet.

- Chat channels + `/played` (see the Commands reference at the top). New `chat_channels.gd` (`ChatChannels`) holds channel names/colors/line
  formats so the sender's echo, the receiver's line (`net.gd`) and the dropdown (`game_log_window.gd`) can't drift. New RPCs
  `_rpc_receive_say` / `_rpc_receive_zone_message`; the say range is judged on the SENDER from replicated positions, so a far
  player is never sent the line. Verified with two processes: heard at 1.7 m, not at 40 m; zone reached 40 m; sticky Tell worked.
  The dropdown takes no keyboard focus (click only) so it can't steal movement keys or reintroduce the Tab-focus problem.
- Playtime persistence bug: `playtime_seconds` only ever reached the character sheet's display, so every save kept 0. `Global.save_player_data_to_file()`
  now banks `get_total_playtime()`. Verified: 120 s banked + ~16 s played saved as 136.
- `Global.format_birthday()` had never been called and would have crashed: it indexed arrays with the floats JSON returns. Now split into
  `format_birthday_ingame()` / `format_birthday_real()` with int casts, plus `format_real_age()`.
- Test-harness lesson: rewriting a save with Python's json.dump writes `Infinity`, which Godot can't parse (Godot itself writes/reads `1e99999`);
  the character then fails to load and the game falls back to the dev defaults, whose autosave overwrote `dev_character_stats.json` (the
  dev-defaults test character, regenerated identically). Test scripts now refuse to run unless their own character loaded.

### Version
- v0.3.0 -> **v0.3.1** (2026-09-19): Say/Zone chat RPCs were added after the 0.3.0 build, so 0.3.0 and 0.3.1 must not mix. From here follow-up
  batches bump the last number (0.3.1, 0.3.2, ...); the middle number is for bigger steps. Stamp a fresh build (commit -> tools/stamp_build.sh -> export).

### Known / left alone
- `Node 'CombatNode:current_hp' not found` log noise on joiners: the CombatNode is created in `_ready()`, after the
  spawn packet's initial values apply. Values arrive on the next change, so it is cosmetic.
- Riposte/attack-debuffs on a monster live only on the server's copy; a remote player's swing resolves against the
  owner's puppet of the monster, so a debuffed monster hits a remote player at full strength.
- Still no server-side re-verification of reported damage; loot is still rolled per-client when opened.

---

## 2026-09-19 — Session 51: Dedicated server (step 1) — headless `--server` startup + server-stored characters (v0.4.0)

First two pieces of the headless "Join a Server" build (see Claude's memory `project_dedicated_server_exploration`).
The LAN host/join flow is untouched apart from a handshake tweak (below). Version bumped 0.3.1 -> 0.4.0: the
`_rpc_version_accepted` signature changed and new login RPCs exist, so older builds are refused at the version check.

### Server startup (`net.gd`)
- `godot --headless --path . -- --server [--name=test] [--port=8910] [--max-players=6]` (also `--port 8910` style), or any
  build with the `dedicated_server` feature, skips the splash and every menu: sets `Net.is_dedicated_server`, caps
  `Engine.max_fps` at 60 (a headless server has no vsync and would otherwise spin a core), hosts, loads the zone.
- `--name` is the server's short name (letters/numbers); it prefixes stored characters. Bad name or a port already in use
  prints an error and exits with code 1.
- `[server]` console lines for start-up, peers connecting/leaving (n/max), logins, refusals, and each player's
  "join/leave: Name (level N Class)" announcement.
- A dedicated server's `max_players` are ALL free seats (a LAN host still occupies one for their own character).

### Characters live on the server (`net.gd`, `global.gd`)
- Stored as `user://server_characters/<server>_<player>_character_stats.json` (e.g. `test_zozuur_...`) — a separate folder
  from `user://saves` so a server run on the developer's own machine doesn't pollute the single-player/LAN character lists.
  The in-game display name is still the plain player name ("Zozuur"); `test_` is only the storage key.
- Handshake: version check -> server marks the peer "awaiting login" (20 s timeout) -> client `_rpc_login(name, creation_json)`
  -> server loads (or, with creation_json, creates) the character -> `_rpc_login_ok(json)` -> client fills `Global.player_data`
  and answers `_rpc_login_ready` -> ONLY THEN the server emits `player_connected`, which spawns the puppet. (The character must
  be in place before the puppet's `_ready` reads it.)
- Refusals (client gets `Net.last_failure_kind` + a readable reason and returns to the multiplayer screen): `invalid_name`
  (letters/numbers only, 2-16), `no_character`, `name_taken` (creating over an existing one), `already_online` (one peer per
  character), `invalid_character` (created characters must be level 1 — you can't upload a maxed-out local save), `server_error`,
  `version`, and `wrong_mode` (a LAN client reaching a dedicated server or vice versa; `_rpc_version_accepted` now carries
  `is_dedicated` + the server name).
- Saving: every existing `Global.save_player_data_to_file()` call site still works. In `Net.remote_character_mode` it uploads
  the JSON to the server (`_rpc_save_character`) instead of writing `user://saves`; bursts of saves collapse into one upload
  1 s later. The server accepts a save only from the peer that logged that character in, only if it parses, is under 1 MB and
  its `player_name` matches, and writes it atomically (temp file + rename). Each login refreshes a `.bak` copy of the last save.
- Leaving right after a save no longer loses it: `Net.disconnect_game()` waits (up to 2 s) for the server to acknowledge every
  outstanding upload. Found by test: `close()` drops unsent ENet data and the server discards packets that arrive alongside a
  disconnect, so a graceful-disconnect drain alone was NOT enough — the explicit ack is what makes it reliable.
- Camp-out / Save & Exit (`game_log_window.gd`, `pause_menu.gd`) now call `Net.disconnect_game()`. Previously the connection
  lingered behind the main menu (true for LAN too); on a server that kept the character "already online" until the ghost
  connection timed out. `/exit` (quit) also disconnects so the last upload lands before the process ends.
- `Global.save_player_data_to_file()` split into `serialize_player_data()` (refresh position/playtime, return JSON) + the write.

### Verified (real headless server + real client processes)
- create -> load back round trip, batched save, save-then-leave (2222 landed), and every refusal above; two clients can't share a
  character; LAN host+join still works and each side sees both players; wrong-mode messages both directions. Test saves were
  backed up/restored around the runs; nothing in the real `user://saves` changed.

### Passwords, character delete, "Join a Server" screen (same session, still v0.4.0 — nothing released yet)
- Per-character PASSWORDS on a server. `_rpc_login(name, password, creation_json)`. Stored as a salted, stretched SHA-256 hash in a
  SIDECAR file (`<server>_<player>_auth.json`) — never in the save the client uploads. Creating needs >= 4 characters; 5 wrong tries lock
  that character for 60 s (`bad_password` with tries left, then `locked`). A character with no auth file (pre-password, or the owner deleted
  the file to reset a forgotten password) takes whatever password its next login supplies (`password_set`). Passwords still cross the wire
  unencrypted (ENet is plain UDP) — DTLS is a possible later step.
- DELETE a character, local and server. One shared dialog (`delete_character_dialog.gd`): the Delete button stays disabled until the
  character's name is typed exactly (case-sensitive), plus a password field for server characters. Local: permanent (`Global.delete_local_character`),
  buttons added to the Load Game screen and next to the LAN screen's character dropdown. Server: needs the password, refused while the
  character is online or has no password yet, and the files are MOVED to `server_characters/deleted/` (never erased) so the owner can undo.
  Server delete is a menu "errand" (`Net.request_server_delete` -> connect, version check, delete, disconnect, no zone; emits
  `server_request_done`) — the same mechanism a future online/version status probe will use.
- "Join a Server" main-menu button + `join_server_menu.gd`: server dropdown from `Data/servers.json` (ships with one placeholder entry,
  127.0.0.1:8910 — put the real address there) or a typed `host[:port]`; character name + password; Enter World, Create New Character
  (password typed twice), Delete Character. Remembers the last address/character in settings.json. A failed or dropped join returns here
  (`Global.return_to_join_server_menu`) with the reason; the LAN screen behaves as before.
- Creating a server character: `character_creation.tscn` runs in server mode (`Global.server_creation`): Confirm validates the name (letters/
  numbers, 2-16) and keeps the character in memory, Begin becomes "Enter the World" and hands it to `Net.begin_join_server()`. Nothing is
  written to `user://saves`.
- Safety fixes found while wiring this: leaving or losing a server now clears the working character (`Net._forget_remote_character`) so a later
  save can't drop a copy of a server character into `user://saves`; the "server lost" handler no longer saves a server character locally.
- Wording: camp messages are now "You start preparing to camp." / "You abandon your camp preparations." (also used by /exit and Save & Exit).

### Verified (real windowed screens + headless server)
- Every screen driven programmatically with screenshots: Join a Server validation messages, server delete (wrong password keeps the dialog
  open, right one deletes and closes it), creating a character on the server through the real creation screen and arriving in the world,
  Load Game and LAN-menu local deletes (wrong-case name keeps Delete disabled), and a failed join returning to the Join screen with the reason.
  Protocol-level: creation/password/lockout/reset/delete refusals, recreate-after-delete. Test saves/settings were backed up and restored.

### Encryption, server-list status, save-on-shutdown, server export (2026-09-20, still v0.4.0 — nothing released yet)
- ENCRYPTION (DTLS over ENet) for every dedicated server; LAN games stay plain. The server owns a key + self-signed certificate
  (created on first start in `user://server_tls/`, or `--tls-dir=`); a client build carries the matching PUBLIC certificate,
  `Data/server_cert.crt`, and trusts only that exact certificate (`TLSOptions.client(cert, "aldenexia")`), so an impostor server or a
  man-in-the-middle can't be talked to. Tested: right certificate connects; a server with a different certificate is rejected in ~30 ms and
  sees no connection; an unencrypted client can't reach an encrypted server. `server.key` must stay private (never in the project).
  The certificate generated on this dev machine was copied to `Data/server_cert.crt` so builds work with the server run from this machine.
- ONLINE / VERSION / PLAYERS indicator: `Net.probe_server()` (a menu errand, like delete) connects, does the version check and reports name,
  players/max, version. The Join a Server list probes each entry one at a time: colored dot + "(2/6)" / "(offline)" / "(update needed)" /
  "(full)" on each item, a status line for the selected one, a Refresh button, and typing an address probes it. A dedicated server has
  8 spare connection slots (`MENU_SLOTS`) so a FULL server still answers probes; the player cap is enforced at login (`server_full`).
- SAVE ON SHUTDOWN: Godot's headless server can't catch SIGTERM/SIGINT (it dies instantly), so a graceful stop is requested by creating a
  stop file (default `user://server_stop`, or `--stop-file=`) — `tools/run_server.sh` does that on Ctrl-C/SIGTERM/SIGHUP (tested both), and a
  window close request does it too. The server then tells every player's game to upload its character right now, waits for each to confirm
  (up to 8 s), saves the world clock, and exits. Players get "The server shut down. Your character was saved." on the Join screen. New
  logins are refused while it shuts down. Tested: an UNSAVED change made just before the stop landed on the server.
- WORLD CLOCK persists across restarts (`user://server_state_<name>.json`, saved every 5 minutes and on shutdown, restored at start-up).
- `tools/run_server.sh` (the launcher to use) and `tools/aldenexia-server.service.example` (systemd; needs `KillMode=mixed` so the wrapper, not Godot,
  receives SIGTERM).
- EXPORT PRESET "Linux Server" (`Builds/Server/Aldenexia_Server.x86_64` + `libterrain.linux.release.x86_64.so`, keep them together).
  Finding: `dedicated_server=true` alone does NOT strip anything; stripping needs `export_filter="customized"` AND an explicit
  `customized_files={"res://": "strip"}` (proved on a tiny test project: 12.5 MB -> 2.5 KB). Result: 1.2 GB -> 155 MB (73 MB of it the engine
  template), ~650 MB -> ~130 MB RAM. Excludes Builds/archive-2d/demo/tools/.blend/.old/.tmp.
- STARTUP WORLD CHECK: 4 s after the zone loads the server logs `World check: ground under spawn OK, navmesh OK (1 region, N-point test path), N
  monsters` (and WARNINGs if collision or navigation are missing) — this is how the stripped build proved collision + navmesh survived.
  Verified against the dev server: same ground/navmesh result, and monsters (rat, snake, skeleton, bat) chase and hit a real client identically
  in the stripped export (scarabs/slimes are passive in both).
- Stdout is block-buffered in release exports (log lines lagged by minutes when piped/journald), so `project.godot` now has
  `run/flush_stdout_on_print.dedicated_server=true` — a feature-tag override that only affects dedicated-server builds.
- Regressions re-run: LAN host+join unchanged (both players visible on both sides); the full UI flow passes against the encrypted server.

### First real deployment fixes + deploy script (2026-09-20)
- The first run on the real server showed two problems that a test from inside the project folder had hidden (Godot resolves some files
  from the current directory there): (1) `day_night_cycle.gd` assigned the sky material to a `ProceduralSkyMaterial` variable, but the stripped
  export turns materials into `PlaceholderMaterial` — `_ready()` aborted; now only real sky materials are kept. (2) The Terrain3D GDExtension
  (used by no scene) failed to load from a folder without the addon; it is now excluded from the "Linux Server" preset, so the build is just the
  one binary (no libterrain file). ALWAYS test an export by running it from a neutral folder.
- Re-verified in a neutral folder: 0 script errors through join, chase, monster attacks, player attacks and kills, and the graceful stop.
- Shutdown no longer logs errors while exiting: it disconnects players but keeps the peer open until the process quits.
- Noted (harmless, exit only): ~60 "Attempt to disconnect a nonexistent connection ... tree_exiting" engine messages while a server with monsters is torn down.
- The first server start had created its OWN TLS key because the key hadn't been copied over — clients would have seen it as offline. `tools/deploy_server.sh`
  now warns when the server's certificate differs from `Data/server_cert.crt`.
- `tools/deploy_server.sh`: one-command update of the server machine (`--export`, `--restart CMD`, `--tls` first-time key copy that never overwrites an
  existing key without `--force-tls`, `--host`/`--dir`). Uploads under temporary names then renames (a running binary can't be overwritten: "Text file
  busy"), verifies the sha256, checks the certificate match. Tested against stand-in ssh/scp.
- `Data/servers.json` now lists `test` (internal IP) and `test-public` (vpn.nctlv.org) as two entries; the second reads "offline" until the port forward exists.

### Kenji turn-in / click targeting fix (2026-09-20, after the first real-server session)
- Report: couldn't hand a rat tail to Kenji. The hand-in is right-click Kenji -> drag the tail into the Give window -> Give (not a drag onto the cat).
  The hand-in itself works everywhere (verified single player AND on the dedicated server: window opens, drop + Give consume the tails, the count is saved
  in the character, and a brand-new session still remembers it: given=5 on the server's save).
- Root cause of the right-click failing: `player3d.gd::_raycast_world_hit` reports only the FIRST physics hit. Simulating clicks on Kenji from 3 distances x 4
  directions (headless, real zone): his hitbox is fine (capsule matches the model) and 3 of 4 sides hit him 100%, but from the +Z side every click hit the level's own
  wall/gate collision instead (0/49 at every distance), and from another side the player's own pet body took 21/49 clicks. Overall 68% of body clicks reached him.
- Fix: `_vendor_near_mouse()` — when the ray hits something that isn't an NPC (wall, pet, creature, ground), pick the nearest VendorNPC whose body centre is within 55 px
  of the cursor and 12 m of the player. Used by right-click (shop/Give window) and left-click targeting. Result: 588/588 body clicks reach him, and clicks 160+ px away
  don't select him. Clicks that hit a real target (monster, guard, ally) are untouched; corpse looting is proximity based and unaffected.
- CLIENT-ONLY change: needs a client rebuild. A new commit changes the build stamp, so a stamped client and the deployed server (098e0d7) won't match until the server is
  re-exported/deployed from the same commit (the editor build, unstamped, connects to any server on the same version).

### EverQuest-style hand-in, draggable Give window, log noise (2026-09-20)
- Report: the user tried to left-click DRAG a rat tail from their bags straight onto Kenji (like EverQuest) — that never did anything (Godot only delivers drops to UI
  controls). Now it works: `slot_button.gd` notices a drag from a bag/basic slot that ended over the world (no UI accepted it, cursor not over a window), and asks
  `player3d.gd::try_offer_item_to_npc_at()` which finds the NPC under the cursor (same forgiving pick as right-click) and calls its `receive_item_drop()`. Kenji opens the Give
  window with the item ALREADY in the slot (`GiveWindow.offer_item()`); the player still presses Give. Too far (> 6 m) -> the usual "too far away" message. Right-click -> Give
  window still works. Verified with real pushed mouse events (press on a slot, drag, release): slot captured the item, drag-end handler ran, Give window opened with the
  tail offered, Give consumed the tails; releasing on empty ground or from 10 m away opens nothing.
- The Give window is now draggable like the other windows (press anywhere on its panel and drag; position saved per character under "give_window" via WindowPosition). Verified:
  moved by exactly the dragged amount, saved, and re-opens where it was left.
- Server log flood: "WARNING: Exponent too high (built_in_strtod)" was Godot's warning for the `1e99999` "infinite" durations of permanent buffs, printed once per buff on every
  save-upload validation and login. The server now validates a copy with `1e99999` swapped for `1e308` (the stored text is unchanged). Client-side loads of a save with permanent
  buffs still print it in the editor console (harmless; the value must stay infinite there).
- New server log line `[server] Long frame: N s (players)` for any server frame over 0.5 s — stalls long enough make every client time out and the server look "offline"
  for a moment, and this is how we would see it (relevant on a shared VM).

### Server sizing (measured 2026-09-20, exported stripped server, headless clients that fight/teleport/save/open windows)
- 1 client for 270 s: no disconnect, no server stalls (0 "Long frame"), ~150-205 KB/s streamed to the client, RTT 12 ms, packet loss < 0.1%.
- 6 simultaneous clients for 120 s: all stayed connected, 0 stalls, 0 script errors. Server CPU 0.56 of ONE core (it is essentially single-threaded), RSS 126 MB, 20 threads,
  ~225 KB/s per player (about 1.35 MB/s = ~11 Mbit/s total upload for 6 players; scales with the number of monsters alive).
- Recommended VM for 6 players: 2 dedicated vCPUs (NOT oversubscribed — a few seconds of CPU steal stalls the loop and clients time out), 1-2 GB RAM, ~1 GB disk, Linux glibc >= 2.28,
  no GPU/X libs, and ~15 Mbit/s of upload if players come in over the internet (LAN is no issue).
- Observation to look at later: the number of monsters in the zone grew from ~25 at start to ~70 during active fighting (spawner respawns + corpses that linger), and
  replication bandwidth grows with it.

### Batch after the first real sessions (2026-09-20): casting messages, /played, Bryn, disconnect diagnostics
- Casting messages now go to the COMBAT window: the caster's own "You begin casting a spell.", effect lines (Life Siphon, slows, roots, buffs, immunities/resists, songs
  starting/ending...) use `GameLog.log_combat` (31 calls in `player3d.gd`'s spell code). Refusals/usage errors ("No target selected", "Insufficient mana", "not ready", "must be
  level N") stay in General as feedback. Other players already received these in their combat window via the existing broadcast. Verified by casting Life Siphon and reading both channels.
- /played showed only the current session because play time was banked only when a SAVE happened, and saves are event-driven — a session ending in a dropped connection,
  a closed window or a crash banked ~nothing (measured: 15 s played, 1 s banked). `Global` now autosaves once a minute while a character is in the world (`AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL`) and
  on window close. Output reads "Total time played: X. This session: Y." Verified on the dedicated server: a 75 s session that never saved still banked 60 s and the next login started from it.
- Sergeant Bryn froze mid-patrol. Reproduced by fast-forwarding the real zone: he stood exactly on a path corner at (-29.6, -200) forever. Cause: corner/waypoint ARRIVAL used 3D
  distance, but navmesh points sit ~0.8 m above the floor his feet are on, so 0.2 m away horizontally was 0.83 m in 3D (> the 0.75 m threshold) and he never advanced. Now measured on the
  ground plane (`GuardNPC._flat_distance`). Safety nets so a guard can never stand still forever: PATROL — no progress toward a waypoint for 20 s -> skip to the next; ENGAGE — chasing without
  getting closer for 8 s -> give up and ignore that monster for 30 s (`_ignored_targets`). Oni shares the movement code and got the same engage watchdog. Verified: Bryn completed the whole route
  (Town Gate -> Mausoleum -> Dock -> South Zone -> Traveling Merchant -> Signpost -> Archery Targets -> Town Gate) and looped again with no stalls (~5 game-hours fast-forwarded).
- Disconnect diagnostics: server log lines now carry a wall-clock time; the server logs `Long frame: N s` for stalls and clients log `[client] This game stalled for N s`,
  a `[client] link to server: RTT.. loss..` line every 30 s and the last reading when the connection is lost. Both stall detectors use real elapsed time — Godot clamps the `delta` it passes
  to _process, so a delta-based detector never fires on a long stall (found by testing). Findings: a client can freeze 25 s and stay connected; a 6 s server freeze is logged and survived. The user
  reported random disconnects on a laptop over WiFi but NONE from a wired PC, which points at the WiFi link rather than the server.
- Monsters standing still: the existing wander logic (`monster3d.gd::state_patrol`) mostly never worked. Measured over 3 game-minutes with no player near: slimes, bandits and bats moved
  ~0-1 m, only 3 of 6 rats moved. Cause: a wander leg picked a random point and re-picked only when the monster got within 1.0 m (3D), but the nav agent stops at
  `target_desired_distance` = 1.5 m and calls itself finished — a permanent deadlock 1.5 m short of the point. Now: ground-plane arrival at 2.0 m, then a pause (IDLE) before the next leg,
  wander points snapped onto the navmesh (6 tries), and a 6 s no-progress watchdog. After: every non-passive type moves (37-153 m walked in 3 game-minutes, within ~10-32 m of home).
  Passive monsters (dune scarab) still stay put until aggroed, by design — open question for the user. Note: monsters now roam more, so they meet guards/players more often.
- Connection patience: ENet drops a silent connection after ~5-6 s by default; measured — a server/network stall of 6+ s dropped a client (WiFi power-save/roaming or a starved VM easily does that).
  Both ends now use `set_timeout(32, 20000, 60000)` (PEER_TIMEOUT_*): 12 s stalls are survived, a stall past ~24 s still drops. Cost: a crashed/dropped client lingers on the server ~33 s.
  So a correctly authenticated re-login now TAKES OVER the old connection instead of being refused "already in the world" (`_peer_holding`, graceful disconnect + immediate puppet removal;
  a forced disconnect left a zombie peer and "Invalid target peer" errors). Characters with no password are never taken over. A wrong password leaves the online player untouched.
- Camping text: "You begin to prepare your camp." followed by a chat countdown "15 seconds remaining" ... "1 second remaining", then "You finish breaking camp." and the camp-out. Same channel for
  /camp, /exit and Save & Exit; damage still interrupts ("You abandon your camp preparations."). Verified with a real 15 s camp and an interrupted one.
### From the first public-server test with a remote friend (2026-09-20)
- Reanimated mummy death animation looped forever. Two causes, both fixed in `monster3d.gd`: (1) its animation library was saved with EVERY clip set to play once (idle/walk/run too) — movement clips are
  now forced to loop at load; (2) other players' games restart any clip that has finished, because a finished one-shot leaves `current_animation` empty which read as "not playing the replicated state" — a
  one-shot state (death, an attack swing) now plays once per state change. Verified: death clip started once in 6 s (it is 2.3 s long).
- Left-click targeting in a fight: the click took the FIRST thing under the cursor, so a corpse (lingers ~60 s, untargetable), your own pet or a wall in front of the enemy swallowed it. New
  `_pick_target_at()` passes the ray THROUGH everything and picks (1) the nearest live monster on the ray, (2) else the nearest other targetable (guard/vendor/pet/player), (3) else the live monster/NPC
  whose centre is within 45 px of the cursor (a near miss). Verified with a corpse directly in front of a live monster (raw ray hits the corpse, the pick finds the monster), a lone corpse selects
  nothing, a click 30 px beside a monster picks it, 140 px away does not. Your own pet is only picked when no live monster is on the ray (F-keys still select group members/pets).
- Earth Totem never warded other players: its scan covered pets, guards and vendors but not the `player` group. Nearby players (8 m) are now included and the effect is relayed to their own machine
  (`_buff_target`). Verified with two clients on the dedicated server: the second player received `earth_totem`.
- Test 2 (Troubadour): (1) Musicianship never levelled — casting trained `spell_casting` and the spell's own school (Song of Courage = enchantment) but nothing ever trained musicianship. Every Troubadour spell is a
  song, so each song cast now also trains musicianship (`cast_spell`). (2) Skill cap: skills and spell schools can only be trained to `skill_cap_per_level` x character level (new knob in
  `Data/combat_balance.json`, shipped as 4 = level 1 caps at 4 points, level 10 at 40; 0 = no cap, the old behaviour). A skill is never lowered, so starting skills above the cap (a Voidknight's necromancy 10,
  a Troubadour's enchantment 8) simply wait for the level to catch up. `Player3D.skill_cap_for()`; the Skills page now shows level / cap instead of level / 275 (its bar fills toward the cap).
  Verified with a level-1 Troubadour casting 120 songs (musicianship 0 -> 4 and stops; enchantment/spell casting stay at 8), then level 5 (cap 20: musicianship 19, enchantment 17, spell casting 13, none above 20).
- Findings not yet acted on (waiting on decisions): the parry/dodge/weapon_mastery skills do not feed any formula (dodge = class base + DEX, parry = class base + DEX + weapon skill) and Voidknights are not
  seeded with them; all pets are melee-only (2.5 m) so ranged pets/projectiles are a new feature; the Windows exe uses Godot's DEFAULT robot icon (`icon.svg`) and Godot has no `rcedit` configured to embed a
  custom one when exporting from Linux; Campfire Warmth and Kenji's Blessing have no buff-bar icon.
- COMBAT TUNING: every combat number is now in `Data/combat_balance.json` (read by `Scripts/combat_balance.gd`, `CombatBalance.num(key)`; each key has a neutral fallback = the old behaviour, and
  the file's `_help` block explains each one). Knobs: `player_hit_bonus`, `monster_hit_bonus`, `hit_level_diff_per_level`, `hit_min`/`hit_max`, `player_melee_damage_mult`,
  `monster_melee_damage_mult`, `player_spell_damage_mult`, `monster_hp_mult`, `monster_hp_per_level`, `monster_damage_per_level`, `monster_scale_min/max`. Players vs monsters are told apart by
  group in `combatnode.gd` (guards/pets stay neutral). Shipped values = the proposed defaults: player_hit_bonus 35 (a level-1 Voidknight now hits Lv2-4 monsters ~49-72% instead of 17-36%),
  player_spell_damage_mult 0.5 (Life Siphon 52 -> 26), monster HP/damage +12% per level above the monster's base level in monsters.json (rat Lv1/2/3 = 20/22/25 HP; snake Lv3 = 31).
  Monster accuracy is unchanged (13-57% on a level-1 player). Set any value back to its neutral number to undo it. Player hit/damage is computed on each player's machine and monster HP/damage
  on the server, so the server and the clients must ship the same file.
- WEAPON SKILL FIX: at login the equipped weapon did not exist yet when `_sync_weapon_skill()` ran (inventory loads after `load_character_data`), so accuracy came from a baseline skill (10) until the
  first "you've become better at..." skill-up applied the real value. Now synced again after the equipment loads (`load_player_data_from_global`); the baseline is only a fallback when the real skill is 0.
  Also found: saved item copies carry stale skill names (a saved rusty sword said "1h slashing"; today it is `slashing_weapons`, which is also the class starting skill), so the weapon skill and the
  skill being TRAINED could disagree. `_weapon_skill_key()` now reads the CURRENT item definition for the sync and both training call sites, and `_migrate_legacy_skills()` maps the old names
  (`1h_slashing` etc.) onto the current ones keeping the higher value so old characters lose nothing. Verified: fresh Voidknight = skill 8 / ATK 38 at login, +1 skill = +2 ATK with no jump;
  a legacy save with `1h_slashing: 47` keeps 47 as `slashing_weapons`.

### Follow-ups to the test-1/test-2 findings (2026-09-20) — icon, ranged-pet projectile, skills wired into the formulas, testing log
- (Correction to the "findings" bullet above: Wildspeaker and Spiritweaver (Phantasmal Echo) pets were ALREADY ranged — 14 m, one shot per 3 s; only the base pet is melee. What was missing was the visible shot.)
- EXE ICON: `Assets/app_icon.png` / `Assets/app_icon.ico` (the neon cat from the studio logo, cropped, rounded corners; ICO 16-256 px). `project.godot` `config/icon` and the Windows export preset's
  `application/icon` point at them. Godot 4.7 embeds the icon itself — no rcedit needed. Verified: a Windows export's exe carries the icon (checked in the PE resources). Placeholder until real art exists.
- RANGED PET PROJECTILE: `Scripts/spectral_bolt.gd` rewritten — a bright green core with a soft halo, a particle trail + drifting sparkles behind it, a burst of particles and a short light flash on impact
  (additive, unshaded soft-dot particles). Phantasmal Echo shoots teal-green (0.3, 1.0, 0.7), the Wildspeaker's spirit lime-green (0.45, 1.0, 0.25) — `BOLT_COLOR` in each pet script.
  Before, the bolt was a local node that only the pet OWNER's machine ever created, so other players saw a pet standing still and the monster losing health. `PetMinion._launch_bolt()` now spawns the real
  (damaging) bolt on the owner's machine and sends `_rpc_show_bolt` (authority -> everyone else, unreliable) so every other player gets a visual-only copy that flies to the same target and bursts but deals
  nothing. Verified headless: a real bolt hits exactly once for its damage, a visual copy hits nothing, a target that dies mid-flight ends the bolt quietly, bolts clean themselves up ~1 s after impact; and a
  windowed render of two bolts in flight and an impact (screenshot reviewed). Not yet verified with two real clients.
- SKILLS WIRED INTO THE FORMULAS: `Data/skill_effects.json` (+ `Scripts/skill_effects.gd`, `CombatNode.skill_bonus()`) is a table "stat: skill: bonus per point", with a `_help` block. Players only —
  `CombatNode.skills` is set by `player3d.gd` (the same dictionary as `skill_levels`), so monsters, guards and pets get nothing and their balance is unchanged. Two stand-in names: `@weapon` (the wielded weapon's
  own skill) and `@category` (the skill the spell/ability belongs to). Shipped values (per skill point): weapon_mastery ATK +0.5 / melee damage +0.15% / crit +0.05; offense ATK +0.5 / damage +0.1%;
  @weapon damage +0.1%; dodge +0.15, parry +0.15, riposte +0.15, block +0.15, shield_defense +0.1 (block only counts with a shield); defense +0.05 to dodge/parry/block; spell_casting +0.1% and the spell's own school
  +0.2% to spell damage AND direct/over-time healing; a physical ability's own skill (backstab, bash, mantis_fist...) +0.2% damage; concentration +0.15 and channeling +0.05 to the keep-your-spell-when-hit chance;
  meditation +0.04 mana per regen tick; athletics +0.05 stamina/s and -0.15% sprint drain (never below half); bind_wound +0.5% bandage healing; double/triple_attack +0.15 to the Aetherfist multi-attack chances.
  Skills are capped at 4 per level so the effect is small early (level-10 skill of 40 = e.g. dodge +6, weapon mastery +20 ATK / +6% damage) and grows with play.
  Training added for skills that had no way to rise: offense and weapon_mastery on every melee hit, defense whenever a monster swings at you (hit or miss), double/triple_attack when the Aetherfist multi-attack
  fires, concentration/channeling when a concentration check succeeds, meditation when mana regenerates while sitting, athletics on jumps and every 10 s of sprinting, bind_wound when bandaging an ally.
  Related fixes found on the way: (1) swapping weapons never re-synced the weapon skill until the next login/skill-up — `_apply_equipment_from_inventory()` now does; (2) fighting with bare hands kept the old
  weapon's skill — unarmed now uses `hand_to_hand` (which is trained by unarmed hits); a weapon with no skill counts as 0 plus the class baseline.
  Verified with a level-10 character: baseline vs skills at 40 gives ATK 62 -> 102, crit 24 -> 26, dodge 1 -> 9, parry 6 -> 14, riposte 7 -> 13, concentration 7 -> 15, mana regen 5 -> 6, average melee hit 17 -> 18, a 50-damage
  evocation 29 -> 33; a monster-style CombatNode gets exactly 0; unarmed switches to hand_to_hand; 300 monster swings train defense 0 -> 26 at the level-10 cap of 40 and the derived dodge moves live; 150 rounds of real melee +
  Life Siphon + being attacked raised offense, defense, weapon mastery, necromancy and spell casting with no script errors.
  Skills with NO mechanic in the game to attach to yet (left alone on purpose, not invented): safe_fall (no fall damage exists), perception, tracking (the tracking window is not skill-driven), lockpicking, trapping,
  legerdermain, stealth (a stance exists but nothing rolls detection), dual_wield (players cannot equip an off-hand weapon yet), taunt/bash beyond their ability damage, archery/throwing (no player ranged attack
  uses them beyond the ability damage), divination/illusion/conjuration/enchantment/alteration/wild_magic beyond spell potency, fishing/forage/prospecting and the other tradeskills that already train from the crafting windows.
- TESTING LOG: `testing_log.txt` (project root) — what each test found and how it ended, in GENERAL and per-CLASS sections, with test sessions (players + characters + classes) at the top.

### Test 3 findings (2026-09-20) — open-world mob density, buff icons, weapon poison on the status window, /camp desktop
- OPEN-WORLD MOB DENSITY (`Scripts/mob_spawner3d.gd`, `Data/lumora_outskirts_spawns.json`). Reported: 30+ mobs in front of the graveyard/mausoleum, an over-abundance at the gate, and an empty world beyond them. Root cause:
  `max_active` was compared with the number of live mobs of that TYPE across the whole zone (`_active[mob_type]`), so all entries of a type shared one pool — the mausoleum's "10 skeletons / 10 mummies" plus the crypts
  and graveyard together approached 25-30, and adding a spawn point for a type added nothing (the goblin camps shared 10 goblins, four spider entries shared 10 spiders, four far rat entries shared 12...).
  Fixes: (1) `max_active` is now PER SPAWN ENTRY (`_active[entry index]`); (2) the data was retuned for that: gate ring ~18 (was ~24), mausoleum/graveyard/crypts 14 (was ~22-30), camps 4 goblins each,
  2 bandits per camp spot, spider den 10, and ~30 new small groups (1-2 mobs each, 1 phantom at two far points) along the roads: south past the graveyard toward the spider den, the west road to the signpost,
  north-west toward the bandit/goblin camps, the south-west, the wagon crash site and the far west — 62 entries, 115 mobs at full cap (was ~70). (3) `density` (top of the JSON, 1.0) multiplies every entry's
  cap (never below 1 for an entry that has any) — one knob for "too many / too few". (4) `activation_range` (400 m): a spawn point only makes mobs while some player is that close, because each mob costs the
  single-threaded server ~0.06 ms of animation + physics per frame (measured: 105 mobs ~6 ms of a 16.7 ms frame). Mobs already spawned stay; 0 = the old behaviour (everything from the start).
  Verified headless: all 62 entries sit on the navmesh (0 m off-mesh; note the navmesh is one flat plane, so this cannot tell about scenery — nudge a point in the JSON if one lands inside a rock or wall);
  a player at the gate settles at ~58 mobs (6-8 within 60 m of the gate, ~25 between 120 and 300 m), and teleporting to the west road brings its spawn points to life (total 70, 37 of them within 450 m).
  Not changed: spawn entries' `min_level`/`max_level` are still ignored (levels come from monsters.json per type), so distance does not make mobs tougher yet; goblin_warrior/goblin_scout/spiderling entries stay
  in the file but are skipped (no model). `Scripts/mob_spawner.gd` is the old 2D spawner (unused).
- BUFF ICONS: Warmth of the Campfire uses the red flame spell icon (`aoefire`), Kenji's Blessing the golden sunburst (`aoedivine`) — `ENVIRONMENTAL_EFFECT_SPELL_ICONS` in `buff_bar.gd`, placeholders until art exists.
- WEAPON POISON ON THE STATUS WINDOW (`buff_bar.gd`, `slot_button.gd`): the poison lives on the weapon item (`poison_bonus_damage`, added to weapon damage by `_apply_equipment_from_inventory`), not in `active_effects`, so nothing showed it.
  Each poisoned equipped weapon now gets a synthetic row "Basic Poison (Rusty Sword)" with the green skull icon, a tooltip ("+2 damage on every hit. It does not wear off."), shown as ∞, not cancellable; a poison on a
  non-main-hand weapon says it has no effect yet. `_apply_weapon_poison()` now also stores `poison_name`. Answer to "did it do anything": yes, +2 flat damage per hit (before armor), applied to the
  main-hand weapon only. Verified in a real window (screenshot): row and icon appear with the buffs, vanish when the poison is removed. Still open: it never wears off and a second application does not stack.
- `/camp desktop` (also `/camp desk`/`quit`/`exit`): the same 15-second camp countdown, then save and close the game; `/camp` (or `/camp menu`) still returns to the main menu; anything else prints the usage line.
  `/exit` is unchanged (same as `/camp desktop`); the help screen wrongly said /exit goes to the main menu and now lists both. Verified: the countdown runs and the process exits by itself after ~15 s.

### Weapon poison lasts 15 minutes; client auto-update system + one-command publish script (2026-09-20)
- WEAPON POISON DURATION: a poison coating now lasts 15 minutes of play, like a standard buff. `basic_poison` has `weapon_poison_duration` (900 s) in items.json; `_apply_weapon_poison()` stores `poison_remaining` on the weapon item
  (saved with it); `Player3D._tick_weapon_poison()` runs it down while the weapon is equipped and, at 0, removes the coating with "<Poison> wears off your <weapon>."; applying it again refreshes the time. The Status Effects row shows the
  countdown instead of the infinity sign. A coating from before this change (no timer) gets a full 15 minutes the first time it is seen. Verified: applied with a 3 s timer it counted down, damage went 8 -> 6 when it expired, log line shown.
- CLIENT AUTO-UPDATE. Players install one FULL build (the "base"); every later update is a small signed PATCH pack downloaded from the server's update address. Pieces:
  * `Scripts/patch_loader.gd` — NEW FIRST AUTOLOAD (project.godot). At startup, before any other game script loads, it swaps a downloaded `user://patches/pending.pck` in as `current.pck`, checks it against this build's base stamp and its sha256,
    and mounts it (`ProjectSettings.load_resource_pack(..., replace_files=true)`), so patches can replace scripts, scenes and data — including the other autoloads. A patch made for a different base is discarded. `--no-patch` starts the plain
    base build; deleting the `patches` folder in the game's user data folder does the same. It can never be updated by a patch itself (neither can project.godot, the executable or the native libraries).
  * `Scripts/game_updater.gd` (`GameUpdater`) — `check(url)` fetches `manifest.json` + `manifest.sig` over HTTP and verifies the RSA-SHA256 signature against `Data/update_public_key.json` (an update is code, so nothing unsigned is accepted; HTTP is fine
    because of the signature and the patch's sha256), then answers `available` / `up_to_date` / `full_build_required` (different game version or different base) / `error` (unreachable, bad signature, older than the installed build).
    `download()` streams the patch to `pending.pck.part` with progress + a stall watchdog, verifies size and sha256, then writes `pending.pck` and `pending.json` (json last, so a half download is never applied). `restart()` uses `OS.set_restart_on_exit`.
    `--update-from=URL` on the command line checks, downloads and stages an update headlessly, then exits (`main_menu.gd`) — for testing.
  * Join a Server screen: when a server's probe says "update needed", a "Download update" button appears; it checks the server's update address (`http://<address>:8911`, or `update_port` / `update_url` in `Data/servers.json`), refuses if the published
    update doesn't match the build the server actually runs, shows a progress bar, then restarts the game into the update.
  * `tools/make_update_key.sh` (one-time): creates `~/.config/aldenexia/update_signing.key` (private, back it up) and `Data/update_public_key.json` (public, goes in the game; stored as JSON so the export packs it). Already run: the key exists.
  * `tools/update.sh` — the publish script: `git commit` -> `tools/stamp_build.sh` -> `tools/update.sh` (or `--stamp` to include the stamping). It exports the dedicated server, exports a client PATCH against the base (`godot --export-patch ... --patches base.pck`), writes and
    signs `manifest.json`, and rsyncs everything to the VM (server binary under a temp name + rename so a running server isn't disturbed; patch, then manifest last; keeps the newest 3 patches), verifies the server binary's checksum and that the manifest is
    being served. `--new-base` makes the current build the new base (exports the FULL Windows client to Builds/Windows and remembers it in Builds/Base); options `--server-only`, `--client-only`, `--skip-export`, `--restart CMD`, `--host`, `--dir`, `--local DIR`.
    Guards: refuses a patch if the game version changed since the base; warns if project.godot changed (patches can't carry it) or the stamp is dirty/not HEAD; an export that "succeeds" without writing its output counts as a failure.
  * `tools/run_update_server.sh` — a 10-line python3 static HTTP server for `<dir>/updates` on TCP 8911; run it on the VM (own tmux window). It needs TCP 8911 open (and forwarded for players outside the LAN).
  Verified end to end with an exported Linux client: base client -> publish a patch (14 KB for a one-script change vs a 1.2 GB pack) -> `--update-from` downloads it -> next start swaps it in, the patched autoload script runs, the stamp changes, a second check says up to date,
  `--no-patch` gives the base build back. Failure cases all handled and reported: manifest edited after signing, signature from another key, corrupted patch (checksum), newer game version, different base, older-than-installed build, missing signature, server unreachable, patch
  missing on the server. The Join screen was driven in a real window (button appears on "update needed", progress bar, staging). The publish script was run against the real Windows preset and the real server export (into scratch folders), and against the VM
  (rsync + checksum verification + HTTP fetch of the manifest and patch over the LAN from a throwaway folder, since removed).
  Found on the way: (1) a lambda cannot report back through captured local variables (the first download "stalled" for exactly this reason) — the updater now uses a dictionary; (2) the editor rewrote `export_presets.cfg` at 18:36 (an export from the open editor
  saves its in-memory copy) and blanked the Windows `application/icon` again — restored; export from the editor's Project > Export dialog only after restarting the editor, or use `tools/update.sh`, which never rewrites the file.
  Limits to know: the first update needs everyone on a build that HAS this system (one more full download — use `--new-base`); a patch holds everything changed since the base, so it grows (the script warns at 300 MB); native libraries, the executable and
  project.godot changes always need a new base. (Windows only at first; both clients are covered — see the next entry.)

### Update script builds BOTH clients — Windows and Linux (2026-09-20)
- `tools/update.sh` now exports and publishes the Windows client (`Windows Desktop` preset -> Builds/Windows) AND the Linux client (`Linux` preset -> Builds/Linux) every time, next to the server: `--new-base` exports both full clients as new bases,
  a normal run exports a patch for each against its own base. `--platforms windows|linux|windows,linux` limits it. Each platform has its own base (`Builds/Base/<platform>/base.pck + base.json`) and its own patch (`patch-<stamp>-<platform>.pck`),
  because a patch is made against one specific base pack. The signed manifest carries one entry per platform (`platforms: {windows: {...}, linux: {...}}`, each with its own base/stamp/commit_count/patch file/size/sha256); the client picks its own by
  `OS.get_name()` (`GameUpdater.platform_key()`), and says "No update is published for your platform" if there is none. Per-platform entries are remembered in `Builds/Update/entry-<platform>.json`, so publishing one platform (e.g. `--platforms linux`)
  never drops the other one from the manifest. The server keeps the newest three patches per platform.
- COMPATIBILITY: the user had already run the first (Windows-only) version of the script — a real base 07f0e41 in `Builds/Base/` and a manifest without platform entries — and may have shared that Windows build. So the manifest format number stays 1 and
  its top-level base/stamp/commit_count/patch_* fields remain the WINDOWS entry (that is all those builds read); the new `platforms` dictionary is only an addition. The script moves an old `Builds/Base/base.*` into `Builds/Base/windows/` and turns the old
  manifest into the Windows entry on its next run. Verified against a copy of the real state: Linux base added, the Windows entry (07f0e41) survived intact, a following patch run made a 36 KB Windows patch against the ORIGINAL base pck and a 14 KB Linux
  patch, top-level fields still correct; an exported Linux client took its own entry end to end (check, download, restart, patched script ran, up to date) and a manifest without a Linux entry gave the "not published for your platform" message.
- STILL TO DO BY THE USER: the Linux client has no base yet (the first run of the old script was Windows only). `tools/update.sh --new-base --platforms linux` (or plain `--new-base`, which also re-bases Windows and would need the new Windows build re-shared)
  creates it from the current tree; Builds/Linux is then the build to run for testing.
- MISTAKE OF MINE (fixed): while testing I "restored" `Data/build_info.json` from an old backup twice, overwriting the stamp the user had made at 20:27 (build 07f0e41, commit #108), and re-exported `Builds/Server` with the stale stamp (2aff48e-dirty). Restored the stamp file to
  07f0e41/108 (matching the manifest that was published) and re-exported the server with it. Lesson: never restore a stamp file from an old copy; test with a scratch stamp and put back what was there when the test started.

### New NPCs: archery-range guard, dockhand, and the traveling merchant (2026-09-20)
- ARCHERY GUARD: "Guard Halric" (a normal `guard_npc.tscn`, stationary) stands at the archery range, at the shooting line by the "Archery Targets" marker, facing the targets (the marker is the firing position, the targets are ~70 m east). He has his own lines
  (`Data/guard_archery_flavor_text.json`: hail / engage / banter) and joins the other guards' banter and monster engaging automatically.
- DOCKHAND: "Dockhand Brek" (`Scripts/dock_npc.gd`, `Scenes/dock_npc.tscn`, half-orc male model) stands by the Dock marker. Hailing or right-clicking him gets a line, and WHICH line depends on where the traveling merchant is: coming down the road, at the dock, or
  already gone up to town (`Data/dock_npc_flavor_text.json`) — the world hints at the schedule, no UI. Built like Kenji (a VendorNPC that supplies open_interaction), so he never opens a shop.
- TRAVELING MERCHANT: "Bramble Tuckwell" (halfling male model). `Scripts/traveling_merchant_spawner.gd` (a node in the outskirts scene with a MultiplayerSpawner, same pattern as the mob spawner) brings him in at RANDOM times: first visit a random 2-8 real minutes after the server
  starts, then a random 20-50 minutes gap after each visit (all in `Data/traveling_merchant.json`, which also has walk speed, stay times, stand offsets, his lines and which model he uses). A visit (`Scripts/traveling_merchant.gd`): he appears at the "Traveling Merchant" marker, WALKS a
  navmesh path (3.5 m/s, ~7 min) to the "Dock" marker, trades there for 5 minutes, walks to the "Town Gate" marker (~6 min), trades there for 5 minutes (he stands 6 m off the marker so he is not on top of Sergeant Bryn), then says goodbye and is gone. All timing is real-world time
  (Time.get_ticks_msec). He only trades while standing still: on the road right-click/hail gets "not now, I'm walking", the shop window closes by itself when he stops trading or leaves, and 30 s before leaving he says he is packing up; while trading he calls out his wares every 1-2 minutes.
  He can't be killed and never respawns. Robustness: a progress watchdog repaths if he stalls for 20 s, and if a leg takes 2.5x its expected time he is simply placed at the stop, so the schedule can't hang. His stock is `traveling_merchant` in `Data/vendor_shop.json` — PLACEHOLDER
  (warlord scimitar, ghostly cloak, traveler's pack, basic poison, alchemy kit at double price) until the special rarer set exists; replace the `stock` list. He also shows up in the tracking window (he is an npc_vendor).
  Multiplayer: the server decides and walks him; the merchant is a spawner-created node so every peer gets him (and a player who joins mid-visit sees him where he is), with position, rotation, stage and animation state replicated (`Scenes/traveling_merchant.tscn`).
  Supporting: `Scripts/npc_race_model.gd` builds an NPC body from any player race model (`Player3D.CHARACTER_MODELS`, e.g. halfling_male) with the full animation set; `Player3D.open_shop_window()` split out of `_open_shop()`; `shop_window.gd` closes itself when its vendor is gone / stops trading.
  Verified: (1) single-player visit run end to end at 90 m/s (whole route in ~47 s): spawn, walk (animation "walk", no stalls across ~2,700 m), stop at the dock, trade (shop opened, 5 stock rows), walk, stop at the gate, warn, farewell, despawn; on the road he refused trading; the shop closed with him; the dockhand's line
  changed with the merchant's stage and went back to normal after; a second start_visit while he exists is refused. (2) dedicated server + separate client process: the client saw him appear, is a puppet of the server's node, watched every stage change with the right position and the walk/idle clip, joined mid-visit and saw him at the gate at once, and saw him removed.
  (3) Real-window screenshots of all three NPCs. Test data was removed afterwards and the config restored.
  Not verified: a real 3.5 m/s walk takes ~7 minutes each way, so only the sped-up run was watched end to end. Where a spawn coordinate lands relative to scenery can't be checked headlessly (the navmesh is one flat plane), so check the dock spot and the gate stand spot by eye.

### Sahren, Tobias Sandcrest, the ceramic token and the first dialogue pass (2026-09-20) — step 1 of the Hollowed/keyword plan
Context: the user shared the world map and the Djhanid lore (see memory project_world_lore); Sandcrest Landing = the Dock marker = the boat gateway of Lumora Outskirts (Lvl 1-3); the boat route goes north to Thallia's Bastion (Lvl 15-20), a later zone.

  Sahren = `human_male` (tanned, dark hair), Tobias = `half_elf_male` (brown-haired, reads as human). `Scripts/dock_npc.gd`/`Scenes/dock_npc.tscn`/`Data/dock_npc_flavor_text.json` (my earlier placeholder "Dockhand Brek") are gone, replaced by `Scripts/harbour_master.gd`,
  `Scenes/harbour_master.tscn`, `Data/harbour_master.json` (node "HarbourMaster" in the outskirts scene).

- TOBIAS is a shop (`sandcrest_harbour` in vendor_shop.json: sells the ferry token; the token does nothing yet). Right-click/hail opens the shop and he says a greeting (35-40% of the time a remark about Sahren that matches where Sahren is: coming, here, gone) followed by an honest
  line that the Bastion ship is a long way out. He also talks on his own: every 3-6 minutes an ambient line to anyone within 12 m, and at night a 20% chance it is a secret line ("singing on the water..."). Server decides, everyone hears (RPC), like the guards' banter.
- SAHREN's lines are the user's own text in `Data/traveling_merchant.json` (greeting on hail, ambient while trading, sales when the shop opens, secret lines with a 20% night chance, quest_hooks stored for the quest system). TOKEN REACTION: if you carry a Ceramic Water-Token when you hail him or open his shop
  he goes still ("Sahren goes very still, his eyes on the clay disc in your pack.") and asks "Where did you get that? Do you know what this is for?" — once per visit; the shop still opens.
  DRAFTS in his voice awaiting the user's approval: busy (on the road), warn, farewell, hail_leaving, the token emote; and for Tobias: ferry_away and the merchant_* remarks.

- Verified: item/shop data (ferry token = 500 copper), Tobias's shop and lines, the Sahren remarks by stage, ambient day/night (night forced with a fake day/night node), Sahren's sales line without a token and the surprise with one (once), busy/leaving lines, and the ambient RPC over a real
  dedicated server + client (client near Tobias heard 5 lines in 30 s at the sped-up interval). Config restored and test files removed afterwards.
- DECIDED but NOT built yet: the Sunmaddened wanderer as a RARE spawn in the outskirts, level 4-6 (their current stat block is a weak level-1: 35 HP / 5 dmg / AC 11 / 12 XP, so the level range alone would not make them tough — plan: level-4 base ~130 HP / 15 dmg / AC 15 / 45 XP),
  dropping the Ceramic Water-Token; the "Release the Hollowed" quest (3 tokens handed to Sahren, XP + coin); keyword conversations (EverQuest style, clickable words in chat); the data-driven quest piece.

### Keyword conversations + the quest piece + "Release the Hollowed" (2026-09-20) — steps 2 and 3 of the plan
- KEYWORD CONVERSATIONS (EverQuest style, `Scripts/npc_conversation.gd`). An NPC's JSON has a `topics` list (id, keywords, lines / sequence / category / dynamic, requires, then). What you SAY near an NPC (typed with /say or a clicked word) is heard by
  npc_talker NPCs within 9 m (`Player3D._npcs_hear()` from `send_say()`); the first topic whose keyword appears (whole words, punctuation ignored, multi-word phrases work) and whose `requires` hold answers, privately to you, its lines a couple of seconds apart.
  Words an NPC wants you to pick up are written {like this} in its lines and shown highlighted and CLICKABLE in the chat (`GameLogWindow._on_meta_clicked` says the word for you). `requires`: quest + quest_state, has_item / no_item, night. `then`: start_quest, open_shop, hail.
  New chat command `/quests` lists your quests and progress (also on the help screen).
- QUEST PIECE (`Scripts/quests.gd`, `Data/quests.json`, `Scripts/item_helper.gd`): static (no autoload — patches cannot add autoloads), state saved in the character's own save (`player_data["quests"]`, so it persists per character, on servers too). v1 objective type "hand_in": bring N of an item to the giver by dragging
  the items from the bags onto the NPC (the EverQuest-style Give window Kenji uses) — partial hand-ins are remembered. Rewards: xp, coin, items. Kenji's rat-tail quest could move onto it later.
- SAHREN's topics (`Data/traveling_merchant.json`): hail, release (offer / active / done), hollowed (before / during / after the quest), token (with / without one in your bags), water, djhanid, dangers, sahren (why he travels), wares (opens his shop). He only talks while standing still (on the road: the busy line).
  QUEST "Release the Hollowed" (`Data/quests.json`): he explains the Hollowed and the taboo; `release` starts the quest; bring him 3 Ceramic Water-Tokens (75 XP + 5 silver — a draft reward). Hand in before starting -> he refuses ("ask me first"); wrong item -> "not what I am looking for".
- TOBIAS's topics (`Data/harbour_master.json`): hail, merchant (a remark that matches where Sahren is), secrets (night only: the secret lines; by day "ask me after dark"), token (opens his shop), ferry (the route north + the honest "ship is a long way out"), waters, cargo, help (nothing to hand out yet).
  His and Sahren's greetings, ferry_away and ambient lines now carry {keyword} markers (your words unchanged except a few short hint clauses added to Sahren's greetings: "I am Sahren, of the {Djhanid}." and "Mind the {dangers} of these sands.").
- ALL topic lines other than the ones you wrote, the quest texts and the reward are DRAFTS awaiting your approval (marked in the JSON files).
- Verified in the zone (headless, real player): every Tobias topic (route/ticket/singing by day and night/Sahren/unknown word ignored/out of range ignored, greeting has clickable keywords, a click behaves like typing), the whole Sahren flow: topic lines, shop via keyword, the token-in-bags branch, refusing a hand-in before the quest, wrong
  item, quest start and /quests, partial then final hand-in (progress kept; the Give window's progress line; XP 2815 -> 2890, silver +5, tokens consumed), quest saved with the character, after-completion lines, the busy reply on the road.
  NOT built yet: the Sunmaddened wanderer rare spawn (level 4-6) and its token drop — until then the tokens cannot be found, so the quest can be accepted but not completed. Guard keywords (kenji, docks, graveyard, vendors, goblins, bandits, merchant, lumora...) are being brainstormed with the user first.
  Test-harness gotcha: a standalone --script that names a class like KenjiNPC compiles it before the autoloads exist and breaks that class for the whole run; reach such classes through load() at runtime instead.

### Guard keywords and the compass (2026-09-20)
- MAP: the user's whiteboard map of Lumora Outskirts (`/home/rwilkinson/Desktop/lumora outskirts.jpg`) fits the scene coordinates once NORTH = the +X axis and EAST = +Z (Lumora north, docks southwest, graveyard west of the gate, spiders far west by the sea, wagon crash on the west coast,
  signpost south, goblin camps southeast, traveling-merchant marker in the southeast corner, the archery targets/training dummies northeast, Dustwind Plateaus south past the zone line). Noted mismatch: the whiteboard puts the NPC vendor well south of the gate along a patrol line, the scene has the vendors ~40 m from it.
- GUARD KEYWORDS (`Data/guard_topics.json`, `guard_npc.gd`): guards join the keyword system (Oni does not: `_can_talk()`). Each guard's own `by_name` topics are checked first, then the shared ones. Shared keywords: hail, merchant (a remark matching where Sahren is: coming / at the docks / at the gate / gone / not in the world), lumora, kenji (also cat, rat tails),
  oni, vendors, docks (Sandcrest, harbour, ferry, Tobias), graveyard, goblins, bandits, archery, spiders, dustwalkers, djhanid, wanderers (hollowed, sunmaddened), campfire, signpost (road, south, Dustwind), danger. Directions are landmark/compass words from the map, no coordinates.
  Specialities: Corwin (gate duty), Reyna (graveyard, mausoleum, undead in her own words), Bryn (patrols, goblins, bandits with a level-appropriate warning), Halric (archery/hunting, docks and the ferry). Keyword markers were added to 9 of the guards' existing hail/banter lines (markup only, words unchanged) so players
  find the keywords by listening. All the topic lines are DRAFTS awaiting approval. Guards answer within 5 m (their hearing range).
- ONE NPC ANSWERS (`Player3D._npcs_hear`): the targeted NPC if it can answer, otherwise the nearest that has an answer — three guards standing together no longer all reply. Tobias and Sahren use the same rule (`can_answer`).
- COMPASS: item `compass` in items.json (brass compass, 5 silver, new icon `Assets/icons/items/compass.png` in the game's stone-frame style; Aldric the Provisioner sells it, the user can change that) and an in-game compass HUD (`Scripts/compass_hud.gd`, `Scenes/compass.tscn`, added to the HUD): a heading strip at the top of the
  screen (N/NE/E..., ticks every 5 degrees, the bearing underneath) showing the way the CHARACTER faces. Shown only while you carry a compass (bags or equipped); toggled with **N** or `/compass` (a raw key, not an InputMap action, so it can ship in a patch; also on the help screen), remembered in the settings. `Data/compass.json` says which way north is per zone
  (`lumora_outskirts3d`: +X; other zones default to Godot's -Z).
- COMPASS IS MOVABLE AND RESIZABLE like the other windows: drag it anywhere, drag its lower-right corner to resize (180x24 up to 1400x160; a faint grip shows on hover); the drawing scales with the height; position and size are saved per character (`ui_positions["compass"]`) and restored, `/resetui` puts it back.
  Verified: dragging and resizing moved/sized it and saved it, both size limits clamp, re-creating the HUD restored the exact saved rect, and a 700x70 compass rendered cleanly in a real window.
- Verified (headless + a real window): all guard topics above, Reyna's own graveyard answer, one guard answers / the targeted guard overrides the nearest, the merchant remark for each Sahren stage, Oni silent, Aldric stocks the compass; compass: HUD present, hidden without the item and "You have no compass.", visible with it, bearings +X=N 0, +Z=E 90, -X=S 180, -Z=W 270, toggle by /compass and by the N key, screenshot of the strip.
- Still not built: the Sunmaddened wanderer rare spawn (level 4-6, waiting on the user's stat review) and its token drop.

### Shop: right-click an item for its properties and a comparison (2026-09-20)
- Right-clicking a row in the shop window (For Sale OR Your Items) opens a read-only item popup (`Scripts/item_inspector.gd`): name, kind (Weapon - Primary - Slashing Weapons), damage / delay / ratio, armor class, stat bonuses, weight, value, WHO CAN USE IT (green "that includes you" or red "you cannot use this"),
  description and lore. For anything wearable or wieldable it also compares with what you have equipped in that slot(s) (rings/ears/wrists/trinkets: both slots): each stat as "6 -> 18 (+12)" in green when better / red when worse (for Delay lower is better), then a verdict — UPGRADE, WORSE or about the same; an item your
  class cannot use says so instead of calling it an upgrade; an empty slot says "this would be your first". Not-equipment items just show their properties. It replaces itself when you right-click another row, has a Close button, and Escape closes it (same layer name as the bag's inspect popup).
  Verified in the real shop with Sahren's stock: Warlord Scimitar vs a Rusty Sword (damage +12, ratio 0.21 -> 0.64, Strength +4, Agility +2, Dexterity -1: UPGRADE), Ghostly Cloak vs Cloth Cape, the pack/poison/kit (no comparison), a class-restricted Rusty Cleaver, and an item from the bags; screenshot reviewed.
  Two bugs found and fixed while building it: a plain Panel does not grow to fit its content (now a PanelContainer), and a popup replacing another in the same frame was renamed by Godot so lookups by name failed (the old one is renamed first). The bag's own inspect popup (slot_button.gd) has the same pattern and was left alone.
- Wanderer stats reviewed with the user (their update: level 4, 120 HP, 15 damage, AC 11, XP 12, no level range): still open — add level_min/level_max 4-6, raise XP (mummy/goblin warrior give 35-36) and AC (peers 12-14) — and the rare spawn + Ceramic Water-Token drop are not built yet.

### Ocean waves near the water (2026-09-20)
- `Assets/soundsforyou-ocean-sea-soft-waves-121349.mp3` (added by the user, 3 min) plays as an ambient loop on the "SFX" bus (so the options-menu effects volume applies) when the local player is near the sea: `Scripts/ambient_water_sound.gd`, node "AmbientOcean" in the outskirts scene.
  The distance is measured to the zone's "Water" mesh itself (the sea runs along the whole -Z edge of the zone, the west on the map; the docks reach out into it), so the docks, the coast road and the spider cliffs all work without listing places. Full volume within 45 m of the water (-5 dB), a gentle swell out to silence at 300 m,
  smooth fades (24 dB/s), it starts at a random point in the loop, and the player stops entirely when you are far away (no cost). Set to loop at runtime (the import setting is off). Not created on a dedicated server. All the numbers are exports on the node.
  Verified: gate/archery range/wagon crash/signpost silent and not playing; spider cliffs (147 m) -13.9 dB; 100 m off the shore -9 dB; the Dock marker -5.4 dB; out on the docks -5 dB; a teleport from shore to far faded (-10.5 -> -21.3 dB in 0.7 s) rather than cut. Not checked by ear: the loop point of the mp3 (a click at the 3-minute seam would be audible); tell me if there is one.

They only exist while a player is near (activation_range), which makes them rare in practice.

Loot is per player (each player rolls their own), so everyone in a group gets a token. Verified 40 of 40 loot rolls gave one. Three kills = the quest.
