### Troll walk animation was not "in place" — fixed, plus a tool to find the same problem elsewhere (2026-09-20)
- REPORT: the Troll Male took 3-4 steps and then teleported back. CAUSE: his `walk` clip (from `Troll Male Walking.fbx`) was downloaded from Mixamo without "In Place" ticked, so the hips travel 2.02 m forward over the 1.4 s cycle and snap back when it loops (his run/idle were fine).
  FIX: the user re-downloaded it with "In Place" ticked (first saved as `Walking.fbx`, then renamed to the standard `Troll Male Walking.fbx`, replacing the old one; the old file and its import are deleted, the .import kept so the uid is unchanged) (hips net travel 0.00, same 1.40 s length, identical skeleton track paths); I swapped it into `troll_male_animations.res` as `walk` (set to loop) — only that one clip changed (checked all 10 clips against a backup) — and checked a live troll: hips bob but end the cycle where they started.
 
- NEW TOOL `tools/check_root_motion.gd` (`godot --headless --path . --script tools/check_root_motion.gd`): scans every `*animations.res` under models/ and lists clips whose hips end more than 0.5 m from where they started (death/sit/jump are info only). Run it after adding or re-downloading animations.
  It found the SAME problem in 10 more libraries (re-download with "In Place" ticked and swap in): WALK — Dark Elf Female (1.42 m), Lizardkin Female (1.54 m), Desert Goblin Scout (1.10 m), Fallen Scout Skeleton (1.13 m; the graveyard skeleton mob); ATTACK (the same clip serves attack_horizontal and attack_downward, so the character lunges 1.1-2.1 m forward and snaps back) —
  Human Female (1.73), Elf Female (1.72), Elf Male (1.47), Troll Female (2.06), the old Desert Goblin (1.15), Reanimated Tomb Mummy (1.88).

### Test 4 findings (2026-09-20, build f799051) — the update function, crafting time, attacked while camping, rain indoors, the dock
- The client auto-update worked flawlessly for the user (updated client, connected). New NPCs (harbour master, traveling merchant) "work well".
- CRAFTING TAKES TIME PER ITEM (`Scripts/tradeskill_window.gd`): cooking 11 raw meat used to finish in one 4 s craft with a single skill roll, so no skill-up. It now makes ONE item at a time, each taking the recipe's full craft time (cooked meat 4 s, basic poison 4 s, so 11 = 44 s), the progress bar fills across the whole batch,
  each item is added to the bags and its ingredients consumed as it finishes ("You create ..." per item), and EACH ITEM ROLLS THE SKILL (`_tick_tradeskill()`), so a big batch is what levels cooking/alchemy. Applies to every station (it is the shared window). The action button reads "Stop" while crafting and stops after the item in progress is
  abandoned (ingredients not yet used stay staged); it also stops on a full inventory, and when you are attacked ("You are attacked and lose your concentration.").
- ATTACKED WHILE CAMPING / SITTING (`player3d.gd` `on_attacked()`, `monster3d.gd` `_resolve_attack_on`, `game_log_window.gd` `start_camp_channel`): every monster swing at a player (hit OR miss, local or relayed) now calls `on_attacked()`, which stamps `last_attacked_msec` and, if you are sitting, stands you up ("You are attacked and jump to your feet!").
  The /camp countdown aborts on it (before it only aborted on damage, so a missed swing did not) and stands you up. Also used by the crafting window above.
- RAIN NO LONGER FALLS THROUGH ROOFS (`Scripts/weather_manager.gd`): the drops now collide with the world. A `GPUParticlesCollisionHeightField3D` (48x60x48 m, 256 map, always updating) hides any drop that hits a roof, wall or the ground, so the town gate, tents and any future roofs stay dry; Kenji is dry under the gate roof.
  It took a lab to get right: as a CHILD of the moving rain node it hid no drops under a roof; moved by hand each frame it hid nearly ALL drops; a static one in the scene works, and so does `follow_camera_enabled = true` (the version used: it re-centres on the camera by itself, so no per-frame code). Verified in an isolated lab (roof slab, camera walked in from open ground: dry under the roof,
  rain still falling on both sides and above) and in the real zone at Kenji's spot (heavy rain in the open with the collider off, none under the roof with it on).
- The rain SOUND is muffled (9 dB quieter, `sheltered_volume_drop_db`) while something is overhead (`_is_under_cover()`, a short ray up, checked every 0.3 s): -15 dB under the gate roof, -6 dB in the open.
- DOCK (the user moved the Dock marker and Harbour Master onto the deck; the moves are in the scene now): the deck top is y=1.0 (a 1 m ledge at the shore, and the navmesh on it is a separate island), so three things were wrong and are fixed in code:
  (1) the Harbour Master stood at y=0, INSIDE the deck (vendors have no gravity): `VendorNPC.snap_to_floor()` (new, with `floor_y_at()`) puts him on whatever is under the spot the scene has him at (one ray, a physics frame after load) and makes that his respawn home; now y=1.0.
  (2) Sahren's navmesh path ended ~25 m short of his dock stop, so he would have stood at the shore until the leg timeout and then been teleported: `traveling_merchant.gd` now walks the navmesh to where it ends, then straight to the stop, HOPPING onto a ledge in the way (`HOP_HEIGHT` 1.4 m), and his stop's y is the real floor height.
  (3) leaving the dock, he first walks straight back to where he came ashore and only then paths to the town gate (the deck is off the navmesh). Verified in the real zone from the shore: 25 s walk + hop to the stop at (-757, 1.0, -997.5), then off the deck and on toward the gate (path of 23 points ending at the gate stop), no "could not reach his stop" warning.
  If the dock ever gets a ramp/steps and the navmesh is re-baked, none of this triggers (the normal path reaches the stop).

### Level resized in Blender: collision + navmesh regenerated, merchant spawn moved (2026-09-21)
- The user shrank the zone in Blender (old file kept as `zones/LumoraOutskirts-original.glb`, the resized one is now `LumoraOutskirts.glb`). Compared mesh by mesh: ONLY `Floor` and `Water` changed (floor x -1486..114 -> -951..114, 165 m south of the docks; Water y -4 -> -9, so the dock deck, y 1.0, is 10 m over the sea); the other 21 meshes are identical, so nothing else moved.
- The scene's baked collision and navmesh were still the old ones (raycasts hit an invisible floor out to x -1485, 530 m past the visible edge). Ran `./tools/regen_lumora_collision.sh` (23 shapes, no degenerate transforms) and re-baked the navmesh (476 vertices / 399 polygons, was 466 / 391). Now collision and navmesh end at the visible edge (x -940 solid, x -960 nothing).
  Backups of the scene and old navmesh were taken first. The Godot editor asks to reload the changed scene: choose Reload.
- NEW `tools/bake_lumora_navmesh.gd` (6 s, headless) and `regen_lumora_collision.sh` now runs it right after the collision step, so a Blender re-export is one command. (The first attempt "hung" for 280 s: a lambda cannot set a local bool, it captures by value; a dictionary is used now.)

Verified the whole first leg on the new navmesh: 1745 m to the dock deck in 126 s at 14 m/s (about 8 min at his real 3.5 m/s), hop onto the deck, then dock -> shore -> town gate as before.

Verified: every one of the 6 patrol legs ends exactly on its waypoint.

### Resized level rolled back (2026-09-21) — the flashing ground, and a stray offset on the NavigationRegion3D
- FLASHING GROUND while running: the resized `Floor` mesh had 28 triangles stacked at y=0 (the original: a clean 10), overlapping and disagreeing about the texture mapping over ~94% of the floor (37,421 of 40,000 sample points), on a double-sided material, plus stray vertices at x=114 with mismatched UVs: classic z-fighting. The rest of that level matched the original.
  (Aside: the whole 1600x2000 m floor is ONE 1024 px sand texture (UV 0..1), which is why the ground looks smeared; tiling the UVs every 10-20 m would fix that. Not done: the level is being rebuilt in Terrain3D.)
- The user is rebuilding the zone in Terrain3D (`Scenes/lumora_outskirts_t3d.tscn`, theirs, untouched) and asked to drop the resize. `zones/LumoraOutskirts.glb` is the committed original again (byte-identical to git HEAD, same .import/uid), the resized glb, `-original.glb` and their extracted texture copies are deleted (the resized glb is backed up in my scratch folder only). Collision regenerated and the navmesh re-baked from it with the new tools (466 vertices / 391 polygons, as before; floor collision ends at x -1486 again).
- STRAY OFFSET FOUND AND REMOVED: the zone scene's `NavigationRegion3D` had been given a transform of (1.28, +1.79, -0.09) by an editor save (the committed scene has none). It lifts the whole level and its collision 1.79 m while markers, NPCs and spawns stay at y=0 (players stood 1.79 m up, NPCs would be under the ground). Removed (identity again), navmesh re-baked. If the editor still has the old scene open, RELOAD it before saving or the offset comes back.
- Kept from the resize work (still valid on the original floor): Sahren's spawn marker at the SE box (-759, 0, 744), South Zone marker at the dock landing (-783.5, 0, -983.4), Sergeant Bryn's patrol without the unreachable Dock marker, the two wanderer spawns at (-880, -60) / (-870, 300), the navmesh-bake tool. Re-verified on the restored level: all 6 patrol legs reach their waypoints, Sahren's dock arrival + hop + return to the shore.

Guards now take hand-ins like Kenji and Sahren (`GuardNPC.receive_item_drop` / `try_give`; dragging an item onto a guard works, and the near-miss click forgiveness includes guards). Guard topics: `Data/guard_topics.json` (by_name for Reyna and Corwin; the order of topics decides which one answers).
- Verified end to end with a script (19 checks): note read -> quest start, wrong item refused, box accepted + exact XP/silver, box consumed, second box -> "already done" with no second reward, medal auto-starts, sigil before the chain refused with Corwin's reason, keyword -> quest start, partial silk 4/6 then a 9-silk hand-in taking only 2, chain step unlocked and completed for 250 XP, journal shows all four.

GATE RAIDS (`Scripts/gate_raid_manager.gd`, `Data/gate_raids.json`, node "GateRaidManager" in the zone scene; `/raid [bandits|goblins]` starts one for testing):
- Every 25-45 min (first one 8-15 min after start), only while a player is within 300 m of the gate: 3-4 bandits (60%) or goblins (40%) (+1 per 2 extra players near, max 6) appear ~110 m out on the SE road and MARCH on the gate (new `Monster.march_target`: a brisk walk to a point 12 m outside the gate, then they mill about; anything that sees a player still chases). Announcement in red, guards SHOUT (new `GuardNPC.shout_to_all`, heard within 70 m): Reyna warns, Corwin rallies, Bryn taunts as they arrive, "one down", "that's the last of them"; raiders shout back ("Open the gates!" / "Shinies!").
- Guards go out to meet raiders up to 32 m from their post (`GuardNPC.raid_alert_range`, leash 47 m) and wear them down but never below ~half health or a killing blow while a player is within 90 m (`raid_soften`), so the players get the kills, XP and loot; with nobody near, or after 2 minutes, they finish it (quietly, as always). Raiders not dead after 8 minutes are removed ("they've broken off").
- Verified in the real zone: 3-4 raiders spawn 110 m out and close ~6 m/s; guards on alert and softening with a player near; after 40 s all raiders alive at 20-50% health; player kills -> raid ends and guards stand down; nobody near -> guards clear a goblin raid alone in 24 s; timeout removes them; second raid refused while one runs; bad type refused.
- Not built: bandits/goblins that attack the gate itself, raid rewards beyond the kills, raids with the named mobs, scaling raids to the zone level.

### Test 5 findings (2026-09-21, build 24015c8) — GM mode, spell levels in the book, meditation, corpse cleanup, quest wording
- GAME MASTER MODE (`Scripts/gm_commands.gd`): `/raid` on the dedicated server said "only the host can start a raid" (the command only ran on the host machine). Now `/gm enable` / `/gm disable` turns Player3D.is_game_master on (saved in the character, replicated, shown as "<game master>" on the nameplate, which already existed); `/gm` alone shows the status.
  `/weather` and `/raid` need it (everywhere: host, single-player, server): a non-host GM's client sends the command to the SERVER through the zone's `GMRelay` node (`gm_relay.gd`: `_rpc_gm_command` / `_rpc_gm_reply`), which looks up the sender's player, checks THEIR flag, runs it and answers; the dedicated server logs every GM command with the sender and ok/denied. New GM commands are one `match` arm in `GMCommands.run` plus a line in the help (pause_menu.gd).
  For now anyone can type /gm enable (as asked); a real gate (a server-side list or password) is the follow-up. gm_commands.gd deliberately has no class_name: net.gd is an autoload that uses it and a brand-new class name is unknown to Godot until its class cache is rebuilt (that broke the whole Net autoload in my first run): it is preload()ed.
  Verified: single-player (refused without the flag, works with it, /gm disable), and against a REAL dedicated server + joined client: without the flag both the client and a forged RPC are refused (the server says so), with it /raid started 4 raiders visible on the client and /weather rain/clear worked and replicated.
- SPELL BOOK (K): every spell shows "Level N" (the class's own requirement, `SpellInfo.required_level`, the same rule the cast gate uses), green when you can cast it now, red with a dimmed name when your level is too low; the list is sorted lowest level first (same level: alphabetical); the tooltip adds "Requires level N (you are level M)". Checked with a level-4 Voidknight: 1, 4 green, 5, 8, 9, 11... red, ascending.
- MEDITATION (`player3d.gd`): sitting now trains it on every 6 s tick whether or not mana is full (it only trained while mana was missing, so a short sit at full mana never raised it; being attacked stands you up, so sitting is always out of combat), and it now matters: each point makes a mana tick 5% bigger while sitting (a quarter of that standing): skill 16 = +80% (21 -> 38 mana per tick at level 4). The old flat entry in skill_effects.json (0.04 per point, thrown away by integer rounding until skill 25) is removed. Verified: 400 forced sitting ticks at full mana raised it 0 -> 16; standing does not train it.
- CORPSES (`monster3d.gd`): a corpse now goes 2 seconds after EVERYONE who fought it (each has their own loot roll) has looted it (emptied it, or found nothing on it), or 5 minutes after death if it is ignored or has loot left in it. The server owns the timer; looters report in by RPC (`_rpc_looted`); a player who fought it and left the game does not hold it up; guard kills (no loot) still vanish at once; a body removed by the server also closes any loot window still open for it (that stayed on screen before). `corpse_loot_timeout` / `corpse_looted_linger` are variables.
  Verified offline (looted, empty, emptied by the player, timed out, ignored) and against a real dedicated server (a client killed and looted a rat: the server removed it 2.3 s later; the one it ignored stayed).
- WORDING: the quest and guard texts no longer tell you to "drag it onto her/him/me": the wagon-crash, medal, silent-den, weavemother summaries and the guards' lines, plus "Release the Hollowed" and Sahren's two token lines ("Place them in my hand"). Dragging an item onto an NPC still works; nothing tells you to.
- Test-data hygiene: my throwaway test characters (zz*) and the test server's Zzclient are deleted from the user-data folder; the real characters were never touched.

### HOTFIX after the Test 5 update: the server showed "offline" for every client (2026-09-21) — never change Net's RPC list
- SYMPTOM (user): after stamp + update the game server shows as offline and nobody can connect; the server log says "The rpc node checksum failed ... Node path: Net" and "RPC '_rpc_server_shutting_down' is not allowed on node /root/Net".
- CAUSE: my GM commands added two RPC methods to the Net autoload. Godot compares each node's RPC list between the two peers; Net's list on the updated server no longer matched the client still running the previous build, so EVERY Net RPC failed, including the version handshake. The client reads that as "offline" and the "Update needed / Download update" button (which appears only when the handshake answers) never shows: an old client could neither connect nor learn it must update.
- FIX: the GM RPCs moved off Net to a new zone-scene node `GMRelay` (`Scripts/gm_relay.gd`; group "gm_relay"); `net.gd` is byte-identical to the build in circulation (24015c8) again, and so are the other autoloads (checked: Global, GameLog, Inventory, PatchLoader). Re-verified GM mode against a real dedicated server (denied without the flag, `/raid` and `/weather` work, no checksum errors in the server log).
- RULE (game_flow.txt + notes): the RPC list of the Net autoload is frozen between released builds; add new RPCs to scene nodes. An old client then reaches the handshake, is told "Update needed", and updates.
- SAFETY NET for the same trap (`join_server_menu.gd`): when a server looks offline, the menu now also asks that server's update address (plain HTTP, no game connection) whether a different build is published, and if so shows "Can't reach the server, and a different build is published (...). Try updating." with the Download update button. Verified with a stub updater (offer / no offer when up to date / no offer when the update server is unreachable). Only builds that contain this change benefit.
- PUBLISH GUARD: `tools/net_rpc_signature.sh` prints the @rpc declarations of the autoloads (net, global, game_log, inventory, patch_loader); `tools/net_rpc_released.txt` is that list for the released build; `tools/update.sh` now refuses to publish when they differ (shows the diff; `--new-base` rewrites the file, `ALDENEXIA_ALLOW_RPC_CHANGE=1` skips the check). Verified: identical passes, a temporarily added @rpc on Net is caught. FIRST VERSION HAD A BUG (false alarm on the user's first run): update.sh runs with `set -o pipefail` and patch_loader.gd has no @rpc lines, so grep exited 1 and the comparison failed on an unchanged tree. Fixed (the script always exits 0; update.sh compares the two texts directly) and re-verified UNDER pipefail: unchanged tree = no alarm, an added @rpc on Net = alarm.
- TO GET BACK ONLINE: stamp + update again (the server gets the corrected net.gd); the clients you have will then reach the handshake, show "Update needed" and can download the patch.

### Test 7 findings (2026-09-21, build 597043a) — rain rebuilt (roofs + random wind), GM nameplate, guards return to post, monsters flee at low health
- RAIN "just a few patches in the sky" (`weather_manager.gd`): the GPU height-field collider I used to keep rain off the gate roof was thinning the rain EVERYWHERE: measured in open desert with no roof near, only ~60% of the streaks survived (score 3907 vs 6488 now), and the ones missing were the low ones, hence patches. Replaced entirely:
  a CPU roof map (`RainCover`, an inner class): for every 1.25 m cell around the player, vertical physics rays against the level's static collision find whether something solid at least 1.8 m above the lowest surface is overhead (roof, arch, wall, ledge; creatures are skipped) and how high. It is CACHED per world cell (the level does not move), a few hundred cells per frame at most, so it costs nothing after the first moments (1024 cells known in the first frames).
  The rain streak now uses a small shader that hides only the part of a streak that is BELOW that height at its position: rain falls freely everywhere else and from above onto the roof, and stops at it. Verified in the real zone at Kenji's spot: heavy rain in the open, none on the gate structure, none where Kenji and the player stand or on the ground under the overhang (and with the roof map switched off the rain goes straight through).
- RANDOM WIND: every time rain starts (naturally or with /weather rain) a wind is rolled: a random compass direction and a random slant of 7-29 degrees (horizontal / vertical speed 0.12-0.55). The server picks a seed and sends it with the rain start (`_rpc_set_raining(on, announce, wind_seed)`; late joiners get the current one), so every player sees the same weather. The streaks tilt along their fall (the shader turns them to the fall direction) and the rain box is placed upwind so the drops land around the player. 8 rains gave 8 different headings; a client on a real dedicated server received the server's seed and wind.
- GM NAMEPLATE: a game master's name shows as "<Zozuur>" in ORANGE (`TargetFrame.nameplate_name` / `nameplate_color`; Player3D refreshes its own label the moment /gm enable or disable runs, via a setter on is_game_master; other players' labels follow the replicated flag). It replaces the old " <game master>" suffix. (It did not change at all on your own screen before: your own label was never refreshed after load.)
- GUARDS WALK BACK TO THEIR POSTS (`guard_npc.gd`): a stationary guard more than 1.5 m from its post now walks back to it and turns to face the way it was posted (before, nothing returned it after a fight, so a raid left them scattered). Bryn (patrol) simply resumes his route. Verified: Reyna moved 9 m off her post walked back in 2.5 s and faced her post again.
- MONSTERS FLEE AT LOW HEALTH (`monster3d.gd`): at 10% health a monster that is not UNDEAD turns and runs straight away from whoever it was fighting for 7 seconds (1.5x speed), then comes back and fights on; it flees once per fight (no rubber-banding); "A Sand Brigand turns to flee!" appears in the combat log (relayed to every player). monsters.json "flees": false opts a type out (none do yet; named mobs flee too: say if you would rather they never do). Verified: a bandit at 9% ran from 2 m to 20 m in 3 s and returned at ~7 s, a skeleton at 5% and a bandit at 30% did not flee, and no second flight.
- "Level 1 Spirit" in the spell book: "Spirit" is the spell's SCHOOL (spell_school in player_spells.json: spirit / physical / psychic ...; it decides which resistance a target rolls against), shown as a small green badge that already existed; the new "Level N" badge sits next to it.

### Test 8 findings (2026-09-21, build 91f3ec5) — death at -30, targeting, guards at 8 m, Troubadour, songs stop on death
- DEATH (`player3d.gd`, `monster3d.gd`): a raid left a player at -165/164 with monsters still pounding the body. TWO causes: (1) monsters run on the SERVER, which never sees the player's `dying` flag (not replicated: only position, health, mana... are), so on a dedicated server they never stopped attacking a downed player; (2) nothing floored health.
  FIX: DEATH_HP = -30. At 0 HP you are downed (frozen, bleeding) and health DRAINS from 0 to -30 over BLEED_OUT_DURATION (now 10 s, was a flat 20 s); at -30 you die; a hit that reaches -30 or below (a raid's simultaneous hits) kills at once; health never goes below -30 (a real overshoot such as -13 is kept). An ally's heal that lifts you above 0 still gets you up.
  Monsters now treat a player as down when their REPLICATED health is 0 or less (`Monster._player_is_down`, used by target choice, visibility, aggro and the attack itself) and give up the fight when everyone nearby is down; before, the server's monsters ignored it.
  Verified on a real dedicated server: a level-1 client with 12 health as a bandit raid arrived was downed at -13, died about 6 s later, respawned at full health; the lowest health seen in the whole raid was -30. Offline: -165 -> -30, -10 bleeds out in 6.9 s, a monster leaves a 0-HP player alone.
- DEATH STOPS ALL CASTING: `_stop_all_casting()` on death clears the spell in progress and every playing song (a Troubadour's songs used to resume after respawning): the song must be played again. "Your song ends." in the combat log.
- TARGETING: being attacked now targets the attacker ONLY when you have no target (or your target is dead/gone): a second attacker never pulls you off your target. Verified: no target -> attacker; already targeting -> unchanged; target died -> next attacker.
- GUARDS ENGAGE AT 8 m (was 4 m stationary / 3 m patrolling): `ENGAGE_RANGE` and `PATROL_ENGAGE_RANGE` 8, `LEASH_RANGE` 6 -> 14 (it has to exceed the engage range). Verified: a rat 6.5 m from Reyna's post is engaged and killed, one 11+ m away is left alone.
- TROUBADOUR "very weak": Dissonant Chord's damage works: the data says 5 and a level-4 Troubadour's cast did 8 damage to a bandit (the spell scaling / skill bonuses) plus the "weakened" debuff. On the survivability: measured over 4 duels each at level 4 against bandits, a Troubadour took 1.76 damage/s vs a Voidknight's 0.75 (2.3x) with about the same damage output (7.7 vs 7.0/s). Buff (`combatnode.gd` set_class): AC bonus 0 -> 3, parry 1 -> 6, riposte 1 -> 3, which measured 0.98/s (1.3x a tank) at the same output. Tune those three numbers; a level-1 character vs the level 2-4 bandits of a raid is hard for every class.
- NOT DONE by design: the female human's jump animation (the user will replace it).

### Test 9 findings (2026-09-21, build e7cc914) — spell range, spell details on scrolls, XP bar, Shadowlight, cast_message variables
- SPELL RANGE (`player3d.gd`): the "range" field of player_spells.json ("15m"; "0m" = self only) is now enforced: a spell aimed at a target (enemy / cone / corpse / line, and a single ally for the non-Troubadour "group" heals and buffs) is refused when the target is farther than its range (+1 m for the target's body): "Your target is out of range for X (15 m)", checked again when the cast finishes ("Your target moved out of range"); mana is spent as before if it moves out mid-cast. A song that keeps playing (auto-recast) just waits until the target is back in reach, warning at most every 5 s. A song that never started because of range is not left "playing". Area songs and self spells are unaffected. Verified: a target 60 m away refused; 8 m cast (8 damage); a target moved to 70 m mid-cast took nothing.
- SPELL DETAILS (`item_inspector.gd`, `slot_button.gd`): a spell scroll (and so a song scroll) now shows what it teaches: the spell's name, the level YOUR class needs (green if you can cast it now, red if not), its description, and its numbers (mana, cast time, recast, range, target, damage), and "You already know this". Right-click in the vendor (unchanged place), and now also in the bag/inventory (the bag popup shared the same content through the new `ItemInspector.fill()` and became a PanelContainer that grows and stays on screen; it also gets the weapon/armor comparison the shop has).
- XP BAR (`character_sheet.gd`): moved from the Stats tab into the top strip under Water, on every tab, in the same style as the other bars ("XP  2844 / 4000").
- DEATH TEXT: "Death is upon you! Returning to your bind point......."
- SHADOWLIGHT now looks like a torch: a Voidknight's Shadowlight is a carried light with the torch's radius (9 m), brightness (2.2) and flicker, in violet, visible to every player through light_item_id ("spell:shadowlight"; a lit torch takes over the slot while it burns). The old small violet glow is retired. Verified: same radius, violet colour, goes out when the spell ends. (Only a numeric check: the test world was in daylight.)
- CAST MESSAGES: "cast_message" only ever showed for spells you cast on yourself, so the ones written for enemy targets (Dissonant Chord, Charm Melody) never appeared. It now also shows for a spell aimed at someone else (before the effect lines, in a soft cream), and supports variables: $targetname (the target: "a sand brigand", "Guard Reyna", "yourself"), $Targetname (capitalised, for the start of a sentence), $caster (your name), $spell (the spell's name). Verified with "The chord makes $targetname wince. $Targetname is not happy, says $caster ($spell)." -> "The chord makes a sand brigand wince. A sand brigand is not happy, says ...".
- HEALING AMOUNTS (answer, measured): a spell's "damage" field is its amount. effect_type "heal" = instant heal of that many points. "hot" (ballad_of_vigor) = the TOTAL healed over "duration" seconds, one tick a second (damage 0 gave only 8 = 1 a second; 24 gave 24; 40 gave 40; 80 gave 80). Both scale with spell potency (spell_casting and the school's skill). Troubadour group songs hit the caster and every ally within "aoe_radius" (default 8 m; the "range" field does not set that).

### Focus target, target-of-target, /assist, and the target's distance (2026-09-21, from the T9 design talk; verified headless and in a real window)
- FOCUS (`player3d.gd`, `mini_target_frame.gd`): a second, sticky FRIENDLY target, the Pantheon "defensive target". Set it with `/focus` (your current friendly target), `/focus <group member's name>`, `/focus self`, `/focus clear`, Shift-click a friend in the world, or Shift-click a group-frame row. An enemy cannot be a focus. It survives your own death and respawn (it clears only when that person is gone from the world). Client-side only.
  ROUTING (single-ally beneficial spells, i.e. every class's heals/buffs except the Troubadour's area songs, which are unchanged): an explicitly targeted friend always wins; otherwise the focus; otherwise you. Detrimental spells still need a hostile current target. So targeting the boss and casting a heal heals the tank you set as focus, and clicking another friend heals THEM, and Escape falls back to the tank. The spell range rule applies to the focus too.
  FOCUS FRAME: a small frame under the target frame ("FOCUS  Guard Reyna  31 m", green bar), the distance turning red beyond 15 m (most spells' reach); draggable, position remembered, /resetui restores it; click it to target the focus.
- TARGET'S TARGET: players, monsters and guards publish who they are fighting as a short key (`target_key`: "p:<peer id>" / "m:<monster name>" / "n:<npc name>"; replicated for players and monsters through their scenes' synchronizers; `TargetFrame.target_key_of / resolve_target_key / target_of`). A "TARGET'S TARGET" frame (right of the target frame) shows who your current target is targeting (target the boss: who is it hitting), and `/assist` targets what your current target is targeting (target the tank, /assist: his enemy). Verified: a chasing bandit publishes the player, Reyna's fight with a bandit -> /assist targets the bandit.
- OPTION (Options menu): "Attack Spells Follow My Friend's Target" (off by default): with a friend targeted, an attack spell goes to the enemy THEY are fighting (only if they have a living hostile target; otherwise the normal "can't target an ally" message). Verified off/on: refused vs 151 damage on Reyna's enemy.
- TARGET DISTANCE: the target frame shows how far the target is at the right end of its HP row ("7.4 m" under 10 m, then whole metres).
- Help page lists /focus and /assist. Not built: a hotkey for /assist (needs a key choice; a patch cannot add an InputMap action, so it would be a raw key).
- Note (again): a brand-new class_name used by an existing script breaks parsing until Godot rebuilds its class cache (dev/CLI runs, patches built from a stale cache): mini_target_frame.gd has no class_name and is preloaded by player3d.gd.

### Test 10 findings (2026-09-21, build 7620382) — encounter frame, F = assist, resizable frames, blade dance icon, hail facing, torch messages
- RESIZABLE FRAMES: the focus and target's-target frames I added last time could only be dragged; they now resize too (lower-right corner), and position AND size are remembered per character (the target frame already did both). Every frame added since follows the rule: draggable + resizable + remembered.
- ENCOUNTER FRAME (`Scripts/encounter_frame.gd`, next to the target frame; draggable by its title strip, resizable, remembered, /resetui restores it, hidden when nobody fights you): one row per enemy in combat with you or a group member (a monster's replicated `target_key` is you or a group member; plus your current target if it is fighting anyone), nearest first, up to 16 (scrolls): name coloured by con (red for bosses), health bar with numbers, distance; the enemy you have targeted is outlined in gold; click a row to target it. Verified in a real window with four bandits: all listed, the targeted one outlined, a click retargets, a dead one leaves the list.
- F = ASSIST (a raw key like N, so it ships in a patch; not while typing in the chat, not with Ctrl/Shift/Alt): target what your current target is targeting. Verified with a synthetic key press.
- BLADE DANCE ICON: new `Assets/icons/spells/aoephysical.png` (the crossed-swords art recoloured crimson, so it is not confused with the orange arcane-area icon that is the same picture) and assigned to the seven physical area abilities that had none (blade_dance, cleave, whirlwind, blade_storm, defiant_roar, fan_of_knives, blade_flurry) with a minimal edit of player_spells.json (your formatting untouched). tools/assign_spell_icons.py knows physical area spells now. STILL WITHOUT ICONS: 40 other physical abilities (31 single-target, 7 group, a few that inherit): there is no physical art for those shapes yet.
- NPCs FACE WHOEVER HAILS THEM: the "snapping back" was mine: last session's walk-back-to-post logic turned an idle guard straight back to his post facing (Halric faced the archery targets again the next frame). Guards now hold the facing for 10 s after a hail or a conversation, then turn back smoothly. Generic vendors (`VendorNPC.respond_to_hail` / `greet_player`) and Sahren now face the player too (Tobias, Kenji and the guards already did). Verified: Halric turned 68 degrees to the hailer, was still there 3 s later, and turned back after the hold.
- TORCH FLAVOUR: light_source in items.json takes "light_message" and "rain_snuff_message" (any future light can have its own); the torch says "You light a torch to keep the darkness at bay." and "The falling rain quickly snuffs the life out of your torch." Verified in the chat log.

### Dual wield only trains with two weapons (2026-09-21, T10)
- Casting Blade Dance (skill category dual_wield) trained the dual wield skill even with empty hands. `_tick_skill("dual_wield")` now does nothing unless a weapon is in the primary hand AND a weapon in the off hand (`Player3D.is_dual_wielding()`; a shield does not count). Verified: 600 attempts with nothing, one sword, or sword + shield left it at 5; sword + dagger raised it to 56. Casting Blade Dance itself is still allowed with any gear (say if it should require two weapons).

### Server maintenance countdown + /announce (2026-09-21, T10 ideas; verified with a real server + client and with a fake-ssh script test)
- WHAT PLAYERS SEE: a big red message in the middle of the screen ("An update approaches and the end draws near! Save your player and log out now!"; fades after ~9 s and is also written to the chat), reminders at 3 min / 1 min / 30 s / 10 s, new logins refused with the same words. The server saves everyone and exits through the ordinary graceful shutdown (`Net.begin_shutdown()`): the moment nobody is logged in (at once if nobody is), or when the minutes are up. All words: `Data/server_notices.json` (drafts; `{time}` is filled in, one line of each list is picked at random).
- `Scripts/server_notice.gd` (scene node `ServerNotice` in the zone next to `GMRelay`; its RPC is on a scene node because the Net autoload's RPC list is frozen) polls a request file every second (`Net.maintenance_file`, default `user://server_maintenance`, `--maintenance-file=`; contents = minutes, or `cancel`). A stale file is deleted at startup and a file older than 15 minutes is ignored (a leftover file shut a fresh server down in testing).
- GM commands: `/announce <text>` (a notice to everyone), `/maintenance [minutes|cancel]` (`GMCommands`; from a client they travel over `GMRelay`).
- FROM YOUR PC: `tools/server_maintenance.sh user@host [--minutes N] [--wait] [--restart CMD]` / `--cancel`, and `tools/update.sh --reboot-in N` = upload the patch, warn, wait (up to N minutes), and optionally restart. CAVEAT: the running server must already be on a build that has this feature; the first update after it was added still needs a manual restart of `./run_aldenexia.sh`.
- Startup log line on the server says an update countdown is available; a server that is counting down says so to anyone whose login it refuses ("shutting_down").

### Chat tabs with filters (2026-09-21, T10 idea; verified headless and in a real window)
- `Scripts/chat_tabs.gd` (new, no class_name; preloaded by `game_log_window.gd`) manages the chat window's tabs. Every message now has a category (`GameLog.classify(text)` from the line's colour/wording: say, tell, party, zone, npc = NPC speech, loot, xp, skill, quest, system; the combat log supplies combat = yours, combat_other = others within 10 m). `GameLog.log_general(text, category := "")` emits the new `message_categorized` signal (and still `general_message`).
- A tab is a FILTER: a message is copied into EVERY tab whose filter shows its category, so a Tell tab does not take tells out of General. A history of the last 1500 messages lets a tab be rebuilt when its filter changes (tick "Loot" on an existing tab and the loot from the last while appears).
- "+" beside the tabs adds a tab (starts with say/tell/party/zone). Right-click a tab: Rename (edited in place), Filters (tick any of General/system, Say, Tell, Party, Zone, NPC speech, Loot & coin, Experience & levels, Skill-ups, Quests, Combat: you, Combat: others nearby; "Show everything" / "Show nothing"; the menu stays open while you tick), Detach to its own window (draggable, resizable, Reattach button, remembered), Clear this tab, Close tab (your own tabs only). General (everything but combat) and Combat keep their old behaviour but their filters can be changed too; Combat keeps its own Detach button.
- A tab that is not in front and has unread messages flashes its name (`» Tells «` alternating with `Tells` every 0.6 s) until you click it.
- Tabs (names, filters, detached windows and their positions) are saved per character (`Global.player_data["chat_tabs"]`, `ui_positions["chat_tab_<id>"]`); the font-size menu applies to every tab. `/log` still writes every message to the file.
- Not built: filtering by a single channel's sender, colour per tab, and a "hide from General" switch (the request said messages must stay in the other windows).

### Pet: routes around the owner and other party members, safety net for falling off the world (2026-09-21, following up on T13)
- OWNER AS AN OBSTACLE: `_ray_blocked()`'s obstacle-avoidance raycast used to always exclude the owner, so it never routed AROUND them — fine for Follow/Guard, where the owner effectively IS the destination, but wrong while chasing an enemy (ATTACK/Assist), where the owner is neither the destination nor unlikely to be standing in the way (Assist puts the pet right where the owner is already fighting). `_move_toward()`/`_steer_around_obstacles()`/`_ray_blocked()` take a `dodge_owner` flag, on only for the ATTACK chase. Other party members were never exempted from this check, so they were already being routed around — verified directly (a second player character placed on the pet's line to its target: the pet reached attack range and started swinging either way).
- CROWDED MELEE, FOLLOW-UP: the pet no longer physically collides with players at all (owner or party) — a raycast-steered route around a body only works when there is room, and a genuinely crowded melee (the owner already at range on the same target) sometimes has none, leaving the pet permanently a step short and wedged. `PetMinion.setup()` sets its own `collision_mask` to a dedicated "solid world geometry only" bit (WORLD_ONLY_MASK, layer 7); its own `collision_layer` is untouched (still layer 1, so monsters and anything else that already detects a pet there are unaffected). `lumora_outskirts3d.tscn`'s `LumoraOutskirts_Collision` carries that bit alongside its original layer (`collision_layer = 65`) so the pet still lands on and is blocked by real terrain/walls exactly as before — verified: it falls through when the world body ISN'T tagged, lands correctly (matching the untouched baseline) when it is. ANY FUTURE ZONE'S terrain StaticBody3D needs the same `collision_layer = 65` or a pet there will fall through the floor; the fall-recovery safety net above catches that automatically rather than losing the pet, but it should still be tagged. The raycast-based visual steering (dodge_owner, above) is untouched and still routes around a player when there is room — this only removes the HARD physical block for when there is not. Verified: the two scenarios that were stuck before (the razor-thin owner overlap, and the "realistic crowded formation") both now reach attack range and land hits steadily; the 8-direction chase, the party-member case, the fall-recovery test and a full live-server fight to the boss's death all still pass with no regressions.
- FALL SAFETY NET: chasing a target's exact position (see the T13 flank-spot fix) can walk the pet off a genuine map edge/navmesh gap the same way a player could, and gravity had no floor to catch it — found live: a pet fell to y ≈ -55,000 over about a minute and was gone for good. After 4 seconds of continuous, uninterrupted falling, it now teleports back beside its owner instead. Verified by dropping a pet 500 m below the world and confirming the recovery.

### Skill mechanics audit (2026-09-21, asked: "check all of the skill mechanics, like dodge, parry, etc and make sure they're working")
- PARRY/RIPOSTE DOUBLE ROLL (`combatnode.gd resolve_attack()`): the riposte gate and the plain parry check each called `roll_parry()` separately — a fresh, independent random roll every time, so a defender effectively got a SECOND, unrelated shot at parrying whenever the first roll succeeded but riposte didn't proc, instead of that one roll deciding both. One roll is now shared by both checks. Confirmed with 20,000 simulated swings: PARRY/RIPOSTE rates now land exactly on the intended math (accounting for the flat miss chance, which is rolled first and never reaches parry at all).
- LEOPARD_STRIKE WAS A DEAD SKILL: Aetherfist starts with 5 points in it, but nothing in the game ever used or trained it — no spell, no code. `stunning_fist` (a stun, matching leopard_strike's own description) was tagged with the WRONG skill_category (`mantis_fist`, a different, non-stun Aetherfist skill that already has 5 other spells correctly using it). Retagged `stunning_fist` to `leopard_strike`. Verified over 150 casts: leopard_strike trains, mantis_fist correctly never does.
- DISARM_TRAPS DIDN'T EXIST: `disabling_trap` and `crippling_trap` (Shadowblade) were BOTH tagged skill_category `disarm_traps`, which is not a real skill anywhere in `Data/player_skills.json` — the actual skill is `trapping` ("detect and safely disable traps", matching their own description). Retagged both. Neither spell ever trained anything, nor gained any bonus from the skill, until now. Verified over 150 casts: trapping trained 20 times, cleanly, with no residual state from any other test.
- Block's own description claimed it also works with fists — `roll_block()` has always hard-required a shield; the description overclaimed. Reworded to match what it actually does.
- SKILLS BOOK NOW SHOWS THE LIVE EFFECT: every skill row shows what it is actually contributing right now, in the player's own numbers (e.g. "+3% dodge chance · +4% to its own abilities' damage/effect"), computed live from the exact same `Data/skill_effects.json` tables `combatnode.gd`'s `skill_bonus()` uses — it can never drift out of sync with the real math. Weapon-type skills also show their melee-damage bonus when that skill matches the currently equipped weapon. A skill with no numeric hook built yet shows nothing rather than a fabricated number.
- AUDITED AND CONFIRMED WORKING AS DESIGNED (no bug found): dodge/parry/block/riposte training on being attacked (`_tick_defense_skill`, hit-or-miss for defense, only the specific result for the other three); offense/weapon_mastery/weapon-type skills training only on a landed hit; weapon skill syncing from the equipped weapon (`_sync_weapon_skill`); double/triple attack (Aetherfist); concentration/channeling training on a successful concentration check while cast under fire; meditation's mana-regen multiplier while sitting; every magic school and every other ability-tagged skill (bash, taunt, hand_to_hand, archery, throwing, stealth, backstab, dual_wield, all 9 magic schools, ...) via the generic "@category" bonus every spell's own skill_category already feeds.
- KNOWN, NOT BUGS — feature not built yet, not broken: Wildspeaker's `forage` (5 starting points), Shadowblade's `lockpicking` (no lockable container exists yet), and `safe_fall` (there is no fall-damage system at all yet). Left alone — building any of these is new feature work, not a fix.

### Tracking now trains while its window is open and finding something (2026-09-21, asked: "make tracking level go up when the window is open and used")
- Tracking had no discrete action (a swing, a cast) to hang training off of, so it never went up at all beyond whatever it started at — flagged in the audit above as a known gap. `tracking_window.gd` now gives it the same "use it to raise it" skill-up roll everything else gets, every 6 seconds (matching `player3d.gd`'s own regen-tick cadence, so it doesn't level faster than anything else just because the window's own display refreshes twice a second) — but only while the window is actually showing something nearby, not while it's sitting open over an empty "Nothing within 50m." Verified: 14 s with nothing in range trains nothing; forcing 200 ticks with a monster in range produced 15 skill-ups, the same decreasing-chance curve every other skill uses; a real 60-second run with monsters in range came up with 0 ticks purely from bad luck (about a 1-in-5 chance over only 10 real ticks) — worth knowing if it ever looks slow to level in play, it is genuinely random, not stalled.

### Tobble Cogfarrow — a gnome tinkerer NPC (2026-09-21)
- New NPC in Lumora, standing with Aldric/the Apothecary's vendor cluster: `Scripts/tobble_npc.gd` (a `VendorNPC` subclass, same shape as `harbour_master.gd`), `Data/tobble.json` (greeting/ambient/secret/keyword-topic lines — all drafts), `Scenes/tobble_npc.tscn` (the `gnome_male` race model via `NPCRaceModel.build()`, already defined in `Player3D.CHARACTER_MODELS` but unused by any NPC until now; a shorter capsule/name-label than the human-sized NPCs). Right-click or hail opens his shop; he also talks on his own to anyone nearby (ambient chatter + a rare night "secret" line) and answers a few keywords (tinkering, gear, tick, compass, schematics) in `/say`, the same keyword-conversation system every other talking NPC uses.
- He does not teach Tinkering — there is still no tradeskill station or recipe list for it (`Data/player_skills.json`'s `tinkering` skill remains one of the "not built yet" ones from the skill audit) — and rather than leave that a silent gap, his own personality (meticulous, always refining, five failures before he trusts a design) is written as the in-fiction reason: everything on his bench is "nearly ready" except the one thing he'll actually sell today. New `Data/vendor_shop.json` entry `lumora_tinkering`: one item, a compass (a real, already-working "gadget"), with a comment marking it as a placeholder for whenever the Tinkering profession itself gets built.
- Verified: hailing him gives the greeting + shop-honesty line; saying "tinkering" nearby gets the in-fiction non-answer; his shop opens and sells the compass; the gnome model loads with a real body, not a placeholder capsule. No Net RPC changes (his own `say_to_all`/`_rpc_say` are on his own scene node, same pattern as every other talking NPC).

### Tinkering profession, first slice: a kit, an ingredient, a recipe (2026-09-21)
- Cooking and alchemy already showed the whole pattern — Tinkering needed no new engine code, only data, the same way the earlier two professions were added: a portable kit item (`basic_tinkering_kit`, `type: "tool"`, `tradeskill_station: "basic_tinkering_kit"`) that Tobble now also sells alongside his compass; a raw ingredient you loot (`scrap_metal`, added to the `humanoid` category loot table in `Data/solgrave_expanse_loot.json` — the file's name is legacy, it's the actual live loot table for every zone including Lumora — so it drops from bandits/goblins the same way leather_scrap already does); a recipe in `Data/tradeskill_recipes.json` (2 Scrap Metal -> 1 Tinkered Cog Trinket); and one line in `tradeskill_window.gd`'s `STATION_SKILLS` map (`"basic_tinkering_kit": "tinkering"`) so crafting trains the skill, exactly like the other two.
- First item: **Tinkered Cog Trinket** (`cog_trinket`, +1 Dexterity, `slot: "trinket"`) — the first item in the game for either trinket slot, which were both completely empty before this. New icons in the item family's existing framed style (built the same way as the pet icons and Taunt's — cloning a real icon's frame and replacing only the interior): `Assets/icons/items/scrap_metal.png` (bent metal, orange "material" frame, cloned from `copper-ore.png`), `cog_trinket.png` and `tinkering_kit.png` (a brass cog and a tool satchel, blue "gear" frame, cloned from `dagger.png`).
- FOUND AND FIXED ALONG THE WAY: no equipped item's own `stat_modifiers` (items.json — e.g. a ring's "+1 Dexterity") has ever actually applied to anything, for ANY item in the game, not just the new trinket — `stat_modifiers` was read only for the inventory tooltip's comparison text (`item_inspector.gd`), never folded into the player's real stats. `combatnode.gd` gets 7 new `gear_<stat>` fields (mirroring the existing `gear_atk`/`gear_ac`/... pattern exactly), added into the effective stat calculation rather than the raw base stat (so it can never get saved into the character or drift stale); `player3d.gd`'s `_apply_equipment_from_inventory()` (and the legacy-save equivalent, `apply_equipment()`) now sums every equipped item's `stat_modifiers` into those fields via a small shared helper, recomputed from scratch on every equip/unequip. This retroactively fixes every existing item that had a stat bonus and did nothing (Tribal Charm, Scavenged Ring, Coinhand's Signet Ring, Chieftain's Bone Charm, Weavemother's Silk Cloak, the plain Dagger's own +1 Dexterity, ...) — none of them worked before today. `charisma` is tracked for symmetry (`gear_charisma`) but still has no effective-stat use anywhere in the combat math, same as the base stat itself; nothing to plug it into yet.
- Verified: bought the kit from Tobble, crafted 15 trinkets across 15 rounds (consuming 2 Scrap Metal each), tinkering trained 3 times over that run (normal for the shared skill-up curve, not a special case); a dedicated test confirmed `gear_dexterity` tracks precisely through equip/stack/unequip (0 -> 2 with two +1 Dex items worn at once -> 1 -> 0) and that it feeds `dex_eff`-derived stats correctly; screenshots confirm the new icons render correctly in the backpack and the trinket shows equipped in the character sheet's Accessories row.
- No skill-level gate to start, by design (user: "I don't want anyone to have to train skills, they learn them by buying the kit and experimenting"): already true for every profession — `tradeskill_window.gd`'s crafting check has only ever been "do the staged ingredients match a known recipe," never a `skill_levels` threshold. Confirmed with the 15-craft test above: a fresh character at 0 Tinkering skill could craft from cast 1.

### Pet window: icon buttons (2026-09-21, asked by T13)
- The 8 command buttons (Attack, Assist, Back, Protect, Sit, Guard, Gear, Dismiss) are icons now, sized like the vendor list's item icons (28 px inside a 44 px tile) rather than the old full-width text buttons — full name + description is in the tooltip. Reused `Assets/icons/items/sword.png` (Attack), `shield.png` (Protect) and `trunk.png` (Gear); 5 new icons in the same framed-tile family (built from `dagger.png`'s own frame, recoloured): `Assets/icons/pet/assist.png` (crossed blades), `back.png` (a return arrow), `sit.png` (crescent moon + Zzz), `guard.png` (a banner on a post), `dismiss.png` (an X). The active persistent mode (Assist/Protect/Sit/Guard) still highlights its own tile, same as before. Verified: all 8 wired correctly (Attack engages a real target, Back stops it, each mode button sets the pet's command and highlights, Gear opens the gear window, Dismiss removes the pet) and a screenshot of the panel at its new, smaller size.

### Test 11 / Test 12 findings (2026-09-21, builds b57406f / 983fe73)
- DEATH ANIMATION TWICE + DOUBLE XP (found by: T11, goblins): on a joined client a spell that killed a monster ran `Monster.apply_damage()` on the client's puppet copy, which called `die()` locally — the death animation played once from that and again from the server's replicated state, and the kill's XP was granted twice (measured: 30 XP for a 15-XP goblin). `apply_damage()` now only runs the kill/ally-call on the authority (server / host); the puppet just nudges its local health. `die()` is also guarded so it runs once per monster (a second attacker, a damage tick or a guard/pet finishing swing on the body no longer re-runs it). Verified with a real server + client: death animation starts once, XP = the kill's worth.
- TAUNT DID NOTHING ONLINE: a non-host player's Taunt only changed the local puppet's threat table (threat lives on the server). New `Monster.apply_networked_taunt(peer_id)` RPC (on the monster scene, not on Net); the player's Taunt sends it. THE TAUNT CRASH (T11) — FOUND from the user's log (it ended in `Parameter "data.tree" is null` at get_tree()): `SlotButton._learn_from_scroll()` removed the scroll from the bag, the inventory refresh made the CHARACTER SHEET take the slot button out of the scene tree at once (the backpack only queue_free()s, which is why it worked there), and the function then called `get_tree()` on the detached button. Every spell scroll learned from a character-sheet bag slot crashed, not just Taunt. It now gets the tree from the player before touching the inventory. Verified by learning a bought scroll from the character sheet's bag.
- OWNED-SPELL INDICATOR (asked by: T11): a spell scroll whose spell you already know shows a green tick badge (top-right of the slot) in the backpack/inventory, and "✔ known" with the name greyed in the vendor's For Sale list (`ItemInspector.teaches_known_spell()`); the item popup already said "You already know this." Skills are not sold as scrolls (only spells are), so there is nothing to mark for them.
- GM COMMANDS ON THE HELP PAGE (asked): Escape -> Controls & Commands shows a "Game Master Commands" section (/gm, /weather, /raid, /announce, /maintenance) only while you are a game master; they are no longer listed for everyone.
- MOUSE LOOK vs UI (found by: T12): right-clicking an item, a chat tab or a window title started the camera's head-turn (hiding the cursor), and the wheel zoomed the camera over panels. `CameraController._pointer_over_ui()` (Viewport.gui_get_hovered_control) now lets a right-click that lands on a control belong to that control, and the wheel over any window/panel no longer zooms. Over the bare world nothing changed (verified: world points turn/zoom; chat window, action bar do not). Mouselook (F12) is unaffected.
- ITEM VANISHED WHEN DRAGGED FROM THE CHARACTER SHEET INTO THE BACKPACK (found by: T12 play, a dagger): an EMPTY backpack tile belongs to no bag (`bag_slot = -1`), and `Inventory._move_basic_to_bag()` filed the item under bag "-1" — a bag that does not exist, so no window showed it (its data sat in `bag_contents["-1"]`). It also ignored bag capacity and the "only an empty bag goes inside a bag" rule. Now the item goes into the bag it was dropped on if that has room, otherwise the first bag with room (stackables join an existing stack); if nothing fits, or a non-empty bag is dragged into a bag, it stays where it was with a chat line. RECOVERY: `Inventory._rescue_orphaned_items()` runs at every inventory load and moves anything stranded under a nonexistent bag into a bag with room (else a free character-sheet slot), with "You find X at the bottom of your pack." — so the lost dagger returns at the next login on the updated build. Verified (8 checks: empty tile, filled tile, full bag, bag-in-bag, recovery).
### Pet: dropped out of attacking mid-fight (found by T13, fighting Sergeant Halvek)
- CAUSE (`pet_minion.gd`): when out of melee range, the pet used to walk toward a "flank spot" computed from the target's current FACING (so it would end up on the far side, away from block/parry/dodge/riposte). Against an enemy that TURNS — most do, since they turn to face and chase whoever they're fighting — that spot orbits the enemy in a circle every frame it's recomputed. The pet chased the circling point instead of the enemy itself and could go the rest of a fight without ever landing within its own attack range: it sat still in Attack mode, never swinging, until the target's facing happened to settle or you re-clicked Attack/Assist. The pet now walks straight at the target (same pattern `guard_npc.gd`'s chase already uses); the flanking idea is dropped rather than patched further, since a moving/turning target makes any fixed offset from it inherently unstable to chase. Reproduced headlessly with a real dedicated server and a real fighting boss: before the fix the pet's swings stopped for the rest of a 45+ second fight while the player's own damage kept landing; after the fix a full fight to the boss's death (43 s, Sergeant Halvek's 300 HP) completed with the pet swinging the whole time. Also checked from 8 approach directions around a stationary target: all reach range and land repeated hits.
- SECOND BUG FOUND ALONGSIDE IT: a saved pet stance (Assist/Guard/Sit) was not restored at login — `Global.player_data.get("pet_mode", 0)` comes back from the JSON save as a float (`4.0`), and GDScript's `match` never matches a float against an int case, so every login silently reset the pet to Follow regardless of what was saved. `_apply_saved_pet_mode()` now reads it as `int(...)`. Verified: a character saved with Assist now logs back in still in Assist (was: silently Follow).
- GAME MASTER PASSWORD (asked): until now anyone could type `/gm enable`, and the server believed the "I am a GM" flag the client reported (so a modified client could fake it). Now on a DEDICATED server a player must `/gm enable <password>`; the server keeps its own list of logged-in game masters (`GMRelay._authorized`, per connection) and every GM command is checked against THAT list, never the client's flag. The password is the first non-empty, non-# line of `<server user folder>/gm_password` (`Net.gm_password_file`, `--gm-password-file=`); it is read at every attempt, so changing it needs no restart; no file = nobody can become a GM (the server log says so at startup). Five wrong passwords lock that connection out until it reconnects. Being a GM lasts for the session: it is not saved and a saved flag is ignored at login on a server (the orange nameplate tag is only cosmetic). Single-player / host: no password, `/gm enable` works as before. Server log lines: "GM login: Name (peer n)", "GM login FAILED ... 2 of 5", "GM command /announce ... ok|denied" (the password is never logged). `tools/set_gm_password.sh user@host` sets it (asks twice; `--generate`, `--show`, `--remove`); the file is mode 600. Verified with a real server + client: no file, wrong password x5 + lockout, faked flag denied, right password works, `/gm disable`, password changed while running, not remembered.
- EVERY MONSTER CAN SPAWN (asked): the spawner's hand-kept whitelist (`VALID_MOB_TYPES`, 20 names) is gone; a spawn entry is valid when `Data/monsters.json` defines it (`MobSpawner3D._valid_mob_types`). That switched on the three Lumora spawn entries that never spawned (goblin_warrior, goblin_scout, spiderling) and makes every future zone table work with no second list. Twelve monsters had no model at all (bonegnawer, dustwalker + scout/ravager/warlord, khepri_beetle, lumora_guard, lumora_outrider, sand_stinger, sandshifter, thornback_lizard, wild_dog): each now borrows an existing model with `model_from` / `model_scale` / `tint` in monsters.json (stand-ins, same as the named mobs: bandit for the humanoids, dune_scarab / spider / slime / snake / rat for the rest). Verified: all 34 types spawn with a model (mirage_phantom is the one that has always had none — it is a special case); Lumora's three entries produce mobs. The other zones' spawn files are still planning sheets with "TBD" coordinates.
- TAUNT ICON: new `Assets/icons/spells/taunt.png` (a bullhorn shouting on the red physical tile); `tools/assign_spell_icons.py` maps effect_type "taunt" to it; four upgrade spells (master_block, enhanced_block, improved_cleave, improved_fan_of_knives) picked up their base spell's icon in the same run.
- ACTION BAR ARRANGING (asked): slots can be picked up. Drag a slot onto an empty slot to MOVE it, onto a filled slot to SWAP the two, off the bar (let go anywhere that is not a slot) to REMOVE it; everything is saved with the character. Abilities Book -> slot still works. A slot now fires on mouse-RELEASE (it used to fire on press, which would have cast the spell every time you started a drag). Verified in a real window with synthetic drags (move, swap, remove, press does not cast, release casts).
- WORKS (T12): /maintenance 5 (banner, countdown, server stopped at the end), /announce, new chat windows and filters. (T11): encounter frame, /focus frame.

### Not built yet
- Nothing on the original server list. Possible later: per-server certificates in servers.json, an in-game admin/console command channel,
  server-side damage re-verification, loot rolled server-side (see "Still-open netcode gaps").

### Crafting system — all 12 tradeskills, gathering nodes, town stations, recipe learning (2026-09-22)
- DESIGN SOURCE: `~/NCT/Aldenexia-Lightfall/Crafting.xlsx` is now the crafting documentation and the single source of truth (Rules tab = the mechanics). New `tools/export_crafting.py` turns it into three generated files — never hand-edit them, edit the workbook and re-run: `Data/tradeskill_recipes.json` (92 recipes keyed by recipe_id, replacing the old per-station proof-of-concept lists), `Data/gathering_nodes.json` (10 node types) and `Data/crafting_items.json` (188 items: materials, crafted gear/food/potions, 7 new kits, vendor tools and ammo, 65 "Recipe: X" scrolls). `inventory_autoload.gd` merges crafting_items.json into the item database; a hand-made items.json entry with the same id always wins (raw_meat, cooked_meat, basic_poison, cog_trinket, spider_silk, ... are untouched). The exporter prints every "Effect / Stats" phrase the engine can't do yet.
- CRAFTING RULES (`tradeskill_window.gd`): recipes are looked up by the station's id (each recipe lists every station that accepts it — town stations take their kit's recipes, the Oven also takes campfire recipes). Success = base + 1% per skill point above the recipe's min skill, max 98%; a success can crit for the crit yield; a failure uses up the ingredients. Skill-ups stop at the recipe's max level (min + 15); a failed craft trains at 25% of the normal chance (`player3d.gd _tick_skill()` takes a gain multiplier now). Recipes must be known: innate ones always are, the rest are learned from recipe scrolls (Learn button, `slot_button.gd`) into `known_recipes` (saved with the character). The window says why a matched recipe can't be made (not learned / skill too low). Ingredient groups: `cooked_meat_any` matches any one cooked meat. Also fixed: an ingredient split across two slots was consumed twice per craft.
- GATHERING (`gathering_node.gd`, new): forage patches, ore veins and trees, right-click in range. Min skill gate, success/crit/bonus-drop rolls, gather time (moving or being attacked interrupts), respawn timer; mining needs a Miner's Pick and woodcutting a Woodcutter's Axe in your bags (vendor), Tinkering's upgrades are faster with a better crit chance. Failures never train and leave the node up. Nodes are built locally on each client from the same seeded positions — each player has their own copy, no networking.
- WORLD PLACEMENT (`crafting_world_spawner.gd` + `crafting_station.gd`, new; node "CraftingWorld" in lumora_outskirts3d.tscn): reads the hand-edited `Data/crafting_placements.json` — 10 town stations in a row in front of the vendor stalls, 82 gathering nodes scattered by area (thistle/tin/fir around the starting dunes, harder nodes further out, Moonpetal and the Glittering Veins by the Mausoleum, palms on the south coast). Placeholder shapes with name labels until the Meshy models exist. FIRST-PASS POSITIONS — check them in game.
- CONSUMABLES: `player3d.gd apply_consumable_effects()` — crafted food/drink/potions can heal instantly, apply a timed buff (regen, heal-over-time, damage_mult, stat_<name>) and cure poisons; new "potion" item type with a Drink button (`use_potion()`). `combatnode.gd`: timed effects can now raise stats with `stat_<name>` modifiers (they feed the effective stats like gear_<stat> does, and invalidate the stat cache when applied/removed/expired).
- DATA: vendors — Aldric sells the tools, supplies, vendor quivers, 6 kits and the Blacksmithing/Woodworking/Fletching/Jewelcrafting/Leatherworking/Tailoring scrolls; the Apothecary the alchemy/brewing kits, vials and Alchemy/Brewing/Cooking scrolls; Tobble the Tinkering scrolls. Loot — species meats (rats drop Raw Rat Meat instead of generic Raw Meat), Raw Hide (rats, snakes), Fletching Feathers (bandits, goblin scouts), Linen Cloth (humanoid category), Uncut Gems (the five named elites); new Lumora tables for spiderlings, goblin scouts and goblin warriors (they had none).
- NOT BUILT YET (marked "Needs engine"/"Deferred" in the workbook): player-lit campfires (Firewood Bundles), ammo-slot damage from quivers, throwables (grenades, explosives), lockpicking, permanent lanterns, quality rolls, fermentation, misfires, % mana regen / spell power / resists / movement speed on gear, damage reflection.
- Verified headless (throwaway copy of a real save): all crafting items/vendor stock/new loot items resolve; 10 stations + 82 nodes placed; gathering (tool check, skill gate, success, node used up), campfire batch of 20 with skill-ups, kit-only recipe refused at a campfire, unlearned and under-skilled recipes refused with the right message, group ingredient, Oven taking a campfire recipe, trivial recipe gives no skill-ups, bronze alloy at the Forge, food satiety + buff, elixir +2 CON (+20 max HP, gone when it ends), healing potion.

### Item data fixes: stat names, missing loot definitions (2026-09-22, asked: "update the existing items with stats, so their stat bonuses work, and fix the items that have no item definition in the loot tables")
- STAT NAMES: 11 items in items.json used `agility` or `stamina` in stat_modifiers, which the engine has never had (combatnode.gd only has strength/constitution/dexterity/intelligence/wisdom/charisma/luck), so those bonuses silently did nothing. Renamed agility -> dexterity and stamina -> constitution: Bone Shield, Cloth Slippers, Scavenged Ring, Warlord Scimitar, Warden's Grave Blade, Venomfang Dagger (+3 Dex +1 Agi -> +4 Dex), Bandit Captain's Vest, Chieftain's Bone Charm, Goblin-Hide Boots, Tarnished Warden Helm, Weavemother's Silk Cloak (+2 Agi +1 Dex -> +3 Dex). Ghostly Cloak's "+2 stealth" was a SKILL bonus (gear can't raise skills yet), so it did nothing — changed to +2 Dexterity at the user's request.
- MISSING LOOT DEFINITIONS: 36 items in solgrave_expanse_loot.json had no items.json entry, so they could never drop (all from Dustwind Plateaus, Ashfall Dunes and the Lumora Outrider / Dustwalker Warlord encounters — none from Lumora Outskirts). Added all 36 with existing icons: 28 trophies/reagents/materials (scorpion, wild dog, thornback lizard, sandshifter, khepri beetle parts; light/heavy leather; maps, keys, insignia, trinkets) and 8 pieces of gear — Worn Sandals (feet AC 1), Sharp Dagger (6/20, +1 Dex), Ravager Earring (+1 Str), Notched Blade (8/28), Combat Wraps (hand-to-hand 6/18, +1 Str), Standard-Issue Spear (two-handed 11/34), Royal Sandals (feet AC 2, +1 Dex +1 Cha); Jeweled Hilt and Conqueror's Map are valuable sell items. Descriptions and stats are drafts to adjust.
- Verified headless: equipping Goblin-Hide Boots / Bandit Captain's Vest / Venomfang Dagger now raises gear dexterity / constitution; every item in every loot table now creates a real item.

### Terrain3D rebuild of Lumora: the clone scene and flat-floor fixes (2026-09-22)
- NEW SCENE `Scenes/lumora_outskirts_terrain.tscn`: a copy of lumora_outskirts3d.tscn (own scene uid) for rebuilding the zone on Terrain3D. Changes from the original: an empty Terrain3D under NavigationRegion3D (data in res://zones/lumora_terrain, collision_layer 65 so pets stand on it — see pet_minion.gd WORLD_ONLY_MASK), the Torches moved out from under the old LumoraOutskirts_Collision to the scene root (same positions) so the old collision can be deleted without taking them along, and its own navmesh file (Data/lumora_outskirts_terrain_navmesh.tres) so baking it never touches the live zone's. The old Blender import (LumoraOutskirts + LumoraOutskirts_Collision) is still in it as a placement guide — delete both once the terrain and new buildings are in.
- Terrain3D gotcha: a hand-written Terrain3D node's collision_layer is dropped on load unless its material/assets sub-resources are saved with it (the collision object doesn't exist yet) — the node was generated by Godot and spliced in, and loads with layer 65.
- START ZONE IN ONE PLACE: `Global.START_ZONE_PATH` (global.gd) is now what character creation, load game and multiplayer (Net.pending_zone_path) load — switching the game to the rebuild is that one line. The rebuild also gets the Lumora music.
- NO MORE FLAT-FLOOR ASSUMPTIONS: gathering nodes accept Terrain3D ground (crafting_world_spawner.gd); vendors that find no ground within 3 m of where they were placed look from high above (vendor_npc.gd floor_y_at); a saved login position with no ground under it (inside a new hill) is lifted onto the ground (player3d.gd _lift_above_ground). Mob spawns already snap to the navmesh.
- `tools/bake_lumora_navmesh.gd` takes a scene (`-- terrain` for the rebuild), includes every Terrain3D under the NavigationRegion3D the same way Terrain3D's editor baker does, and saves to whichever navmesh file that scene uses.
- Verified headless: the rebuild loads with a character (Terrain3D on layer 65, torches in place, 82 gathering nodes, 10 stations, NPCs); its navmesh bakes without touching the live one; the full crafting test still passes on the original zone.

### Terrain3D rebuild: playable test pass (2026-09-22)
- The vendors, mobs, stations and gathering nodes were never deleted — they are all built at runtime, so the editor shows none of them. What actually broke them on terrain: Terrain3D's collision_mode was DYNAMIC_GAME (1), which only builds collision within 64 m of the camera, so far mobs fell through, the node spawner found no ground, and the camera-less dedicated server would have had no terrain collision at all. Now FULL_GAME (3).
- Filled 20 empty terrain regions (the 14 squares over spawns / nodes / waypoints — including the start area west of the gate and the player spawn point — plus a row of seabed under the water and dock); the 46 sculpted regions are untouched. 66 regions.
- New town (the user's gate model: west wall at x~58 with the gatehouse at z 2-23, north/south walls to x~111, open to the east; two boxed areas in its west corners): vendors along the north wall of the north-west box (Aldric, Lira, Tobble, Ilsabet; Xalvyr on its west wall), the 10 crafting stations in two rows in the south-west box (Data/crafting_placements.json — shared with the flat zone, where those spots are inside the old town too), Kenji and Oni's patrol in the open town, Town Gate marker just outside the new gate, new-character spawn at (20, 13). The town + gate front are keep_clear for gathering nodes; the rat and dune scarab spawns that fell inside the new walls moved to (150, 45) and (20, -60).
- Added Scripts/structure_collision.gd as a StructureCollision node: every model at the scene root gets trimesh collision on layer 65 at load (gates, graveyard, dock, both tower spikes; water skipped).
- Re-added a NavigationRegion3D (it had been deleted with the old model — no navmesh at all). The first whole-terrain bake crashed Godot (about 1.8 x 2.6 km at 0.5 m cells); the terrain navmesh now bakes only the playable box (x -940..270, z -1110..810) at 1 m cells: 7,176 polygons in 11 s. Bake tool uses the whole terrain (require_nav false) until navigation is painted.
- Verified headless: terrain collision mode/layer, player spawns on the ground, every NPC on the ground with its model, gate/wall collision (gatehouse top at 5 m), far-away ground solid, 10 stations in the crafting box, 82/82 gathering nodes on the ground and none in town, paths from the spawn into town and to the goblin camps, 60 mobs spawned and none fell through; the flat zone's crafting test still passes. KNOWN: the dock deck is 2 m above the sand, beyond a walker's 0.5 m step — the navmesh stops 27 m short and the traveling merchant would have to hop 2 m onto it.

### Terrain3D rebuild: stalls, tents, tower-spike arch (2026-09-22)
- VENDORS UNDER THE LEAN-TOS (user's 4 "vendor lean-to" stalls): Aldric, Lira and Ilsabet under the three along the vendor box's north wall, Tobble under the one in the crafting box (next to the stations he'd supply), each standing just inside the stall facing out of its open side (the model's local +z); Xalvyr stays on his own against the west wall. vendor_npc.gd floor_y_at now casts from 1.5 m above (was 3 m) so a vendor under a 2.3 m lean-to roof can never snap up onto it — still high enough to find a 1 m dock deck.

- structure_collision.gd also makes models inside plain grouping Node3Ds solid ("Vendor Stalls", "Enemy Camps", and the existing Archery Targets / Bone Spears): 19 solid models. Navmesh rebaked (7,217 polygons).
