### Test 35: Tenchijin (Arcanist), build 8748b53 (2026-09-25)
- Passed: logging in after the fall put him at the zone-in point; /teleport; Magic Missile's volley ("looks amazing").
- FACETED CHARACTER FACES (half-elf female, Lira; screenshots): not LOD — measured: the Meshy FBX exports carry hard edges
  on many triangles (27% of the half-elf female's were flat-shaded, 12% of Lira's, 16% of the guard's; the old player
  model 1%), so each facet was lit on its own. New Scripts/mesh_smoothing.gd (MeshSmoothing): recomputes vertex normals
  from the triangles touching each point within 60 degrees (Blender-style auto smooth: real creases stay sharp), keeps
  skinning/UVs/blend shapes, re-aligns tangents, cached per model (~60 ms once), skipped on the dedicated server. Used for
  player race models, NPC race models (NPCRaceModel), vendor models and humanoid monsters; NOT the guards (the user likes
  them as they are). Faceted share after: 1%. Also set meshes/generate_lods=false on all 336 character/monster FBX imports
  (Godot's automatic LODs on skinned models; scenery GLBs keep theirs).
- OUTSKIRTS NORTH TRIMMED past the mountains: the user's ridge runs x ~100-116 from z -992 to 1024 with the gap to Lumora
  City's gate (z ~-30..70). The last region column also holds the town area, so instead of removing regions the terrain
  is cut with HOLES north of x 132 (314,880 points; the ridge and town side untouched) and ZoneBoundary (new
  limit_min/max_x/z exports) walls the zone at x 133. The one rat spawn at (150, 45) moved to (20, 75). Navmesh re-baked
  (10,451 -> 10,376 polygons). Terrain backup in the session scratchpad.
- THE MAUSOLEUM (outstanding since test 33): a walking capsule couldn't get in through its (open, 14 m) door, and inside
  stuck at random spots. Cause: its floor sits exactly on the ground (y 0), and a trimesh floor coinciding with the
  terrain snags a moving character on its triangle edges; its walls were also one-sided (some face inward), so they could
  be walked through from the wrong side. structure_collision.gd now leaves out upward faces lying on the ground (within
  0.25 m of the terrain) and makes every structure's collision solid from both sides. Verified with a capsule: in and out
  through the door, all the way to the back wall, and every wall solid from inside and outside.
- The user's zone design (~/NCT/Aldenexia-Lightfall/Solgrave Expanse Map.drawio): Dustwind's spawns re-laid to follow it —
  Stone Circles west (spiders + Broodmother; the nest is our addition), two Dustwalker camps in the Mirage Zone (the
  Warlord's in the south one), the Undead Roaming Area east (skeletal remains, the Abandoned Watchtower, the Old Bleached
  Banners) with the Graveyard Area on the mesa (higher undead, Tomb Guardian, rare Grave Abomination), and along the south
  the Nomad Camp (Djhanid), the Arcanist Portal (kept clear), the Destroyed Caravan and two Bandit Camps (Dustwalker
  raiders for now); rare Sunmaddened and the Stinger Elite on the high dunes. 36 spawn points.
- check_monster_sheet.py also keeps the sheet's Area column = the places each monster spawns; filled for the Outskirts and
  Dustwind (backup Zone Spawn Sheet.before_sync_2026-09-25.xlsx).

### Test 44: Leeia (Woodstalker), build 31fb9ba — and crime, the courthouse, Lumora's stand-ins (2026-09-26, overnight)
- Working (per the notes): the new sitting postures, the lizardkin tails, on-demand zones (only the Outskirts up at
  login; /teleport into each zone started it).
- JUMPING lost your speed: the moment you left the ground your speed was multiplied by 0.3 ("air control"). Now a jump
  keeps its take-off speed (walking or running) and the keys only steer a little (AIR_STEER 4 m/s²).
  And the jump clips didn't fit the jump: the game's jump is 1.22 s in the air, the clips ran 1.0-3.2 s with a crouching
  wind-up first and their own 0.4-1 m hip rise, so through a real jump the model was still crouching to take off.
  tools/fit_jump.gd keeps just the airborne part of each model's clip, fitted to the real airtime, without the clip's own
  rise (the physics carries the body). Checked with renders.
- The reclining sit (sit_3, from the lizardkin female) bent humans' necks back at a right angle ("the neck look broken"):
  on every model that got it by retargeting, the neck and head now keep their standing angle to the body (they look
  straight up). tools/relax_sit_neck.gd. Checked with renders of all 22.
- CRIME AND THE COURTHOUSE (approved 2026-09-25; the user: "make it so"): Scripts/crime.gd. In town (Lumora, or inside
  the Outskirts' walls), a PvP blow a guard sees (within 40 m) is a crime: 5 silver on your Lumora bounty and -15 with
  the Wardens of the Sacred Flame (a duel isn't a crime; the same victim counts once a minute). A guard who sees someone
  with a bounty within 12 m stops them: Pay, or Resist (can't afford it: "settle it at the courthouse"; asked again after
  90 s). Resisting: every guard attacks on sight (their blows are rolled on your own game, like a monster's); killed by a
  guard, the fine comes out of your purse and the rest stays as bounty. The Magistrate (Magistrate Corvane, at the
  Lumora courthouse) takes a bounty and sells back the standing crimes cost (2 copper a point). Player3D.wanted /
  resisting are replicated (player3d.tscn properties 22/23). Tests: test_crime (35 checks).

  make_lumora_standins.gd builds a stand-in per district (Scenes/props/lumora/*.tscn: sandstone walls with a doorway, a
  roof, collision, a name sign; each its own scene to move or replace): the Sunlit Rest, the Oasisheart Citadel, the Hall
  of Arms, the Courthouse, the Lycaeum Annex, the Temple of the Dawn, the Lantern Lodge (Old Town), the Cisterns, and
  Ralph's Last Round at the Outskirts gate; each behind its district marker, door facing it (patrols stay clear).

### Zones on demand (2026-09-26; the user: "yes build on demand zones. this will help with server resources as well")
- Only the always-on zones run all the time (Data/zones.json "always_on": the Outskirts, which is also the login server).
  Every other zone's server is started by the login server (the world link hub) when a player needs it: crossing a zone
  line, gating or dying to a bind there, or logging in to a character left there. It answers once the new zone's world
  link says hello (measured locally: up in ~4 s, the player there ~10 s after stepping over the line). A zone started this
  way (--on-demand) shuts itself down after 10 minutes with nobody in it (saying "closing" first so nobody is sent to it
  as it goes; someone arriving just then waits for the old server to finish, then it starts again). When the login
  server stops it tells the zones it started to stop too.
- tools/run_world.sh: starts only the always-on zones and gives the login server the command to start the rest
  (ALDENEXIA_ZONE_LAUNCH: run_server.sh, the same options, its own world_logs/world_<zone>.log). --all-zones = the old way
  (every zone, always); --zones=a,b also starts those; --idle-shutdown=<seconds> for testing. The user's start script
  needs no change.
- Client: crossing a zone line says "You set out for <zone>..." and leaves once the server says the zone is up (or "The
  way to <zone> is closed right now" after a failure / 70 s). Logging in straight to the last zone's port (the zone hint)
  when that zone is asleep: no answer -> it logs in through the login server, which wakes the zone and sends it on.
- New RPCs on WorldLink (not Net): _rpc_wake_zone / _rpc_zone_ready. Net's RPC list unchanged.
- Also across zones now: the character list's "online" dot, the "can't delete, they're online" check, and the Join
  screen's player count (the whole world, not only the login server's zone).
- Checked end to end with a local world and a headless client: login (only the Outskirts running), into Dustwind (started
  for it), logged out (Dustwind stopped itself 26 s later with --idle-shutdown=25), logged back in (the client's hint
  failed, the login server woke Dustwind and sent it there), back to the Outskirts; stopping the world stopped the
  on-demand zone too. Tests: test_ondemand (22 checks).
- Tester guide: a "Travelling to another zone" step (a quiet zone takes a few seconds to open) and "New full builds:
  download the zip again" (user: when a build can't be patched, testers download the full zip from the same link, which
  is synced with every build; characters stay on the server).
- Housekeeping: the "[monster-diag]" rat diagnostic is gone (user: the rats "appear to be working"); its comment had
  split the Taunt RPC's comment in two, rejoined.

### Sitting up straight, cross-legged, and lizardkin tails (2026-09-26, during test 42)
- SITTING, THREE WAYS (user: "i'd like all three of those sitting models in game, and randomly pick one when you sit").
  The models had three kinds of sit between them: cross-legged (the human male's 10.9 s idle; the human, elf and dark elf
  females' one-frame poses), legs out leaning back on the hands (the other one-frame poses), and reclining (the
  lizardkin female's). tools/share_sit.gd gives every model all three: sit (cross-legged), sit_2 (legs out), sit_3
  (reclining), keeping each model's own original in its slot and retargeting the rest from the human male, the half-orc
  female and the lizardkin female. Each time you sit, one is picked at random (Player3D._sit_anim / pick_variant), kept
  until you stand; other players see the same one. Only the cross-legged one was leaning back in game (user: "the upright
  one, was the one that looked like the upper body was angled back"): tools/straighten_sit.gd sets its back segments,
  neck and head to the model's standing angles (heads up, per "make sure their heads are upright"); the other two are
  as made. Both run on each model's own library and its _pack.res. Originals: ~/Documents/aldenexia_model_originals/
  2026-09-26_sit. Checked with renders. The dwarf male, gnome female, lizardkin male and ogre male keep their own
  one-frame pose (knees up, hunched) as their sit_2.
  Gotchas found: these rigs number the spine top-down (Hips -> Spine02 -> Spine01 -> Spine -> neck), and an
  AnimationPlayer leaves bones a clip doesn't key where the last clip left them (reset the skeleton before measuring).
- LIZARDKIN TAILS (user: "animate the lizardkins' tails in their animations"). Their rigs had no tail bones (the tail was
  skinned to the hips: it stood rigid and went through the floor when they sat). tools/make_tail.gd fits five bones along
  each tail (found from the smoothed mesh, strays dropped) and re-weights the tail onto them, blending into the hips at
  the root: models/Lizardkin */lizardkin_*_breathing_idle_tail_mesh.res (mesh + skin + bones as metadata). At runtime
  TailRig (Scripts/tail_rig.gd) adds the bones, swaps the mesh in, and swings the tail over every animation: a lazy sway
  standing, a swing walking, a quicker wider one running (a wave down to the tip), lifting the root whenever the tip
  would sink into the floor (sitting, dead). The FBX, smoothed mesh and animations are unchanged. Re-run make_tail.gd if
  smooth_models.sh ever rebuilds a lizardkin. Checked with renders (idle, run, sit, both sexes).
- PET FALLING AGAIN AND AGAIN (test 43, Zuikasoz's Echo of Shadewend: walking in place, "...falls back in beside you"
  every few seconds): the fall safety net put the pet at its owner's height 1.5 m to the side, which on a slope is under
  the ground, so it fell straight through again. It now drops it onto the ground found by a ray there
  (PetMinion.rescue_spot()), and says the line once a minute at most.
- TEST 43 DISCONNECTS: the Proxmox backup at 02:00 (a 4 KB write took ~30 s on the VM; server frames stalled up to 41 s).
  Not a game bug. With the user's OK the VM's idle Project Zomboid LinuxGSM cron jobs were disabled and n8n stopped.
- Tests: test_test42 (every model has all three sits, the cross-legged one upright; tail meshes, bones, weights; the pet
  rescue). Suite 4384.

  group. The hub (the Outskirts' own zone) only re-sent the groups when another zone's roster changed, never its own:
  it now pushes them whenever someone arrives in or leaves its own zone too (world_link.gd _share_roster / the roster tick).
- AGGRO AFTER DEATH: Maedianie died fighting Halvek and he chased her to her bind point. Dying now wipes you off the hate
  list of every monster within 300 m (player3d.gd _wipe_aggro(); a remote player's request goes to the server), and a
  monster whose first target is down goes for whoever else is still fighting it.
- HEAL MESSAGES: the healer reads "You heal Maedianie for 100 with Regrowth.", the healed "Zozuur heals you for 100 with
  Regrowth.", everyone else "Zozuur heals Maedianie for 100 with Regrowth." (herself / himself on a self-heal).
- NETWORK WIDGET: it forgot where you put it at every login (its position loaded before your character did); it now
  loads it once your character is in.
- PET TITLES: a groupmate's pet showed "<Default Hero's pet>"; the title is refreshed from its owner's name.
- RATS TURNING ROUND AND ROUND: a player and an assisting pet traded the top of a rat's hate list blow by blow, and it
  turned between them. A monster now keeps its target until someone has 10% more threat than it (a taunt still takes it
  at once): monster3d.gd THREAT_SWITCH_MARGIN.
- AUTO-LOOT ON KILL: a new Options toggle (Escape -> Options, off by default). When a monster that was in a fight, or
  that you had targeted, dies within 10 m of you, you loot everything within 10 m, as the G key does (loot is personal:
  only ever your own share). Quiet when there's nothing.
- KENJI'S TAIL TIP stayed put while the middle of the tail swept: the part curled round in front of his paws was left
  out of the tail. The tail now follows the curl round to the tip (animate_sitting_cat.py TAIL_FRONT_Y); checked with
  renders.
- TROLL SHOULDERS: running, the troll's upper back bunched into a hump: the run shrugs the clavicle bones 20-40 degrees
  and a troll's heavy back comes with them. New tools/relax_shoulders.gd keeps 40% of the shoulders' movement away from
  their idle pose in the run and walk (the arms still swing fully); applied to the troll male's library and pack
  (it survives a pack rebuild). Before/after renders.
- LIGHTMENDER: Spiritual Weapon is a level 1 spell (the user's player_spells.json change, and now the sheet too) and a
  starting spell, like the Wildspeaker's and Gravecaller's pets. It has 40% of the caster's health like the other pets
  (the spell had 0.3; description and sheet match). It holds no weapon (user: "it is not a living pet per se"): its pet gear still
  counts, nothing shows in its hands (SummonedPet.HOLDS_NO_GEAR).
- CHARACTERS: an account holds 14 characters, one of each class (was 8). Tester guide updated.
- CAMPFIRES YOU LIGHT: right-click a Firewood Bundle in your bags -> Light. A campfire burns a step in front of you for
  10 / 20 / 30 minutes (fir / ironwood / palm), then burns down. You can cook at it and lingering by it gives Warmth of
  the Campfire as at the roadside fires; everyone within 15 m earns +3 / 6 / 10% experience from kills (the best fire
  only; worked out on the server where the kill's XP is shared, so the save checks count it). Not in town (within 40 m
  of a vendor, guard, townsperson, crafting station or the bank, or anywhere in the city), not in combat (or hit in the
  last 10 s), not within 20 m of another fire. Everyone sees it, players who arrive later too
  (Scripts/campfire_relay.gd, riding on WorldItems like trading).
- SPAWNS: "i can't kill enemies without them spawning on top of me. there is no way to setup a camp". A spawn point's
  timer started when its monster SPAWNED, so by the time you'd killed it, its replacement came at once (and 10-40 s
  timers). Now each dead monster's own timer starts when it dies (EverQuest-style), the common monsters' timers are 6x
  longer with a 1 minute floor (Outskirts, Dustwind, Ashfall: 12 s -> 72 s, 18 s -> 108 s, 40 s -> 4 minutes; named and
  rare ones unchanged), and nothing ever appears within 12 m of a player. A new spawn point still fills up 5 s apart.
- HALVEK now waits inside the graveyard mausoleum (at the back, x 58 z -244; the door faces west); the quest and guard
  lines say so.
- FEAR, EVERQUEST-STYLE (user: "randomly either lower your stats or make you run randomly depending on your save roll"):
  a monster's fear (Halvek's Grave Command, Dread Mirage, Wail of the Grave) still gets the full resist roll first; if it
  lands, a fear save (even at the caster's level, +/-5% a level, +2% a Wisdom point over 10, +5% a point of racial fear
  save: the Lizardkin's +2 is live now; 10-90%). Made: SHAKEN (the spell's own penalty plus -2 Strength, Dexterity,
  Wisdom). Failed: TERRIFIED, you run blindly (a new direction every 1.5 s, turning off walls), can't attack or cast (a
  cast in progress is interrupted), until it wears off, a cure removes it, or you die (player3d.gd receive_fear()).
- A pet with Assist, and macros, errored when the owner's target had just been freed ("Trying to assign invalid
  previously freed instance", pet_minion.gd; it made test_test41 fail now and then): fixed.
- Tests: test_test42 (51 checks). Full suite: 4327 checks, RPC list unchanged.

### The static critters move (2026-09-26; user: "can you animate the static enemies as well please?")
- Every humanoid monster was already rigged; the seven critters (rat, snake, bat, slime, spider, spiderling, dune scarab)
  were single meshes with no bones. Shaders/critter_motion.gdshader moves their vertices on the graphics card by body
  type: the rat scurries (bob, sway, paddling feet, a swishing tail), spiders and the scarab step their legs in two
  alternating sets and bob, the snake slithers in an S-wave with a steady head, the bat's wings beat, the slime squashes,
  stretches and jiggles. Faster when they move, gentle idle motion when still, a lunge / strike / rear-up / swoop / surge
  on each attack. Same textures as before (the shader does the PBR).
- monster3d.gd: CRITTER_MOTION (body type, and which of the mesh's axes the body lies along, measured from each mesh),
  _update_critter_motion() every frame on every screen (speed from the position, so copies on players' screens move too),
  and a critter's attack now sets the replicated anim_state to "attack" for a moment so everyone sees the lunge.
- Checked with renders of all seven (standing, two moments walking, attacking). Tests: test_critters (49 checks).
- Not yet: summoned animal pets that use these meshes stay still (a follow-up).

### Test 41: Zozuur (Voidknight), Maedianie (Wildspeaker), build 3b3d5ed
- Working (per the notes): shields right on the troll; torches and campfires "much better".
- GROUP XP: a kill's XP went only to whoever landed the killing blow, so a healer standing back got nothing. Now everyone
  grouped with the killer, in the zone and within 100 m of the kill, shares it: split evenly with a 10% bonus per extra
  member (two players: 55% each), whether or not they hit it; faction changes from the kill reach the whole group. On a
  server the group comes from the world link (by name); on one machine from the killer's group (monster3d.gd
  xp_recipients / group_xp_share).
- PETS WIELD THEIR GEAR: the pet's primary and offhand from its gear window (P) show in its hands, for everyone (a
  replicated held_gear on the pet, as on players); summoned humanoid pets too (user: "please do the same for all summoned
  humanoid pets"). Animal pets have no hands, so nothing shows on them.
- PETS AND THE TANK: on Assist, if you have a /focus set on another player, your pet fights what that player is fighting
  (they have it targeted and stand within 8 m of it): a healer's pet helps without the healer swinging.
- PETS STICK: a pet stays on the target it started on until that dies (it already did; the owner switching targets never
  moves it), unless you send it at another with its Attack button; when it dies, it now goes straight on to your (or the
  tank's) current target without waiting for you to attack.
- STARTING WEAPONS: every class's starter weapon (Player3D.STARTER_WEAPONS; character_creation uses the same table) is
  given once at login to a character made before theirs existed (Maedianie, a Wildspeaker from before test 34, had no
  Fir Staff), unless it's already in their bags or hands.

### Kenji and Oni move (2026-09-25; user: "Oni walking / running, her tail moving, and the same for Kenji")
- ONI: the user's rig (~/NCT/Aldenexia-Lightfall/models/Oni/Oni Rigged.blend: 32 bones, the mesh fully weighted) gets three
  animations from tools/blender/animate_cat.py: idle (breathing, a lazy tail sway, a look around), walk (a cat's
  diagonal gait, paws lifting on the forward swing, a small bob, the tail waving in a travelling curve) and run (a
  bound: front legs together, back legs together, the spine flexing, the tail streaming). She's turned 4 degrees
  straight first. Exported to models/Oni/oni_animated.glb. In game she plays idle / walk / run by how far she really
  moves each frame (so it works on every screen, not only on the server that moves her): her patrol is a walk, a chase a
  run. The user's .blend is not changed.
- KENJI is sculpted sitting (his tail curled round his paws), so he can't walk; tools/blender/animate_sitting_cat.py gives
  him shape keys found from the mesh itself (the tail sweeping in and out, the head turning both ways, breathing) and a
  6-second idle mixing them (models/Kenji/kenji_animated.glb). For Kenji to walk too he needs a standing model.
- CatModel.build_animated() / animate_by_speed() (cat_model.gd). Checked with Blender renders of every key and frames, and
  in Godot (textures, facing, clips). Tests: test_cats (16 checks).

### Duels and PvP (2026-09-25; user: "both a pvp and a duel system, /pvp and /duel, targeted or name of player")
- Scripts/player_versus.gd (a "Versus" node on every player), commands in the chat box:
  /duel [name] (or /dual, or with a player targeted): they get Accept / Decline; a 3-second count, then "Fight!"; it
  ends when one would fall to 0 (left at 1 health: nobody dies), with /yield, 40 m apart, or after 5 minutes.
  /pvp [name]: a declared fight, no consent needed: they're warned and get 5 seconds, then it's to the death (normal
  death rules) until 10 minutes pass without a blow; level 5 and up, within 5 levels of each other.
- Only the two are hostile to each other (Player3D.hostile_to, replicated; TargetFrame.faction_status() needs it both
  ways); to everyone else they stay allies. Melee and single-target spells work on a foe; the blow is rolled on the
  attacker's machine and taken on the victim's, which checks the attacker is a foe and close by. A PvP death tells
  everyone nearby who did it. Not yet: area spells and pets against players, ailments (stun, root...) on players, and
  the crime system (attacking a player in town).
- Checked with two headless clients on a local world: "/dual Zzopponent", the accept popup, the count on both machines,
  the foe shown as an enemy, blows landing on the other machine.
- Tests: test_versus (26 checks).

### Version 0.4.6: the sky, cartography, binding (2026-09-25)
- VERSION 0.4.6 (project.godot): a new full client base is needed (tools/update.sh --new-base); the tester guide's
  examples say v0.4.6.
- THE SKY (Shaders/sky.gdshader, driven by day_night_cycle.gd): realistic colour with warm sunrises and sunsets, the sun's
  disc and glow, procedural twinkling stars at night, the moon with its phases (a 14-day real-time cycle, the same for
  everyone: the new moon is the dark moon), and drifting clouds that thicken and turn grey in rain and hide the stars.
  No textures needed. The sun and the moon now cross the sky (rising on one side, setting on the other; shadows turn
  through the day); a thin moon lights the land less. Checked with rendered shots (noon, sunset, rain, full and crescent
  moon) and in the Outskirts itself.
- CARTOGRAPHY (Scripts/cartography.gd, map_window.gd; the Game Systems notes, section 6): you start with no map. Buy the
  Scroll of Cartography (5 silver), Blank Parchment (5c) and a Charcoal Stick (2c) from Cartographer Ivo Quill by
  Lumora's south gate (with compasses); learn the scroll, carry the kit, and the land is charted as you walk (fog of war,
  per character, per zone, saved on the server with the character). M opens the map: a window you can move and resize,
  wheel to zoom, drag to pan, right-click (or "Add note") for your own notes. It's drawn from the zone itself: shaded
  hills, and walls, buildings and camps in ink. The skill (to 100; Woodstalkers fastest, Shadowblades and Troubadours
  next) rises as you chart new ground; place names and zone borders at 21, camps, people, resources and contour lines at
  51, patrol routes, rare creatures and hidden places at 76. A compass adds "you are here". The server lets the skill
  rise past the usual level cap (it's walked, not levelled). New: scrolls can teach a skill ("teaches_skill").
- BINDING (user's clarification): Attune Spirit binds ONLY the target (or yourself with no target), and can be cast
  again and again (recast 5 s): bind the whole group before a hard dungeon, one at a time. The hour is each person's:
  their bind point changes at most once an hour.
- Shadowlight lasts 15 minutes (was an hour), like every non-combat buff (spells JSON and sheet).
- THE WALLS LOOKED STRETCHED: the Lumora Gates and City Wall models mapped their whole texture once across the whole
  wall (UVs 0..1 over 85 m and 1,222 m), and both carried an unapplied, uneven scale (City Wall 1 x 0.2 x 2.5). Fixed in
  the user's .blend files (~/NCT/Aldenexia-Lightfall/models: scale applied, UVs box-projected at 4 m, so the stone
  repeats like real masonry) and re-exported over models/Lumora Gates/*.glb; the geometry and placement are unchanged
  (checked). Originals kept in ~/Documents/aldenexia_model_originals/2026-09-25/walls.
- SAND INSIDE THE TOWN: the Outskirts town's ground texture stopped a metre short of its walls, so a strip of sand showed
  along them (81 spots). The town ground now runs under the walls (Terrain3D control map, x 60..117, z -28..57; only the
  two regions there changed; heights checked identical).
- A basic forge by the Outskirts gate, beside the gate's campfire (user: a level 2 can make a first ingot without the
  walk into Lumora); the Outskirts navmesh rebaked.
- Tests: test_cartography (45 checks, with the sky's sun, moon and phases).

### Test 39: Zozuur (Voidknight), Maedianie (Wildspeaker), build adc0f32
- Working (per the notes): party chat across zones; macros (a taunt macro). Macros already live on the server: shared
  ones on the account, the rest in the character, so they follow a player to any PC.
- /TELL ACROSS ZONES: the chat box refused a name that wasn't in your own zone before the world link ever saw it (the
  bot test called the send directly, so it passed). It now lets the server find them, and only refuses in a LAN game.
- INVITES ACROSS ZONES: /invite name reaches a player in any zone (world_link.gd: a message to one player by name,
  routed like a tell); they get the Accept / Decline popup; accepting joins the leader's group on the hub (group_join),
  which every zone then shows. Full group, already grouped, offline and declined are all reported to the inviter.
  Checked with two headless clients (Outskirts and Dustwind): invite, accept, both group frames, tells both ways.
- Stances (and a tank's group stance, renewed every few seconds, which read "0:00") show no timer; nor does anything
  without an end (Starving, Thirsty).
- The group window fades a member beyond a heal's reach (16 m: "15m" plus the 1 m slack) or in another zone.
- WEAPONS IN HAND (Scripts/held_gear.gd): your equipped weapon and shield show in your hands, always, and everyone sees
  them (a replicated held_gear string on the player). items.json "held_model" says which model an item shows: swords
  -> shortsword; daggers -> dagger; cleaver and war axe -> one-handed axe; staves -> staff; tin shield -> buckler, bronze
  shield -> kite shield, bone shield -> round shield. Spear and hand-to-hand wraps: nothing (no model). The fit is
  worked out from each race's own skeleton (a sword points forward from the right fist, a staff stands up, a shield
  straps to the left forearm), scaled by the forearm, so no race needs hand-tuning; the recolour and texture override
  leave held gear alone. Checked on human, dwarf, ogre, gnome and elf: grip in the hand, blade and shield clear of the
  body while idle. Not yet: bows, and weapons on monsters and guards.
- BINDING (user: "anywhere for casters, just like in eq"): Attune Spirit is now the seven casters' (Lightmender,
  Spiritweaver, Wildspeaker, Gravecaller, Runecaster, Arcanist, Chaosborn; it was every class): it binds the caster where
  they stand, or a friendly player they target within 20 m (their own machine checks the caster is really there).
  Others ask a caster, or pay Soulbinder Maren at Lumora's Dawnspire Altar a silver (Scripts/soul_binder_npc.gd,
  Data/soul_binder.json). Once an hour however you're bound. A non-caster who learned the spell before can't cast it.
- Tests: test_test39 (87 checks).

### Test 38: Zozuur (Voidknight), Maedianie (Wildspeaker) (2026-09-25)
- Hail a targeted player or pet: you say "Hail, Zozuur!" (H or /hail; guards and NPCs as before).
- LUMORA is its own zone (Scenes/zones/lumora.tscn, zone id "lumora", port 8922 — inside the opened 8920-9019 range;
  first pass: flat ground, town music, rain, the world link). Entered through the Outskirts' town at its far north end
  (a zone line across the gated area, x 113); the Outskirts' north wall moved in to x 118. You arrive at the matching
  marker facing into the zone.
- GROUPS SURVIVE ZONING: the world link keeps every group by character name for the whole server (the hub holds them,
  every zone gets a copy). Your group frame lists members in other zones as "Name (Zone)" without bars; /party reaches
  every member in any zone; a real logout (not a zone change) leaves the group and the next member leads.
- GM announcements (/announce) reach every zone.
- /gm enable is the character's: a flag file next to its server password file (server_characters/<key>_gm.json). Zoning
  keeps game master mode on quietly; /gm disable (or logging out of GM) clears it.
- A group buff's tooltip says "Caster: Maedianie" (any buff someone else cast on you), instead of "(from your group)".
- Rain works in every zone (checked by test for each).
- BUG FOUND IN TESTING, FIXED: two characters on the same spot stood on each other's heads. Each client lifts its own
  body on top of the other, so they could climb 140 m into the sky and fall to their deaths; and one standing on
  another was carried off with them when they zoned or gated (dragged 1,200 m, through the zone line). Players now pass
  through each other as in EverQuest (collision exceptions both ways; targeting rays still hit them), and nothing
  underfoot carries a player, monster or pet (platform_floor_layers 0: there are no moving platforms).
- Checked end to end with two headless clients on a local four-zone world, both arriving on the same spot: the one
  staying behind stayed put; the group kept both members on both sides ("Zzstay (Lumora Outskirts)" / "(Dustwind
  Plateaus)"); /party both ways across zones; the zoner's logout emptied the other's group.
- Tests: test_test38 (34 checks).

### Animation pack, step 1: every race gets every cast and death (2026-09-25)
- tools/make_animation_pack.gd pools the casting, buff and death animations from all 22 race folders (32 files) and
  retargets each onto every race's skeleton. Each model's pack (<library>_pack.res, what CHARACTER_MODELS now loads)
  keeps its own library exactly as it was and adds the rest as variants: cast_beneficial_2.., cast_detrimental_2..,
  death_2.. . Per race: 9-10 buff casts, 5-6 offensive casts, 12 deaths.
- Retargeting: each bone's turn from its T-pose is carried over; the ARMS are aimed at the source's directions instead,
  because the models' T-poses differ (ogre and dwarf arms rest 30-37 degrees lower than the elf's). Checked: arm
  directions match the source to within a hundredth of a degree; legs and spine within 11 degrees (their rests differ).
- No repeats: clips are compared by motion (where the arms point at nine moments), not file name. 5 files were the same
  motion under a "-buff" and a plain name (2H Cast Spell 01, 2H Magic Attack 01, Two Hand Spell Casting, 1H Cast Spell
  01, 2H Magic Attack 03): each kept once; variants matching a race's own clips are left out.
- The game picks a random variant for every cast and every death (Player3D.pick_variant; guards too); the choice is
  replicated like any animation.
- Tests (test_models): every race uses its pack, keeps its own clips, has its variants, and has no repeated motions.

### Appearance: sliders at creation, the Silvered Mirror, the look locked (2026-09-25)
- User's rules: the look is chosen once — at character creation, or once at Lumora's mirror for characters made before
  this (both test the same window) — then locked; afterwards only the hair (colour now; hair and beard styles and
  accessories when their models exist) can change.
- Scripts/appearance.gd (Appearance): sliders height (-1..1: +/-6% of the model, 5% dwarf/gnome/halfling, 7% ogre/troll),
  weight (-0.5..1), muscle (-0.3..1), and for women bust, waist, hips (-1..1); skin colour (a palette per race), hair
  colour (14), eye colour (10, SWITCHED OFF: EYE_COLOUR_ENABLED, the automatic eye masks only work on a few models), "" = as painted; hair_style / beard_style / accessories keys, empty for now. validate(),
  merge() (the lock), apply() (blend shapes, height, material). Saved as player_data "appearance", replicated as
  Player3D.appearance_json (so everyone sees it), applied whenever the model is built. Player3D.set_appearance().
- The server enforces it: ServerTrust.check keeps a locked look except the changeable keys, keeps every value in range,
  locks a look the first time it arrives, and logs attempts to change a locked one.
- Body sliders are blend shapes in the rebuilt meshes: tools/blender/shape_body.py morph() (weight, muscle for all;
  bust, waist, hips for women), each built on its own copy, subdivided identically, stored as the difference;
  make_smooth_models.gd carries them into the .res. All 22 models rebuilt.
- Colours: tools/blender/make_masks.py finds each model's skin, hair and eyes (a flood fill over the mesh from seeds at
  the cheeks / nose / forehead and the back of the head, voting and competing colours, the clothes as rivals so a white
  blouse isn't skin), per-model tuning in tools/blender/mask_config.json; tools/make_masks.sh -> <scene>_mask.png +
  .json (each region's own colour). Shaders/appearance.gdshader recolours a region keeping its light and shade.
- Scripts/appearance_editor.gd: the window — a turning preview (drag), sliders, colour swatches, Reset / Cancel /
  Save; at the mirror's first use saving asks "This is final: save?". Character creation: "Appearance..." button (the
  look is saved locked with the new character). The mirror: "The Silvered Mirror" by the town gate (66, 20;
  world_objects.json, WorldNote opens "mirror").
- Tests: test_appearance.gd.

### Overnight 2026-09-25 (4): character models rebuilt smooth; the elf male's run
- Diagnosis (Blender renders): the TEXTURES are fine; the GEOMETRY was the "low poly" look — ~10k triangles with hard
  edges on most of them and flat custom normals (a nose of four triangles). Shade Smooth didn't help because the file's
  custom normals override it. Runtime normal smoothing (test 35) only half-fixed it.
- tools/blender/smooth_character.py: clears the custom normals and sharp edges, merges split vertices, shades smooth,
  one level of Catmull-Clark subdivision (skin weights interpolated): ~10k -> ~62k triangles. (Decimating back down
  smeared the lips, so it doesn't.) tools/make_smooth_models.gd turns that into <scene>_smooth_mesh.res next to the FBX:
  bone indices remapped by name onto the FBX's own skin, distance LODs generated, compressed. tools/smooth_models.sh
  does all 22 race models (done). The FBX files are NOT changed: MeshSmoothing.use_rebuilt_mesh() swaps the mesh in at
  load (players, NPCs, vendors, pets, the editor preview); delete a .res to go back. +39 MB of meshes.
- Elf male "pigeon toed, run doesn't look right": measured and rendered — his feet toe OUT like everyone's, but his
  Mixamo run was made for wider hips: each foot landed on or across the middle (closest 0.028 m apart), knees knocking.
  tools/widen_stride.gd swings both thighs out 2 degrees in his run (closest now 0.061 m, like the human's scaled to his
  hips); walk untouched (it was fine). The library is edited in place and marked, so re-running changes nothing.
- Tests: test_models.gd (each rebuilt mesh fits its skeleton and skin, weights, sits vertex for vertex on the old one,
  is subdivided; the elf's feet never cross). Suite: 3,451 checks pass.

  letter is capitalised; the server refuses a new character with any other name or with a "surname" in its data. Older
  names still log in.
- Verified headless: every class's armour list; travel for Arcanist/Wildspeaker/Chaosborn (attune, chooser, portal on
  cast, arrival at the stone, evac to 20,13, gates to the bind point, bind + 1-hour refusal, moving closes the portal,
  Troubadour gate isn't a song); Perception at the gate (WIS 8: 2 of 3 notices, WIS 18 + skill 20: all 3); surnames (level
  gate, once, GM change/clear, nameplate, /who, bad names) and the creation filter ("tar gon 2" -> "Targon"). NOT verified:
  other group members crossing a portal / being pulled (needs two players), node positions on the real terrain.
- Player's Manual regenerated and republished (armour per class, caster travel, gates, perception, names).

### Multi-item quests and item tracking in the journal (2026-09-24)
- quests.gd: a hand-in objective can ask for several different items — {"type": "hand_in", "items": {id: n, ...}} — handed
  in in any order, a few at a time; progress is saved per item ("given": {id: n}, "progress" = the total). The single-item
  form ("item"/"count") and old saves (only "progress") still work (Quests.requirements() / given()). Handing in more of an
  item than it still needs: guards say "I have all the X I need. Keep it.", Sahren shakes his head. auto_start and quest
  chains work with any of the items; /quests and the Give window show "Lock of Hair 1/1, Small Tin Cup 0/1".
- QUEST JOURNAL (J): every item a quest asks for is a row with its icon — the number you're carrying on it like a bag
  slot, dimmed when you have none — and "given N / M · carrying K"; yellow when you carry enough to finish that item,
  green with a tick when it's done. Refreshes when your bags change, not only when quest progress does.
- Verified headless (a test quest wanting 2 rat tails + 1 bat wing): part hand-ins, "have enough", completion, journal rows
  (icon, stack number, dimming, colours), and a pre-change save finishing Release the Hollowed. Not yet seen in a window.
- DECIDED (user): quest recipe rewards are the recipe SCROLLS. Armour follows D&D: casters cloth; rangers leather; rogues
  leather/chain; tanks leather/chain/plate — and Troubadours can wear plate. Not built yet (see outstanding_items.txt).

### Racial traits completed (2026-09-24)
- The creation screen listed every race's traits but only part were live (apply_racial_modifiers(): health/mana/damage/
  dodge/resist numbers, dark sight, torches, amphibious). New Player3D.apply_racial_traits() reads the rest straight from
  character_options.json "traits": skill bonuses (Dwarf Blacksmithing/Prospecting +15, Gnome Tinkering +20, Halfling Forage/
  Fishing +10 and Stealth +15, Elf/Half-Elf Tracking/Divination, Vol'kyne Stealth/Lockpicking +15, Half-Orc Defense +10);
  skill growth (Human +5%, Half-Orc combat skills +5%, Half-Elf -5%); Human health/mana and Half-Elf mana regen +5% (with
  fractional carry so it isn't rounded away); faction offsets (Human +10, Half-Elf +5); Elf +10% speed outdoors; Gnome 5%
  spell crits (175%; logged "Your spell strikes with unusual force!") replacing the melee crit bonus it wrongly had;
  Vol'kyne necromancy/illusion +10%; Lizardkin bite (+1d6 bare-handed) and in-combat regen. Penalty notes given numbers:
  Vol'kyne regen halved by day, Lizardkin -5 accuracy at night, Half-Elf -5% skill growth, Troll/Vol'kyne -10 and
  Gnome/Half-Orc -5 faction.
- Racial skill points count wherever the skill is USED (combat skill effects, craft and gather success, perception) but not
  for recipe/node minimums or skill-ups (Player3D.effective_skill()). The abilities book lists them ("Racial bonus: +15").
- FIXES: Lizardkin +2 AC was added to gear_ac, which the first equipment change recomputed (lost); now race_ac_bonus.
  Lizardkin charisma was cut by 2 a second time at every login (racial_stats.json's base 8 already has it) — removed.
- Nothing is saved: traits are worked out at login, so all 9 server characters get them with the update. Checked the
  server saves: every character's stored stats = its race's base + exactly 4 points (no fixes needed).
- Not yet (no system): research, intimidation, swamps, fear saves, knockback immunity (outstanding_items.txt).
- Verified headless: a real player per race (all 11) — skill bonuses, gain/regen multipliers, faction, speed, AC after an
  equipment reset, charisma, 106/2000 Gnome spell crits, Vol'kyne necromancy 60 -> 66. RPC list unchanged (patch-safe).
- Player's Manual regenerated and republished: races now show live traits, the few planned ones, and the drawbacks in numbers.

### Quest discovery audit: every quest can now be found by talking (2026-09-24)
- "Reyna never gives the wreck quest": the quest was only started by SAYING a keyword (wagon, wreck, strongbox...) that nothing on screen offered; her hail is a random line from the shared guard pool (links: Lumora, dustwalkers, raiders, Kenji). Audit of all five quests: The Wreck (Reyna) undiscoverable except by finding the wreck; A Warden Not at Rest fine (starts when you loot the medal) but no hint to hand it in; The Silent Den (Corwin) only via looting silk; The Weavemother's Mark: "Ask me about the den" wasn't clickable; Release the Hollowed (Sahren) reachable only while carrying a token (Djhanid -> water -> token looped).
- HAIL HOOKS (new, Data/guard_topics.json "hail_hooks" by guard name; guard_npc.gd _hail_hook_line()): hailing a guard says the first hook whose requirements hold instead of a random line, linking the keyword that starts or finishes the quest. Reyna: medal in pack -> hand it in; strongbox in pack -> hand it in; wreck not started -> {wagon}. Corwin: sigil in pack; silk in pack; den done & mark not started -> {den} ("after_quest" condition); den not started -> {spiders}. No hook -> the usual random line. DRAFT lines.
- quests.json: The Silent Den's complete/already_done texts link {den}. traveling_merchant.json: Sahren's token line links {Hollowed}.
- Verified headless (real guard scene, stand-in player): every hook fires in the right state, clicking wagon / den starts the quests, looting the medal / silk starts theirs, a guard with nothing to offer gives a random line.

### Player's Manual; testing checklist split into outstanding / completed (2026-09-24)
- PLAYER_MANUAL.html (project root, new; published as an artifact, same look as TESTER_GUIDE.html): the world and the Lightfall (from History.txt), your first hour, how the game works (levels, food/water, death, poison, casting, campfires, groups, trading, bank, languages, XP table), all 11 races (stats, resistances, languages, classes, traits — the ones the engine doesn't do yet marked "planned"), all 14 classes (role, stats, races, starting kit, stances, full spell list with mana/cast/recast/range and which scribe sells it), spells and abilities (learning, upgrades, passives, stances, songs, pets, pickpocket), skills (what each does), crafting (rules, gathering nodes, every recipe by trade with scroll price and vendor) and Lumora Outskirts (places, people, quests, gate raids, bestiary).
- It is GENERATED: tools/make_manual.py fills tools/manual_template.html (the hand-written text — edit words there) from Data/*.json, the starting spells in character_creation.gd and the full controls list in TESTER_GUIDE.html (kept in one place). Re-run `python3 tools/make_manual.py` after data changes and republish.
- testing_items.txt is now in two parts, OUTSTANDING (72) at the top and COMPLETED (13) below, each with the same section headings; the two "- working" Blademaster items moved to COMPLETED.

## 2026-09-17 — Session 45: Generic spell-effect engine, 7 more classes brought to parity, tradeskills (cooking/alchemy), Wildspeaker pet, fear/charm mechanics, multiplayer CC/heal relay, UI theme + resize pass, 5 new mob models, network widget

### Generic, data-driven spell-effect engine (the foundation for everything else this session)
- Before this session, only Voidknight/Spiritweaver's spells had any real behavior
  beyond damage — every other class's spells either dealt generic damage only or did
  nothing at all, despite having real `effect_type`/`duration`/`damage` data.
- `player3d.gd`'s `_apply_generic_spell_effect()` now drives real behavior off each
  spell's own data for `heal`/`hot`/`dot`/`buff`/`debuff`/`snare`/`root`/`blind`/
  `silence`/`absorb`/`stun`/`fear`/`charm`/`mesmerize`/`confuse`/`cure`, for any class,
  present or future — named per-spell special cases (Voidknight/Spiritweaver's
  hand-tuned spells) still take priority and are untouched.
- Fixed a real pre-existing bug found while building this: every cone-target spell
  (any class) was unconditionally applying Voidknight's Gravechill slow to whatever it
  hit, instead of its own effect.
- Added the missing `pbaoe`, `chain`, and `none` (teleport) spell target types —
  previously silent no-ops that still consumed mana/cooldown.
- Fixed 8 spells across several classes mis-tagged as `target: enemy` despite being
  self-buffs/shields by description (`arcane_armor`, `ki_barrier`,
  `wholeness_of_body`, `detect_magic`, `unstable_ward`, `wild_barrier`, `chaos_weave`,
  `chaos_shield`).
- "Cures" (`remove_curse`, `purify`) previously had `effect_type: null` — pure flavor
  text with zero effect, even single-player. New `cure` effect type actually removes a
  debuff (picks the first active effect whose originating spell was enemy-targeted —
  data-driven, no per-spell wiring needed for future cure spells).
- stun/fear/charm/mesmerize/confuse (Aetherfist/Blademaster/Gravecaller/Runecaster/
  etc.) share one honest approximation for now (target can't attack for the duration)
  since real flee/allegiance-flip/confuse-movement behavior doesn't exist yet — Fear
  and Charm got upgraded past this later in the session (see below).

### Player_spells.json classification import + Chaosborn fix
- Imported the user's "Player Spells Classifications.xlsx" spreadsheet: added a new
  `skill_category` field (separate from `spell_school`, which still drives physical-
  vs-magic damage math and would have broken if overwritten) to 559 of 560 spells, so
  every spell now raises the correct skill on use via the existing skill-xp system.
- Fixed a class-name mismatch the user found and corrected mid-session: Chaosborn's
  41 spells were keyed under the old name "Wildmage" in `class_level_requirements`.

### 7 more classes brought to full parity (spell data + scrolls + starting kit)
- **Arcanist, Aetherfist** — first two classes done. Aetherfist also got: its
  previously-dead `resolve_aetherfist_attack()` (Double/Triple Attack at L10+/15+)
  wired into the real attack path; a real starting `worn_hand_wraps` weapon (found and
  fixed a pre-existing bug in the process — every class silently got a default
  `rusty_sword`, and melee classes got a *second*, duplicate one); `STARTING_SPELLS`
  fixed (a stale comment claimed Aetherfist had zero spell data at all — it has 16).
- **Blademaster, Lightsworn, Lightmender, Woodstalker, Gravecaller, Runecaster** —
  same full-parity pass.
- **Chaosborn** — same pass, once its class-name mismatch (above) was fixed.
- Authored 130+ new scroll items + vendor stock entries across all of the above
  (placeholder icon, per the user's direction, on every new item lacking real art).
- Fixed a pre-existing town-wide bug while auditing item icons: 21 of the 22 items
  that actually drop in Lumora Outskirts pointed to icon files that were never
  created (`Assets/Items/*.png`) — all now use the placeholder icon instead of
  rendering as a blank/black block.
- Shadowblade, Troubadour, Wildspeaker got the same spell-data/scroll treatment, but
  their signature mechanics (real stealth/pickpocketing, a toggle-song-with-delayed-
  effect system) are still not built — flagged, not silently skipped.

### Tradeskills — cooking and alchemy (proof of concept)
- New generic `TradeskillWindow` (draggable, persistent, resizable) shared by every
  station — recipes live in `Data/tradeskill_recipes.json`, so a future stove/forge is
  just new data, no new code.
- Right-click a campfire (range-based, not raycast — it has no collision body) to
  cook; right-click a new Basic Alchemy Kit item to mix.
- Dragging in a full stack (e.g. 4 raw meat) crafts all 4 at once instead of requiring
  an exact 1-for-1 match — fixed after the user reported it only working for 1.
- Fixed the ingredient slot's icon not rendering at all (had used a custom `_draw()`
  call instead of `texture_normal`, the pattern the backpack actually uses) and added
  a hover tooltip showing the item name.
- New items: Cooked Meat (eatable, +25 satiety), Basic Poison (right-click an
  equipped weapon to apply +2 damage on hit), Basic Alchemy Kit.

### Fear and Charm — real mechanics, not the generic CC placeholder
- Fear: the monster now actually flees in a random direction, picking a new one every
  4 seconds, for the spell's duration — running within ~1.5m of another social
  monster aggros it onto the player (reuses the existing threat-table system).
- Charm: casting it pops the *same* pet-control window used for a real summoned pet
  (`pet_frame.gd`, reused verbatim) against the charmed monster — Follow/Attack/Sit/
  Guard/Assist/Dismiss all work, with Guard/Assist targeting other monsters instead of
  the player.
- Wildspeaker's pet, "Spirit of the Woods" (new starting spell): a simple placeholder
  visual (translucent green glow, matching how Phantasmal Echo's own code describes
  its pre-art placeholder) that does ranged damage and casts a real heal-over-time
  (5/sec for 15s, every 30s) on whichever group member is most damaged.

### Multiplayer — CC/debuff/heal/cure relay to the real authoritative copy
- Found and fixed the same class of bug twice: mutating a target's `combat_node`/
  state directly only works when you're the machine authoritative for that target.
  Player-to-player heals/buffs were silently no-ops for anyone but the host; the same
  was true in the other direction for every debuff/snare/stun/fear/charm cast on a
  monster by a non-host player (monsters are server-authoritative).
- Added `apply_networked_heal`/`apply_networked_buff` (player-to-player) and
  `apply_networked_effect`/`apply_networked_fear`/`apply_networked_charm`/
  `apply_networked_disable` (player-to-monster) RPCs, mirroring the existing damage-
  relay pattern — retrofitted onto the pre-existing hardcoded Voidknight cases
  (Necrotic Grasp, Curse of Weakness, Plague Strike, Improved Disarm) too, not just
  new spells.
- Charm's control-window commands now relay too (applies locally first for instant
  UI feedback, then via a new RPC when the caster isn't the real authority) — closes
  the gap flagged earlier in this same session. `cmd_attack`'s target crosses the
  wire as a NodePath string, same reasoning as the owner param above.
- Also found and fixed while closing that out: a charmed monster's kill didn't credit
  the actual charm owner (would have wrongly credited the host); the `dot` effect
  type was missed in the original relay pass and still bypassed it; Shadow Aura's
  continuous nearby-enemy debuff tick had the same unrelayed mutation; and a related
  bug in `campfire.gd` — its warmth trigger runs identically on every peer and could
  apply the warmth buff to a remote player's puppet from a non-owning machine (fixed
  with an authority check, not a relay, since a player is always authoritative over
  themselves).

### UI — theme consistency, resizable windows, and several reported bugs
- Backpack: fixed a solid-vs-semitransparent inconsistency across windows (new shared
  `Global.window_bg_style()`, with a live player-adjustable transparency slider in
  Options), and fixed its item grid only filling about a quarter of the window
  (hardcoded 4-column grid regardless of window width).
- Every window (Abilities Book, Shop, Tracking, Corpse Loot, Character Sheet, Group
  Frame, Pet Frame/Gear, Target/Player Frame, Buff Bar, Cast Bar, the new Tradeskill
  window, the new Network widget, etc.) is now resizable by dragging its bottom-right
  corner, matching the backpack. Root cause of the reported Abilities window text
  truncation: skill/spell rows used fixed-size manual layout regardless of window
  size — fixed at the row level (autowrapping instead of clipping), not just made the
  window bigger around the same broken rows.
- Fixed the buff bar flagging "Aura of the Shadow" (and by extension every spell like
  it — a self-buff that debuffs *enemies*) as a red debuff on the player's own bar. It
  was keying off `effect_type` (the spell's own mechanical shape) instead of `target`
  (whether it was cast at an enemy) — also found ~20 other spells with unreliable
  `spell_type` data that would have caused the same false positive.
- Both town vendor NPCs (Aldric the Provisioner, the Apothecary) had no `combat_node`
  at all — showed as 0/0 HP wherever anything expected one. Now 100/100.

### 5 new mob models wired up
- `goblin` (base type), `rat` → Desert Scavenger, `spiderling` → Juvenile Spider (the
  latter two mapped by an exact description match against the zone's own mob-list
  sheet: "a rat" = "A Desert Scavenger"). `goblin_scout`/`skeleton` were already wired
  from a prior session — reverified, not re-done.
- Built a new visual pipeline for non-humanoid critters with a real but unrigged
  model (single static mesh, no skeleton/animations) — didn't exist before, since the
  only prior model-swapping path was for Mixamo-rigged humanoids.
- Orientation/scale for the two brand-new critter meshes hasn't been visually
  confirmed in-engine yet (file loading verified headless, not the same as seeing it)
  — flagged as the first thing to check if either looks wrong on sight.

### Misc
- New F11-toggled network diagnostics widget (draggable, persistent, EverQuest-style)
  — latency/packet-loss/bandwidth are real ENet statistics, not approximated,
  color-coded green/yellow/red at the user's specified thresholds.
- Night is a bit darker again (moon/ambient energy dialed back ~35-40% from an
  earlier "too dark" fix that had overshot into "too bright"); Shadowlight is more
  than 2x brighter and reaches its full stated 5m radius.
- Main menu: copyright line moved from under the title to the bottom of the screen
  and reworded to "Copyright 2026 Kuro Neko Sama Games"; the Load Character screen
  (previously a small box at a hardcoded pixel position left over from an older menu
  layout) is now a proper centered, styled, closeable window.
- New "Controls & Commands" panel in the Escape menu — a read-only new-player
  reference (keybinds + chat commands); remapping is a planned follow-up, not built
  yet.

## 2026-09-16/17 — Session 44: Spiritweaver rename, real cast bar, Phantasmal Echo pet, invisibility system, new races, skills fixes, vendor UI, tracking window, netcode phases 1-3

### Spiritcaller renamed to Spiritweaver
- Renamed across every live file — scripts, `player_spells.json`'s
  `class_level_requirements`, `class_restrictions.json`, `character_options.json`,
  `items.json`'s scroll class arrays, `player_skills.json`, `lumora.json`. User's
  reasoning: sounded too close to Gravecaller. Left the dead legacy 2D
  `Scripts/player.gd` untouched (not reachable from the live game).

### Real cast-time gameplay + cast bar
- `combatnode.gd` had unused `is_casting`/`current_cast_time`/`total_cast_time`/
  `start_spell_cast()`/`interrupt_spell()` scaffolding, and every spell in
  `player_spells.json` already had a `casting_time` field — none of it was wired
  up; `cast_spell()` resolved every spell instantly regardless. Now genuinely
  delays resolution until the cast bar fills (`player3d.gd`'s `_tick_spell_cast()`),
  interruptible by taking damage (`_check_spell_interrupt()`, using
  `interrupt_spell()`'s concentration-roll scaffolding for the first time) —
  applies to every caster class, not just one.
- Fixed a real bug in `interrupt_spell()` while wiring it up: it set
  `is_casting = false` unconditionally before checking success/failure, so even
  a successful concentration check would silently cancel the cast. Success now
  only logs and lets the cast continue.
- New `Scenes/cast_bar.tscn` / `Scripts/cast_bar.gd` — shows spell name, a
  filling bar, and time remaining, draggable and position-persisted like every
  other HUD frame. Yellow fill (was purple in an earlier pass, per feedback).
- Fixed a crash: if the cast's target (e.g. a pet) died/was freed during the
  cast, `_tick_spell_cast()` still passed the freed object into
  `_resolve_spell_cast()`'s typed `Node` parameter — now checked with
  `is_instance_valid()` first and passed as null instead.

### Phantasmal Echo — Spiritweaver's level-1 pet
- New `phantasmal_echo` spell (was previously unreachable/didn't exist —
  Spiritweaver had no level-1 pet at all) grants a pet that heals the group's
  lowest-HP member periodically and attacks at range via a real homing
  projectile (`Scripts/spectral_bolt.gd`) instead of melee — extends
  `pet_minion.gd` (Voidknight's pet) rather than duplicating its follow/nav/
  command plumbing.
- Wired the user's "Spirit Pet" model in, including a green heal-pulse VFX and
  full PBR texture set (normal/roughness/metallic), on top of the base
  albedo-only override every other Meshy model here uses.
- Fixed her rendering in the ground and facing backward — root cause was the
  mesh's own baked axis transform and off-base pivot, not the CharacterBody3D's
  own transform (an earlier attempt edited the wrong layer and made it worse —
  briefly went sideways before landing on the real fix: 180°-yaw + a vertical
  lift, matching every other Meshy model's convention).
- Both pets (skeleton + Echo) got a second, smaller nameplate line under their
  name reading `<OwnerName's risen minion>` / `<OwnerName's phantasmal echo>`.
  Fixed it initially overlapping her head once her real height was corrected.

### Pet behavior fixes (both pet types — shared pet_minion.gd)
- Pets no longer stutter/chase when the owner just turns in place: the
  "3m behind wherever you're facing" follow spot used to recompute every
  frame purely from facing direction, so spinning with zero actual movement
  swept that point in an arc and dragged the pet along with it. Now cached and
  only refreshed once the owner has moved >0.5m.
- Pets now hard back off to 3m minimum distance from their owner every frame
  (`_maintain_min_owner_distance()`), fixing them stuttering into/overlapping
  the player at close range.
- Fixed Assist mode attacking the instant a mob was targeted rather than
  waiting for the player to actually attack — was gated on `autoattack_enabled`
  alone, which is sticky across target changes; now requires the owner to have
  landed a real attack within the last 1.5s (`last_attack_time_ms`, a new
  field set at every real attack action).
- Fixed a crash-causing bug in `_target_alive()`: it assumed every target was
  a `Monster` (`target.State.DEAD`, a Monster-only enum) and crashed instantly
  if called on anything else — e.g. simply targeting your own pet to heal it
  while it happened to also be checked by the pet's own auto-engage logic.
  Now uses `combat_node.is_alive()` generically.
- Fixed the pet HP bar in the group window showing pink instead of the
  standard red used everywhere else.

### Invisibility / stealth / game-master nameplate system
- New `CombatNode` helpers: `is_currently_invisible()`, `has_see_invisible()`,
  `is_stealthed()`, `break_invisibility()` (called on any attack, melee or
  spell — attacking always reveals you).
- Wired the `invisibility` spell (Runecaster) for real — its data was broken
  (`target: "enemy"`, `spell_type: "detrimental"` despite being a self-buff per
  its own description), fixed to `target: "self"` / `"beneficial"`.
- New `TargetFrame.is_hidden_from_local_player()` / `nameplate_name()` —
  shared, per-viewer (not networked) logic used by both players and monsters:
  hides the 3D model and blocks click-targeting unless the viewer has
  see-invisible; shows a vague parenthesized name ("(a shadowy figure)", or a
  monster's own flavor description) when perceived but not yet identified.
  `[stealth]`/`<game master>` tags are wired into the same formatter, ready to
  display, but nothing sets them yet (no stealth skill mechanic exists; GM
  status is a new `is_game_master` save field with no in-game way to grant it
  yet).

### Vendor/session bug fixes
- Fixed autoattack spamming "You can't attack an ally" every single frame
  (not just every cooldown — `attack_cooldown` never advanced past the
  ally-check, so it retried next frame forever) whenever an ally (e.g. your
  own pet, mid-heal) was targeted while autoattack was still on from a
  previous fight.
- Fixed losing the mouse cursor after `/camp` or "Save and Exit" — neither
  reset `Input.mouse_mode` before switching to the main menu, so mouselook
  left it captured/hidden with no way to click anything.
- `/exit` and the pause menu's "Save and Exit" now route through the same
  15-second, damage-interruptible camp channel `/camp` already used, instead
  of instantly saving and leaving — closes an instant-escape-from-a-bad-fight
  exploit that existed on both of those paths.

### New player models: Elf Male/Female, Dark Elf Male/Female
- Wired all four into `CHARACTER_MODELS` (animations + texture overrides) and
  their character-creation portraits.
- Elf Female needed a per-model scale correction after her re-rig — the new
  export was at a different unit scale than every other model. Took three
  passes to land on the right value (an AABB-based math approach turned out
  unreliable for this skinned/rigged asset both times it was tried; the final
  value was back-solved from real in-game height comparisons against Guard
  Reyna instead). Also rebuilt her animation library when the re-rig replaced
  her source files, and again once a real "Sitting Idle" clip was added
  (previously fell back to reusing idle).

### Player skills system
- Confirmed `character_creation.gd` already correctly reads `class_skills`
  from `player_skills.json` for all 15 classes (a hardcoded Blademaster-only
  block found in `player3d.gd` turned out to be an unrelated dev/debug
  fallback, not the real path).
- Fixed the actual bug: `_tick_skill()` required a skill to already exist in
  `skill_levels` to ever level up, so a class could never gain a skill outside
  its starting set (e.g. picking up a weapon type your class didn't start
  with stayed stuck at 0 forever). Any recognized skill now starts accruing
  from 0 on first real use.
- Fixed the skill cap being hardcoded to 252 in two places (`player3d.gd`,
  `abilities_book.gd`) instead of reading the real `skill_max` (275) from
  `player_skills.json`.
- Fixed a latent bug in the skill-description loader: it iterated every
  top-level key in `player_skills.json` including `class_skills`, silently
  stuffing entries like `_skill_data["Blademaster"] = {...}` into what's
  supposed to be a flat skill-name-to-description map.
- Added the missing `BLOCK` skill tick (HIT/PARRY/DODGE/RIPOSTE all already
  ticked their matching skill; BLOCK never did).
- Magic school skills (abjuration/evocation/etc.) explicitly deferred — audited
  `player_spells.json` and found 516 of 559 spells still tagged with the
  placeholder `spell_school: "physical"`, with no real mapping to
  `player_skills.json`'s D&D-style school list. User will fix the spell data
  directly. Crafting skills also deferred — no crafting system exists yet.

### Light spells
- Fixed Shadowlight (Voidknight) — was only ever a buff + log message
  ("a dim violet light kindles..."), never an actual light. Now creates a
  real dim violet `OmniLight3D`, same toggle-off-the-active-effect pattern
  `deathly_visage`'s existing night-vision light already used.
- Audited every spell in `player_spells.json` for anything light-related
  (`effect_type: "light"` is an existing, reliable tag) — Shadowlight and
  `deathly_visage` are the only two that exist; no other class has a light
  spell in the data despite the assumption most would. User will add more as
  part of an upcoming spell/skill data pass.

### Vendor window: quantity + usable-only filter
- Buy rows now have a quantity spinner (1-99); sell rows get one too, but
  only when a stack has more than 1 (a lone sword doesn't need a picker).
  `Inventory.add_item()` already supported a quantity; added a matching
  `Inventory.consume_amount()` for selling N in one operation instead of N
  separate `consume_one()` calls (`consume_one()` now just calls it with 1).
- New "Only show items I can use" checkbox filters the For Sale list against
  each item's own `class`/`race` restriction arrays (`items.json`) — reuses
  data every scroll and piece of equipment already carries, no new
  eligibility system.

### Tracking window (T key)
- New `Scenes/tracking_window.tscn` / `Scripts/tracking_window.gd` — granted
  by race (Elf, Half-Elf) or class (Woodstalker, Wildspeaker, Troubadour) via
  `has_tracking_skill()`, not a learned spell/skill. Draggable, resizable,
  position/size persisted (`WindowPosition`, same pattern as the character
  sheet), lists every monster/guard/vendor within 50m (not just threats —
  tracking is about knowing what's nearby) sorted by distance, refreshing
  twice a second, con-colored by level using the exact same scale targeting
  uses (`TargetFrame.con_color()`, made static and reused rather than
  duplicated). Correctly hides monsters the player can't perceive via the invisibility
  system above.

### Netcode — Phase 1: fix "wrong player" bugs
- Every HUD script that grabbed `get_nodes_in_group("player")[0]` — a real
  crash/wrong-data risk with 2+ real players, confirmed as the single biggest
  documented gap — now finds "my own local player" via a new
  `TargetFrame.local_player()` helper: `player_frame`, `target_frame`,
  `action_bar`, `stance_bar`, `buff_bar`, `pet_frame`, `group_frame`,
  `game_log_window`, `slot_button`, the new `cast_bar`, plus `global.gd`'s
  save-position write and `monster3d.gd`'s XP-award (which was calling
  `.on_level_up()` on whatever node happened to be at index 0 — a real crash
  risk on a puppet's missing `combat_node`). Left the confirmed-dead legacy 2D
  `chat_window.gd`/`npc.gd`/`lumora_outskirts.gd` untouched.

### Netcode — Phase 2: real player combat sync
- Every player (puppets included) now gets a real `combat_node` at `_ready()`
  — previously only the locally-authoritative player ever had one, so a
  remote player's `combat_node` was always null (the actual cause of empty
  HP/MP bars for group members, and a class of Nil-property crashes).
- Added `current_hp`/`max_hp`/`current_mana`/`max_mana`/`level` to
  `player3d.tscn`'s `SceneReplicationConfig`, replicating a remote player's
  real stats the same way `player_name`/`player_race` already did. A puppet's
  copy is never locally simulated — it's a pure receiver for whatever the
  authoritative owner replicates out.
- Known limitation, not yet addressed: healing/buffing another real player
  still only updates your own local view, not theirs — needs its own RPC per
  spell type, deliberately not attempted in this pass.

### Netcode — Phase 3: server-authoritative monsters + pets, real damage sync
- `monster3d.gd`: non-authoritative peers (every client but the host, in
  multiplayer — never triggered in single-player) now just mirror replicated
  position/rotation/animation/HP/state instead of running independent
  AI/combat simulation. New `anim_state` field replicates idle/walk/run/
  attack/death the same way player puppets already mirror `anim_state`.
- `monster_template.tscn` got a `MultiplayerSynchronizer` (position, rotation,
  `anim_state`, `current_state`, `CombatNode`'s `current_hp`/`max_hp`) —
  applies automatically to every instance, including the zone's hand-placed
  monsters.
- `mob_spawner3d.gd` rewritten: spawn *decisions* are now server-only; actual
  node creation goes through a real `MultiplayerSpawner` (mirrors
  `multiplayer_player_spawner.gd`'s existing pattern for players) instead of
  each client independently instantiating its own copy.
- Pets networked: `player3d.tscn` gained a `PetSpawner`/`PetContainer` pair
  (a `MultiplayerSpawner`, spawn_function `_build_pet()`) so `_summon_pet()`
  spawns a replicated copy for every peer instead of a local-only node — a
  remote player's pet was previously invisible to everyone else. Authority
  is set to the OWNER's own peer id (not always the server, unlike monsters
  — a pet belongs to a specific player), propagated automatically since
  `PetSpawner`/`PetContainer` are static children of `player3d.tscn` and
  inherit whatever authority that player instance already has. Both pet
  scenes got `top_level = true` on their root (so they move independently in
  world space instead of inheriting the owner's transform as a child) and a
  `MultiplayerSynchronizer` (position, rotation, `anim_state`, `pet_name`,
  `CombatNode`'s `current_hp`/`max_hp`). HUD frame / save data / the
  "You summon..." message stay owner-only (`is_multiplayer_authority()`);
  everyone else just sees the pet itself, fighting and healing.
- Real damage sync: a client-authoritative-damage / server-authoritative-
  application split, not a full server-side recompute — the attacking
  player's own client still rolls the real hit/miss/damage locally (using
  its own accurate stats, unchanged), then relays the resulting number to
  the monster's real (server-side) `combat_node` via a new
  `apply_networked_damage(amount, attacker_peer_id)` RPC whenever the target
  isn't already locally authoritative. Replicating a full combat stat kit
  (strength/weapon_skill/gear bonuses/etc — Phase 2 only replicated the
  "bar" values) just to let the server redo the roll itself would be a much
  bigger change; trusting the attacker's reported number is an accepted
  trade-off for a private LAN co-op game. Wired into both melee call sites
  (`attack_current_target()`, `perform_melee_attack()`), both spell-damage
  branches (`enemy`, `cone`), and both pet attack paths (`pet_minion.gd`'s
  melee, `phantasmal_echo_pet.gd`'s bolt impact).
- Kill/XP attribution fixed to actually credit the real killer: `die()`
  gained a `credited_peer_id` parameter — `apply_networked_damage()` passes
  the reporting attacker's peer id when it's what brings HP to 0, instead of
  always crediting `TargetFrame.local_player()` (which, called from the
  server on a non-host client's kill, would have wrongly credited the host
  own character). Since `Global.player_data` is only ever the current
  process's own save data, crediting a different peer routes through a new
  `player3d.gd` method `grant_xp(amount)` — called directly when the
  credited player is the local machine's own, or via a new
  `receive_kill_credit(xp_gain)` RPC targeted at that specific peer
  otherwise.
- Bonus bugs found and fixed while wiring the relay above: both the "enemy"
  and "cone" spell-damage branches in `_resolve_spell_cast()` never actually
  called `.die()` on a killing blow at all — no loot roll, no XP, no
  despawn timer, for any kill made via single-target or AOE spell damage,
  regardless of multiplayer. Pre-existing, unrelated to this session's
  networking work, just found while touching the same code.
- Deliberately NOT attempted: full server-side recomputation of hit/miss/
  damage (would need the stat-replication expansion noted above); a real
  player healing/buffing another real player across the network (still only
  updates the caster's own local view — noted as a gap in Phase 2 above).

---

## 2026-09-16 — Session 43: New race/mob/vendor models, attack/cast animation timing fixes

### Attack animation timing (player3d.gd, monster3d.gd)
- Fixed a real cutoff bug: `attack_current_target()`/`perform_melee_attack()` used
  to hold the swing animation for a hardcoded 0.3s before letting movement-based
  animations take back over — but real swing clips run ~2.3-3.8s, so every attack
  was visibly chopped off after a fraction of its length. Both now hold for the
  real clip length instead (`_attack_animation_duration()`).
- Since that hold is now longer than the 1.0s attack cooldown, a new swing can
  legitimately start before the previous swing's hold-timer finishes — added an
  `_attack_generation` counter so an earlier swing's delayed timer can't wrongly
  cut a newer swing's animation short.
- Per user request ("make sure the mobs don't have a much faster attack than the
  player"): `attack_cooldown` itself is now set to the real swing length each
  attack too (previously a flat 1.0s), and `monster3d.gd`'s `perform_attack()`
  got the same treatment (previously a flat 1.5s regardless of animation) so
  humanoid mobs' attack rate stays in line with the player's instead of
  outpacing it.

### New player race models (Half-Elf Female/Male, Troll Female; Human Female rebuilt)
- Built animation libraries + wired into `player3d.gd`'s `CHARACTER_MODELS` for
  Half-Elf Female, Half-Elf Male, and Troll Female — same pipeline as the
  existing Human Female/Male, Troll Male entries (see `change_list.txt`'s "how
  to rig a new character model" notes).
- Human Female's model/rig was replaced by the user (old one had animation
  issues) — rebuilt `female_animations.res` from the new source clips and
  repointed her texture override at the new `_v2_` texture file.
- Half-Elf Male was added in two passes: shipped initially without
  idle/jump/sit/attack/death (only running/walking/mesh/textures), so left off
  `CHARACTER_MODELS` for a session rather than wired in half-broken; wired in
  properly once the user added the rest same day.

### Casting animations for all player classes
- Per user request: added `cast_beneficial` and `cast_detrimental` animation
  clips to all 6 dedicated race/sex models, built from each model's
  previously-unused Magic Spell Casting/"-buff(s)"/Magic Attack source clips.
- `cast_spell()` now plays the right one automatically — `_is_beneficial_cast()`
  classifies by the spell's target (self/group = beneficial; enemy/pbaoe/chain/
  cone/line = detrimental; corpse/none are genuinely mixed in
  `player_spells.json`, so it falls back to whether the spell deals damage).
- The 8 races still without a dedicated model (elf, dwarf, gnome, halfling,
  ogre, dark_elf, half_orc, lizardkin) have no cast clips yet and silently keep
  playing idle/walk through a cast, same as before — `models/player/`'s
  default model has no leftover raw casting downloads to build from.

### Guard: new model + sprint-to-combat
- Swapped the guard's model/animations from the old `models/guard/` placeholder
  over to the user's new dedicated `models/Lumora Guardsman/` set (also added a
  texture override — Meshy exports never carry their real texture through to
  Godot reliably).
- Per user request: guards now play a "run" animation and move at 2x speed
  (matching `monster3d.gd`'s existing chase-speed multiplier) while actively
  closing distance on an engage target — patrol/idle speed is unchanged.

### Dedicated mob models: goblin_scout, goblin_warrior, skeleton
- Built animation libraries and wired `monster3d.gd`'s `HUMANOID_MOB_TYPES`/new
  `MOB_MODELS` dict for the user's new `models/mobs/desert goblin scout/`,
  `models/mobs/desert goblin/` (→ `goblin_warrior`), and `models/mobs/fallen
  scout skeleton/` (→ `skeleton`) models — same per-species pattern as the
  player's `CHARACTER_MODELS`.
- Bonus fix: `goblin_scout`/`goblin_warrior` weren't in `HUMANOID_MOB_TYPES` at
  all before this — a pre-existing gap where both (live in the goblin camps in
  `lumora_outskirts_spawns.json`) rendered as the plain placeholder box even
  before any of today's model work.
- Base `goblin` and `bandit` still have no dedicated model and keep using the
  original shared player model, unchanged.

### Skeleton Pet (Voidknight pet)
- Wired the user's new `models/Skeleton Pet/` model into `pet_minion.gd`
  (previously reused the generic shared humanoid model) with its own
  idle/walk/attack/death library and a texture override.

### New vendor: the Apothecary (female vendor)
- `vendor_npc.gd` now builds its visual dynamically from a `VENDOR_MODELS`
  dict keyed by a new `vendor_model_key` export (same pattern as
  `CHARACTER_MODELS`/`MOB_MODELS`) instead of a single static model baked into
  `vendor_npc.tscn` — needed now that a second vendor has a different visual.
- Aldric now uses the user's new dedicated "male vendor" (Blacksmith-flavored)
  model instead of the old shared player model.
- Added a second vendor, "the Apothecary" (`vendor_model_key="female"`), a few
  meters from Aldric in `lumora_outskirts3d.tscn`, using the user's new "female
  vendor" (Apothecary-flavored) model. Placeholder stock only
  (`lumora_apothecary` in `vendor_shop.json` — trail rations/water, since no
  potion/elixir items exist in `items.json` yet) — user said they'll customize
