## 2026-09-14 — Session 42: Lumora monster roster, /follow, food auto-consume, position save, minor fixes

### Monster roster reconciled against `Zone_Spawn_Sheet.xlsx` (`Data/monsters.json`, `Data/lumora_outskirts_spawns.json`)

User supplied a spreadsheet (3 tabs: LumoraOutskirts, DustwindPlateaus,
AshfallDunes — the latter two are the *next two zones*, not yet built, so
their content is reference-only for now) listing the intended monster
roster per zone. Read it via raw zip/XML parsing (`zipfile` + `xml.etree`
against `xl/sharedStrings.xml` and `xl/worksheets/sheetN.xml`) since neither
`openpyxl` nor pandas's Excel engine is installed in this environment.

- Added 4 new monster types to match the sheet: `spiderling` ("a spiderling
  swarmer", Ensnaring Web), `goblin_scout` ("a desert goblin scout", fast/
  light), `goblin_warrior` ("a desert goblin warrior", tougher pack leader),
  `ghost` ("a tormented spirit", undead).
- `ghost` replaces `mirage_phantom` as the graveyard's rare elite spawn in
  `lumora_outskirts_spawns.json` — the sheet's AshfallDunes tab confirmed
  `mirage_phantom` is actually a level 10-12 Ashfall mob (way too strong for
  a level 1-4 starter zone); the sheet's "Tormented Spirit" is clearly the
  intended replacement for that graveyard-elite role.
- Rezoned (via `monsters.json`'s `zone` field only — cosmetic, this field
  isn't otherwise consumed for spawn placement, confirmed via grep) several
  mobs whose "Old Name" in the sheet's DustwindPlateaus/AshfallDunes tabs
  matched a current key here, meaning they belong to those later zones, not
  Lumora: `dustwalker`, `dustwalker_scout`, `dustwalker_ravager`,
  `dustwalker_warlord`, `wild_dog`, `khepri_beetle`, `sand_stinger`,
  `sandshifter`, `bonegnawer`, `sunmaddened_wanderer` → `dustwind_plateaus`
  or `ashfall_dunes` as appropriate. None of these were actually referenced
  in the live spawn table, so this was pure data hygiene, zero gameplay risk.
- Fixed a leftover typo the user hadn't caught yet: `skeleton`'s description
  was still "a fallen scount skeleton" — fixed to "a fallen scout skeleton"
  to match the sheet's "A Fallen Scout Skeleton". Every other Lumora-zoned
  monster's description already matched the sheet (the user had updated them
  correctly).
- Added `special_ability` text (shown in the appraisal system,
  `player3d.gd`'s `try_appraise_target()`) where the sheet specified one:
  Poison Bite (spider, snake), Life Tap (bat), Acid Attack (slime),
  Ensnaring Web (spiderling). Also recategorized `slime` from `"animal"` to
  `"elemental"` and gave it `weaknesses: ["cold", "fire"]`, matching the
  sheet's explicit Type column and flavor text.
- **All 13 LumoraOutskirts-tab monsters are now live** in
  `lumora_outskirts_spawns.json`: added `spiderling` to the spider den,
  `goblin_scout`/`goblin_warrior` to the goblin camps (alongside base
  `goblin`, at lower/higher spawn chance respectively — scout common,
  warrior rare pack leader).
- Left `thornback_lizard` and `mummy` zoned `lumora_outskirts` unchanged —
  neither appears anywhere across all 3 of the sheet's tabs, flagged to the
  user rather than guessed at. `mummy` is still live in the mausoleum spawn.
- `lumora_guard` and `lumora_outrider` (Lumora-faction NPCs, not hostile
  wildlife) were left alone — out of scope for a hostile-mob spawn sheet.

### `/follow` command (`player3d.gd`, `game_log_window.gd`)

`/follow [name]` — follow an NPC or another player around the zone (e.g.
`/follow Sergeant Bryn` to tag along on his patrol route). See the Chat
Commands reference at the top of this file for full behavior. Implementation:

- `try_follow(query)` resolves a target: empty query uses `current_target`
  (Tab/left-click selection); otherwise searches groups `npc_guard`,
  `npc_vendor`, `monsters`, and `player` by case-insensitive substring match
  on display name (`TargetFrame.display_name()`, with a `player_name`
  check first for future multiplayer), nearest match wins. Calling it again
  on whoever you're already following toggles follow off.
- `_handle_follow_movement()` steers toward the target using
  `NavigationServer3D.query_path()` directly — the same self-managed
  approach `guard_npc.gd`'s patrol system uses (Session 39/41), not
  `NavigationAgent3D`, for the same reason: `NavigationAgent3D`'s cached
  path was found to go stale over long distances / after the target moves
  out from under it. Repaths every 0.75s (`FOLLOW_REPATH_INTERVAL`) rather
  than every frame, drops waypoints as it passes within 1.5m of them
  (`FOLLOW_WAYPOINT_EPSILON`), stops 3m short of the target
  (`FOLLOW_STOP_DISTANCE`) instead of walking into them, and turns via
  `look_at()` toward the next waypoint (Y-flattened so it doesn't pitch).
- Wired into `handle_movement()`: any manual movement input (forward,
  backward, strafe, turn, jump) cancels follow immediately and hands
  control back, same as pressing backward already cancels Autorun.
- Also invalidates cleanly if the target despawns mid-follow
  (`is_instance_valid()` check each frame).

### Chat commands are now Linux-style prefix-matched (`game_log_window.gd`)

`_resolve_command()` added: given a typed command, matches it against a
`COMMANDS` list of canonical names — exact match wins immediately, else any
*unambiguous* prefix resolves (e.g. `/fol` → `/follow`), else it prints
"Ambiguous command" with the candidate list, or "Unknown command" if nothing
matches. Renamed the old literal `/loc` command to `/location` as the
canonical name (`/loc` still works, it's just an abbreviation now rather
than the literal command) — the clearest example of the new behavior, and
what prompted doing this generically for every command instead of one-off.

### Food/water auto-consume + flavor text (`player3d.gd`, `Scripts/inventory_autoload.gd`, `Data/food_drink_flavor.json`)

- `Inventory.find_first_by_restores(restores)` — new helper, searches basic
  inventory slots then bags for the first item whose def has a matching
  `restores` ("satiety"/"thirst"), returning the same
  `(slot_type, slot_index, bag_slot, item_index)` addressing
  `Inventory.consume_one()` already expects.
- `player3d.gd`'s `update_vitals_decay()`: the moment satiety/thirst ticks
  below 25 (the existing "low" warning threshold), `_try_auto_consume()`
  now fires first — eats/drinks a matching item if one's carried, which
  naturally suppresses the low/empty warning messages that follow in the
  same tick since the vital is back up. No item carried → same warnings as
  before, unchanged.
- New `Data/food_drink_flavor.json` (`eat`/`drink` line pools, `%s` for the
  item name), loaded via the existing `NPCFlavorText` helper class (same
  reusable picker `player_death_flavor.json`/`guard_flavor_text.json` use).
  `consume_food_or_drink()` (used by both manual Eat/Drink and the new
  auto-consume path) now prints a random line from it, e.g. "You consume
  Iron Rations. Yumm! That was tasty!" / "You quench your thirst with A
  Flask of Water. Ahhh! That hit the spot." — replaces the old plain
  "You eat X. (Hunger: N/100)" message.

### Player position saved/restored across sessions (`Global.gd`, `player3d.gd`)

- `Global.save_player_data_to_file()` now captures the live player's
  `global_position` into `player_data["last_position"]` right before
  writing — done centrally here rather than patching every one of the
  several call sites (pause menu Save/Save & Exit, bind point setup, etc.)
  individually, so it can never be forgotten at a new call site later.
- `player3d.gd`'s new `_restore_last_position()` runs in `_ready()` (after
  `load_player_data_from_global()`, before `_ensure_bind_point()`): sets
  `global_position` from `player_data["last_position"]` if present. No-op
  for a brand-new character (nothing saved yet), which just keeps the
  zone's default spawn transform. Distinct from `bind_point`
  (death-respawn location, set once on first spawn and otherwise only
  changed by a future bind spell/NPC) — logging out and dying restore to
  different places, same as EQ.

### Minor fixes

- **Aldric the Provisioner (and any vendor) now opens his shop when
  hailed** (`player3d.gd`'s `try_hail_nearby_npc()`) — `respond_to_hail()`
  used to just print a greeting line for vendors, so hailing did nothing
  useful; you still had to right-click precisely on the vendor's 3D model
  to actually shop. Hailing a `VendorNPC` now calls `_open_shop()` directly
  (which also handles the greeting), same as before for guards.
- **Right-click cursor no longer snaps to screen center**
  (`camera_controller.gd`) — every right-click (even a quick click to loot/
  shop, not just a head-turn drag) was switching `Input.mouse_mode` to
  `MOUSE_MODE_CAPTURED` for the "hold right-click to head-turn" feature.
  `CAPTURED` locks the OS cursor to the window center every frame to report
  unlimited relative motion; releasing it left the literal OS cursor
  sitting at that center point. Head-turn is capped to ±90°/±45° and never
  needed unlimited motion, so switched to `MOUSE_MODE_HIDDEN`, which
  reports real relative motion without warping the cursor position.
- **Fixed a glowing horizon seam at night** (`day_night_cycle.gd`) —
  `_apply_lighting()` animated the sky dome's `sky_top_color` and
  `sky_horizon_color` between day/night values every tick, but never
  touched `ground_horizon_color` (the lower half of the sky dome), which
  sat at its bright default around the clock. Invisible by day, but at
  night the sky above went dark while the ground half of the horizon
  stayed lit, producing a bright horizontal line right at the horizon in
  every outdoor zone at night. Fix: mirror `ground_horizon_color` to the
  same computed `sky_horizon_color` every update — verified via a headless
  test forcing 2 AM game time, confirming both values match exactly.

## 2026-09-14 — Session 41: Guard patrol — local obstacle steering, faster aggro scan

Reported after further testing: Bryn got stuck again, this time at the
wagon crash site (unrelated geometry to the gate fixed in Session 39 —
confirms the issue isn't one bad spot, it's that global pathfinding alone
doesn't reliably route around every piece of debris/prop collision on a
long cross-map route); and separately, he "walked through a group of
enemies and only engaged the one he ran into directly."

### Local obstacle steering (`guard_npc.gd`)

- New `_steer_around_obstacles(direction)`, called from `_move_toward()`
  right before committing to a movement direction: casts a short forward
  ray (`OBSTACLE_CHECK_DISTANCE`, 1.5m) along the intended heading; if
  clear, uses it unchanged; if something's directly ahead, tries a series
  of alternate headings (`OBSTACLE_AVOID_ANGLES_DEG`: ±30°, ±60°, ±90°,
  widening outward) and walks the first one that's actually clear instead.
  A real-time local sidestep layered on top of the global path-following
  system rewritten last session — rather than continuing to hand-fix
  individual navmesh trouble spots one at a time as they turn up (the gate,
  now the wagon crash site, potentially more elsewhere on a ~4000-unit
  route), this handles "something's in the way right now" generically,
  independent of whether the baked navmesh accounts for that particular
  piece of geometry. The existing stuck-detector (Session 39) remains the
  fallback for anything this can't steer around.
- Verified headlessly: a direction with nothing ahead passes through
  unchanged; a real `StaticBody3D` wall placed directly in front produces a
  genuinely different, clear heading instead of driving straight into it.

### Faster aggro scan (`guard_npc.gd`)

- `SCAN_INTERVAL` was `1.0` — at ~3 m/s and a 3m `PATROL_ENGAGE_RANGE`, a
  patrolling guard could cover nearly its entire engage range in the gap
  between two consecutive scans, meaning most of a cluster of monsters
  would simply never be "in range" at the exact instant a check ran. Dropped
  to `0.2` (caps travel between scans to ~0.6m, reliably catching anything
  the engage range actually covers). Stationary guards are unaffected
  beyond reacting slightly sooner.
- Verified headlessly: a cluster of three monsters placed ahead of a moving
  guard's path is now correctly noticed and engaged, where before only
  whichever one he happened to be standing next to at a scan tick would be.

### Combined regression check

Ran the full patrol loop for 2 minutes straight after both changes: steady,
uninterrupted progress the entire time (~15.5 units every 5s, matching his
move speed) covering 343 units total with zero stalls — well past where he
previously got stuck, and no jitter/instability introduced by the new
per-frame steering raycasts.

---

## 2026-09-14 — Session 40: Ambient wind loop for Lumora Outskirts

Requested while the guard-patrol fix was being tested: a downloaded wind
sound as background ambiance for the zone.

### `Scenes/lumora_outskirts3d.tscn` — new AmbientWind node

- No zone-level ambient/background audio system existed at all —
  `global_background_music.gd` only plays in the main menu/character
  creation screens (`allowed_scenes` explicitly excludes every gameplay
  zone), and nothing else in the project loops non-positional audio for an
  entire level (`torch.tscn`/`campfire.tscn` are `AudioStreamPlayer3D`,
  positional, tied to a specific prop).
- `Assets/wind woosh loop.ogg` (copied in from the user's Downloads,
  keeping the original descriptive filename per the project's existing
  convention). Loop enabled via the import settings (`loop=true` in the
  generated `.ogg.import`, same mechanism `Torch Loop.ogg`'s own looping
  crackle already uses) rather than any script-side handling.
- New `AmbientWind` (`AudioStreamPlayer`, non-positional so it plays evenly
  regardless of where the player is in the zone) node, `autoplay = true`,
  `bus = "SFX"` (matches campfire/torch's own classification as
  environmental audio, so it responds to the same Options-menu SFX volume
  slider), `volume_db = -10.0` to sit as a background layer rather than
  compete with combat/UI sound.
- Verified headlessly: loading the real zone scene, the node is present,
  its stream has `loop == true`, it's on the `SFX` bus, and it's actually
  `playing` immediately (autoplay fired correctly).

---

## 2026-09-14 — Session 39: Guard Bryn's patrol stuck at the gate — NavigationAgent3D going stale over long distance

Reported immediately after Session 38, with a screenshot: Bryn walked
~20m out of the town gate and stopped dead next to a pillar, never resuming.

### Three attempts, in order, before landing on the real fix

1. **First hypothesis (wrong): another instance of the vertical navmesh/
   floor-height bug.** Confirmed via a headless test that
   `nav_agent.get_next_path_position()` was returning a point ~0.72m away
   with the entire difference in Y and zero in X/Z — looked exactly like
   the bug already fixed in `monster3d.gd`, and this file's own
   `path_desired_distance`/`target_desired_distance` were still the
   original, un-fixed `0.5`. Bumped to `1.5` to match. This DID get him
   moving (confirmed: ~17.5 units in 6s) — but only past the very first
   corner. Longer-duration testing showed he stopped again a few meters
   later, and a forward raycast in his direction of travel hit a real wall
   with `normal: (0,0,1)` — `1.5` was loose enough that the agent considered
   itself "arrived" at corners near the gate's tight passage and walked
   straight through a wall corner instead of hugging around it.
2. **Second attempt: a stuck-detector, position-based.** Tightened
   `path_desired_distance`/`target_desired_distance` back down to `0.75`
   (enough margin over the measured 0.72m gap without being as loose as
   1.5) and added logic to detect "hasn't moved in 2s" and snap the Y
   coordinate to the navmesh surface. Extended testing showed this WASN'T
   enough either — he kept re-getting stuck at nearly the identical XZ
   spot, orbiting a few meters for 75+ seconds straight.
3. **Root cause, finally isolated**: a fresh `NavigationServer3D.query_path()`
   called directly from his exact stuck position, every time, consistently
   returned short, sane, obviously-correct corners — but
   `nav_agent.get_next_path_position()` kept offering a corner that led
   right back to the same spot. `NavigationAgent3D`'s own internal path had
   gone stale relative to where he actually was (most likely compounded by
   the earlier stuck-detector attempts externally repositioning him without
   the agent noticing) — the underlying "trust the agent to track its own
   progress along a path this long, then let it recompute on its own
   schedule" approach was fragile regardless.

### The actual fix (`guard_npc.gd`)

Rewrote `_move_toward()` to manage its own corner list
(`_current_path: Array[Vector3]`) via direct
`NavigationServer3D.query_path()` calls instead of relying on
`NavigationAgent3D.target_position`/`get_next_path_position()` at all — the
agent node stays in the scene (still configured, harmless) but is no
longer used for path-following decisions. The path is recomputed
(`_recompute_path()`) whenever it's empty, the target has moved
significantly, or the stuck-detector requests one; corner advancement
(`CORNER_ARRIVAL`, 0.75) is tracked entirely in guard-owned state, so an
externally-moved position (combat interrupting patrol, the stuck-detector,
anything else) can never leave a dangling reference to the agent's own
notion of "where am I on this path." The stuck-detector now just clears
`_current_path` to force a fresh query rather than trying to manually
nudge a position — simpler, and doesn't need to guess how far to move.
Also clears `_current_path` explicitly whenever combat ends and a
patrolling guard returns to `PATROL`, since fighting can move a guard well
off whatever path was cached before the fight started.

Verified headlessly: Bryn now covers real, continuous ground — 308 units
over 100 seconds with steady, unbroken progress the whole way, cleanly
through the exact gate passage that stopped him before. Re-verified combat
interrupting and resuming patrol still works correctly (engage on a nearby
monster, kill it, patrol resumes and moves normally afterward), and
stationary guards (Corwin/Reyna) are unaffected.

---

## 2026-09-14 — Session 38: Third guard patrols a full loop, all guards banter periodically

Requested while the right-click fix was being tested: a third guard that
loops through six 3D markers the user placed (Town Gate → Dock → South
Zone → Traveling Merchant → Signpost → Archery Targets → back to Town
Gate), attacking any monster within 3m along the way; and — using a new
"banter" section the user added to guard_flavor_text.json — all guards
occasionally say one of those lines to themselves when not in combat,
roughly every 7 minutes.

### Patrol capability (`guard_npc.gd`)

- Guards were stationary-only: `GuardState { IDLE, ENGAGE }`, engaging
  anything within `ENGAGE_RANGE` (4m) of a fixed `home_position`. Added a
  third state, `PATROL`, and a new `@export var patrol_waypoints:
  Array[NodePath] = []` — empty (the default) keeps a guard exactly as
  before; setting it makes that guard walk the given nodes in order,
  looping forever, via the same `_move_toward()` helper `ENGAGE` already
  used to close distance on a target.
- Since a patrolling guard has no fixed post, `_scan_for_targets()` now uses
  a new `PATROL_ENGAGE_RANGE` (3m, from the guard's CURRENT position) instead
  of `ENGAGE_RANGE`/`home_position` for patrol-configured guards; stationary
  guards are unaffected. Leashing during combat was reworked the same way —
  a new `_engage_origin` (set to wherever the guard was standing the moment
  it entered `ENGAGE`) replaces the old fixed-`home_position` leash check,
  which unifies stationary and patrolling guards under one mechanism instead
  of needing two (for a stationary guard, `_engage_origin` and
  `home_position` are practically the same thing anyway). A new
  `_default_state` (IDLE for stationary, PATROL for patrolling) is what
  combat returns to once a fight ends, instead of hardcoding `IDLE`.
- **Bug fix, found while testing**: a patrolling guard didn't move at all —
  confirmed headlessly that `NavigationAgent3D.get_next_path_position()`
  was returning a point ~0.72m away with the ENTIRE difference in Y and
  ZERO in X/Z. This is the exact same bug fixed in `monster3d.gd` earlier
  this session (the baked navmesh surface sits a bit above the true
  collision floor, so a fresh path's first waypoint is nearly a pure
  vertical correction) — `monster3d.gd`'s `nav_agent.path_desired_distance`/
  `target_desired_distance` were bumped from 0.5 to 1.5 back then, but that
  fix was never applied to `guard_npc.gd`, which nothing had exercised over
  a long enough distance to expose until this patrol route. Applied the
  same fix here.
- New third guard, "Guard Bryn," added to `lumora_outskirts3d.tscn` at the
  Town Gate marker with `patrol_waypoints` pointing at all six of the
  user's placed `Marker3D` nodes in the requested order.
- Verified headlessly, post-fix: Bryn covers real ground now (~17.5 units
  over 6s toward the first leg, matching guard move speed), still correctly
  engages a monster placed within 3m while patrolling, and the existing
  stationary guards (Corwin/Reyna) are unaffected — still engage normally
  from their fixed posts.

### Periodic banter (`guard_npc.gd`)

- Every guard (all three) now has a randomized `_banter_interval` (5-9 min,
  averaging ~7 — deliberately not a flat 7 minutes so multiple guards don't
  end up banter-ing in lockstep) that, whenever NOT in `ENGAGE`, counts up
  and fires a random line from the flavor file's new `"banter"` array via
  the existing `_say_flavor()`/`say()` path — same hearing-range gate
  (`HEAR_RANGE`, 5m) as every other guard line, so it's silent unless the
  player's actually close enough to hear it. The timer resets (not just
  pauses) on entering combat, so a fresh interval starts once a fight ends
  rather than potentially firing mid-swing.
- Verified headlessly: forcing a guard's banter timer past its interval
  (with the player in hearing range) correctly logged a real random line
  from the new banter pool.

## 2026-09-14 — Session 37: Right-click still didn't work — real cause was a mouse-mode race, not targeting

Reported after Session 36's fixes: "right clicking him still does nothing.
hailing him and targeting him work." Since hail/target (both range-based,
untouched by the Session 35 raycast rework) worked fine, this pointed
straight back at the raycast path itself.

### Bug: `Input.mouse_mode` was always wrong by the time the gate checked it

`_try_open_shop_or_loot()` gated the raycast on `Input.mouse_mode ==
MOUSE_MODE_VISIBLE`. But `camera_controller.gd`'s `_input()` handles the
*same* right-click press first — every node's `_input()` runs before
`_unhandled_input()`, where `_try_open_shop_or_loot()` lives — for its own
unrelated "hold right-click to head-turn the camera" feature, and
immediately calls `Input.set_mouse_mode(MOUSE_MODE_CAPTURED)` the instant
right-click is pressed, regardless of whether mouselook is even on. By the
time `_try_open_shop_or_loot()` ran, `Input.mouse_mode` had already flipped
to `CAPTURED` for *every* right-click, so the `MOUSE_MODE_VISIBLE` check was
never true — the raycast never fired, and every right-click silently fell
through to `_try_loot_corpse()`. This is why it "did nothing" against the
vendor specifically: with no corpse nearby, that fallback had nothing to do
either.
- Fixed by gating on `Global.mouselook_enabled` instead — the actual
  persistent user toggle (F12), which the head-turn feature's transient
  capture never touches (`camera_controller.gd`'s own head-turn code only
  captures the mouse "if not Global.mouselook_enabled" in the first place,
  so this flag correctly reflects normal cursor-visible play regardless of
  what a same-frame right-click did to `Input.mouse_mode` a moment earlier).
- Verified headlessly: calling the gating logic with `Input.mouse_mode`
  forced to `CAPTURED` (reproducing the exact same-frame ordering) but
  `Global.mouselook_enabled` left `false` still correctly finds the vendor
  via the raycast; with `Global.mouselook_enabled` genuinely `true`, it
  still correctly skips. (The full end-to-end call through
  `_try_open_shop_or_loot()` itself couldn't be exercised headlessly —
  `Viewport.warp_mouse()` needs a real display server — but the fixed logic
  itself is proven correct in isolation.)

---

## 2026-09-14 — Session 36: Vendor targeting/hail were never wired up, plus his tilted transform fixed

Reported right after the raycast fix: "I'm not able to target, hail, or
right click Aldric the Provisioner now." Target (Tab/left-click) and hail
had actually never worked on him at all — VendorNPC was never added to
either system when it was built, this wasn't a regression from the
right-click change. Right-click itself turned out to have a real
contributing cause: his placed transform.

### Root cause of the right-click trouble: the same tilt flagged last session

The "incidental finding, not touched" note from the right-click fix — his
transform had a ~10° pitch and ~3° roll beyond a clean facing turn — turned
out to matter after all: `Scenes/lumora_outskirts3d.tscn`'s VendorNPC now
requires clicking his exact collision shape, and that shape (and the
visible character mesh, which inherits the same parent tilt) was measurably
offset from where it naively should sit. Computed the clean replacement
(`Basis.get_euler()` to extract just the yaw, `Basis.IDENTITY.rotated(Vector3.UP,
yaw)` to rebuild without the pitch/roll) and swapped in that transform,
keeping his exact position and facing direction. Verified: the collision
capsule now sits exactly where a straight, upright NPC's would
((45.075, 1.0, -15.055), basis.y exactly (0,1,0)).

### Target / hail extended to vendors (`player3d.gd`, `vendor_npc.gd`, `target_frame.gd`)

- `tab_cycle_target()`/`_try_click_target()`: both now include group
  `"npc_vendor"`/type `VendorNPC` alongside monsters and guards.
  `_is_targetable_alive()` already had a generic fallback for non-`Monster`
  nodes (guards used it too), so no change needed there.
- `try_hail_nearby_npc()`: now scans `"npc_guard"` + `"npc_vendor"` and
  calls `respond_to_hail()` on whichever's nearest (guarded by
  `has_method()` rather than assuming every hailable NPC type shares it).
  New `VendorNPC.respond_to_hail()` reuses `greet_player()`'s existing line
  — hailing him is just a way to get his greeting without opening the shop.
- `TargetFrame.faction_status()` didn't know about vendors at all and fell
  through to its default `"Enemy"` — meaning targeting him (once added to
  the systems above) would have shown him red or hostile. Added
  `target.is_in_group("npc_vendor")` alongside the existing guard check, so
  he shows `"Ally"` (blue) like a guard does.
- Verified headlessly: `Tab` correctly selects him when nearby, left-click
  correctly selects him when the raycast lands on his (now properly
  aligned) collision shape, `faction_status()` returns `"Ally"`, and hailing
  him logs his greeting line.

## 2026-09-14 — Session 35: Right-click vendor now requires clicking his model

Reported: "sometimes I have to kill enemies near him, but I can't loot them
because it is opening his vendor window instead of the corpse" — right-click
opened the shop for the nearest vendor within 5m regardless of where the
cursor was, so a vendor standing near a fresh corpse always stole the
right-click.

### `player3d.gd`

- `_try_open_shop_or_loot()` (formerly range-based — nearest `"npc_vendor"`
  within 5m, unconditionally) is now raycast-based: the shop only opens if
  the cursor is precisely on that vendor's own collision shape, the same way
  `_try_click_target()` already raycasts for left-click monster/guard
  targeting. Everything else — including a corpse standing right next to
  the vendor — falls straight through to the unchanged, range-based
  `_try_loot_corpse()`.
- Only fires in `MOUSE_MODE_VISIBLE`, matching `_try_click_target()`'s own
  gating: while mouselooking (captured), there's no real screen-space cursor
  position to raycast from (Godot's virtual mouse position drifts based on
  accumulated relative motion while captured, it doesn't track "where the
  camera is looking"), so right-click just goes straight to loot-corpse in
  that mode, same as before.
- `_find_nearby_vendor()` removed — replaced by `_raycast_vendor(screen_pos)`,
  which casts a ray from the camera through the given screen position
  (`camera.project_ray_origin()`/`project_ray_normal()`) and returns the hit
  only if it's a `VendorNPC`.
- Verified the raycast mechanism directly (positioned a camera to look
  exactly at the vendor's real collision-shape center via
  `camera.look_at()`, then fed `camera.unproject_position()`'s result back
  into `_raycast_vendor()`): correctly found the vendor when aimed at him,
  correctly returned nothing when aimed elsewhere. Verifying the full
  right-click flow end-to-end wasn't possible headlessly — `Viewport.warp_mouse()`
  needs a real display server, unavailable under `--headless` — but the
  function it depends on is proven correct in isolation, and the
  surrounding logic (mouse-mode gate, fallback to loot-corpse) is a handful
  of lines checked by inspection.
- Incidental finding while testing, not touched: the vendor's placed
  transform in `lumora_outskirts3d.tscn` has a slight pitch/roll beyond a
  simple facing rotation (its basis.y isn't exactly `(0,1,0)`) — likely from
  a free-rotate drag in the editor rather than a clean Y-axis turn. Harmless
  for this fix (the collision capsule rotates correctly with it either way),
  but worth a look in the editor if the model ever looks subtly tilted.

## 2026-09-14 — Session 34: Assist-range bug fix, real monster patrol

Reported while testing the death system: "if I'm fighting a bandit and
another spawns during the fight, it will come assist even if it is 20m
away" — plus a request for monsters to actually wander instead of standing
still ("add a patrol function so the enemies move around and are not static").

### Bug fix: ally-assist range was effectively unlimited (`monster3d.gd`)

`call_nearby_allies()` compared a real meter distance
(`global_position.distance_to(ally.global_position)`) against `aggro_range`
— but `aggro_range` is documented (and used everywhere else) as the RAW,
unscaled 2D-era JSON value, not meters (e.g. a bandit's `aggro_range` is
`200.0`). So allies within roughly a 200m radius, not 20m, were being pulled
into the fight — the 20m the user actually observed was well inside that,
not an edge case. Fixed with a dedicated `ASSIST_RANGE := 5.0` constant
(real meters, matching the requested 5m), decoupled entirely from
`aggro_range`. Verified headlessly: an ally 20m away stayed put, one 3m away
correctly joined.

### Real monster patrol (`monster3d.gd`)

A patrol mechanism nominally already existed (IDLE had a 1%-per-frame
chance to switch to PATROL, then wandered within a flat 10-unit radius of
spawn) but read as functionally static — a 10-unit bubble with a generous
1-unit "close enough" arrival radius meant a monster's actual net movement
over any given stretch of time was tiny, easy to miss entirely. Confirmed
headlessly before changing anything: a bandit idling for 12s only ended up
~3 units from its start position.
- Replaced the per-frame RNG roll with a deterministic timer
  (`_idle_timer`/`_idle_duration`, randomized `IDLE_MIN_DURATION`–
  `IDLE_MAX_DURATION` = 2–5s, rolled fresh via a new `State.IDLE` case in
  `change_state()`) — patrol now starts reliably instead of "eventually,
  probably."
- Widened the wander area: `pick_new_patrol_point()` now rolls a distance
  between `PATROL_MIN_DISTANCE` (5) and `PATROL_MAX_DISTANCE` (18) from
  spawn instead of a flat `0–10`, which also guarantees every leg is long
  enough to actually read as movement (no more trivially-short hops).
  Passive-behavior mobs are unchanged — they still stay put until aggroed,
  same as before.
- Verified headlessly: the same bandit setup now covers ~14 units of total
  path over 10s and reaches ~12 units from spawn (previously ~3 units net
  over 12s), confirmed still in `PATROL` throughout.

---

## 2026-09-14 — Session 33: Player death/respawn system, monster death lines, and a serious double-damage bug fix

Prompted by: "I went out and found the bandit camp, and they swiftly kicked
my ass!" — an EQ-style death flow was requested: incapacitate at 0 HP
(EXISTING and existing HP=0 previously just froze the player forever, no
recovery), bleed out ~20 more "HP" over time, then true death → respawn at a
bind point, with monsters resetting and walking home rather than farming a
downed player. Investigating "swiftly kicked my ass" surfaced something far
more consequential than expected.

### Bug fix: monster attacks against the player were dealing EXACTLY DOUBLE damage

Root cause, `monster3d.gd`'s `perform_attack()`: `CombatNode.resolve_attack()`
already applies a hit's damage directly (`target.current_hp -= damage`,
`combatnode.gd`) as part of resolving it — but `perform_attack()` then ALSO
called `target.take_damage(result["damage"], self)` for the same hit when
the target was the player, and `player3d.gd`'s `take_damage()` applies
`combat_node.take_damage(amount)` on top of that. Every monster hit against
the player was landing twice. Player-vs-monster, guard-vs-monster, and
pet-vs-monster attacks were all already correct (none of those call sites
had the redundant second application) — this was isolated to monster-vs-player,
which is exactly the direction that made the bandit camp brutal.
- Verified with a headless test before touching anything: a bandit hitting
  the player for a raw, `resolve_attack()`-reported 18 or 47 damage was
  landing as 36 or 94 through the full `perform_attack()` call — exactly
  double, every time.
- Fixed by removing the redundant `take_damage()`/`apply_damage()` calls
  from that branch entirely and adding `player3d.gd::on_combat_node_hit()` —
  handles ONLY the side effects `resolve_attack()` doesn't (auto-target-switch
  to the attacker, the incapacitation check) without re-applying damage.
  Applied the same fix to the parallel (currently unreachable, but
  structurally identical) case of a monster hitting some other
  `CombatNode`-carrying target — added an explicit death check there too,
  since removing the old `apply_damage()` call also removed the death
  check it used to carry internally.
- Re-verified after the fix: the same bandit now lands for exactly 18/47,
  matching `resolve_attack()`'s own reported damage 1:1.

### Player death / incapacitation / respawn (`player3d.gd`)

- `dying: bool` already existed (froze movement/regen via existing gates in
  `_physics_process()`/`_process()`) but nothing ever set it back to false —
  hitting 0 HP was a permanent, unrecoverable freeze. Kept `dying` as that
  same "can't act" gate, layered a real state machine on top:
  **incapacitated** (0 HP, `is_incapacitated=true`, `dying=true`) → 20
  seconds of passive bleed-out (`BLEED_OUT_DURATION`, periodic "You are
  bleeding out..." reminders every 5s) → **true death** → a black respawn
  screen for `RESPAWN_DELAY` (3s) → teleport to bind point at full HP/mana/
  stamina, `dying` cleared.
- The bleed-out window is deliberately passive/deterministic, not more
  combat damage — monsters disengage the instant incapacitation starts (see
  below), so nothing can interrupt or accelerate it. Regen is also frozen
  throughout (the existing `dying` gate already did this), so there's no
  chance of self-stabilizing mid-bleed-out.
- A single overkill hit can drop `combat_node.current_hp` well below 0
  (`resolve_attack()` doesn't clamp) — `die()` clamps it back to exactly 0
  on entry so the HP bar and the bleed-out window both start clean
  regardless of how large the killing blow was.
- Sequence at true death, refined through two rounds of feedback (an EQ1
  reference — red "you have been slain" text, a random flavor line, then a
  black "LOADING, PLEASE WAIT" screen — then "don't copy those messages
  exactly, have something similar"): a red `"You have been defeated by
  <attacker>!"` combat-log line (attacker captured at the moment of
  incapacitation, not true death, since the killing blow's source is what
  actually matters — `_last_attacker_desc`), a random line from a new
  `Data/player_death_flavor.json` (own wording — "Darkness folds in around
  you.", "Your legs give out beneath you.", etc., not EQ's actual text),
  then a full-screen black `CanvasLayer` reading "RETURNING TO YOUR BIND
  POINT..." (own wording, not "LOADING, PLEASE WAIT") held for
  `RESPAWN_DELAY` before the teleport.
- **Bind point**: no bind spell/NPC exists yet, so
  `_ensure_bind_point()` defaults it to wherever the character first spawns
  into the world, stored as `Global.player_data["bind_point"]` (a plain
  `[x,y,z]` array — `Vector3` isn't JSON-serializable), persisted once and
  read back by `get_bind_point()`. Structured so a real bind
  spell/NPC can just overwrite that same field later without touching
  anything else.
- Verified headlessly end-to-end: forcing the bleed-out timer past
  `BLEED_OUT_DURATION` while the player stood far from their bind point
  correctly fired the "defeated by" message, held the black screen, then
  teleported back to the bind point at full HP with `dying`/
  `is_incapacitated` both cleared.

### Monsters disengage and go home instead of farming a downed player (`monster3d.gd`)

- New `force_disengage()`: clears `aggro_table` and, if currently
  `CHASE`/`ATTACK`, switches to `PATROL` with `patrol_target` set straight
  to `spawn_position` (overriding `change_state()`'s own random nearby
  patrol point) — reads as "gives up and walks home" rather than wandering
  off to a random nearby spot. Called on every monster in the `"monsters"`
  group the instant the player becomes incapacitated (from `player3d.gd`'s
  `die()`).
- `can_see_player()` now returns false whenever `player.dying` is true — so
  a monster that's mid-`PATROL` back to spawn (or any other monster in
  `IDLE`/`PATROL`) won't re-aggro the same downed/dead-pending-respawn
  player standing right next to it. Once the player actually respawns
  (potentially far away, at their bind point), `dying` clears and normal
  sight-based aggro resumes as before.
- Verified headlessly: a monster mid-CHASE against the player, on
  `player.die()`, correctly switched to PATROL with an empty aggro table,
  and `can_see_player()` returned false while the player remained `dying`.

### Monster death lines (`Data/humanoid_death_lines.json`)

- New user-editable flat line pool (`{"death": [...]}`, same shape/loader —
  `NPCFlavorText`, already built for `guard_flavor_text.json` — as the
  existing guard hail/engage system), 10 starting lines. `monster3d.gd`'s
  `die()` logs a random one, attributed to the monster by name/description,
  for any monster in the `"humanoid"` category (bandits, goblins, etc. —
  NOT skeletons/mummies/mirage_phantom, which are `"undead"`, a distinct
  category that wouldn't make sense saying human last words).
- Verified: a dying bandit correctly logged one of the file's lines; a
  dying skeleton (undead category) correctly logged nothing, confirming the
  category gate works as intended.

---

## 2026-09-14 — Session 32: Coin sound effect on loot/sell, vendor greeting line

Requested right after testing the character sheet redesign: play a
downloaded sound ("yodguard-coin-collect") whenever the player loots coin
from a corpse, or sells an item to a vendor.

### `Scripts/global.gd` — new `play_sfx()`/`play_coin_sound()`

- No existing precedent for a one-shot, non-positional UI sound anywhere in
  the live 3D codebase — the only `AudioStreamPlayer` usage in the project
  is either looping ambient audio (campfire, torch) or belongs to dead
  2D-era scenes (`magic_missile.tscn`, `ranged_attack.tscn`, unreachable
  from the live game). Built fresh on `Global` (already the autoload that
  owns the "SFX" bus and its Options-menu volume slider), rather than a new
  parallel audio system.
- `play_sfx(stream, volume_db=0.0)`: spawns a short-lived `AudioStreamPlayer`
  as a child of `Global`, on the `"SFX"` bus (so it respects the existing
  SFX volume setting automatically), plays it, and frees itself on
  `finished` — a fresh player per call rather than one shared/reused player,
  so looting several coin drops in quick succession layers the sound
  instead of each new play cutting the previous one off.
- `play_coin_sound()`: the specific entrypoint, preloading
  `Assets/yodguard-coin-collect-3-540190.mp3` (copied in from the user's
  Downloads folder, following the project's existing convention of keeping
  long descriptive downloaded filenames as-is — see
  `soundsforyou-campfire-crackling-fireplace-sound-119594.mp3`).
- Wired into the three places currency actually changes hands in the
  player's favor: `corpse_loot_window.gd`'s `_apply_drop()` currency branch
  (manual Take/Loot All/loot-preference), `monster3d.gd`'s
  `_auto_process_loot()` currency branch (auto-looted coin), and
  `shop_window.gd`'s `_sell()`. Deliberately NOT wired into buying from the
  vendor (`shop_window.gd`'s `_buy()`) — the request was specifically about
  receiving coin, not spending it.
- Verified headlessly across all three trigger points plus a direct call:
  `Global`'s child count increments by exactly one `AudioStreamPlayer` per
  coin event and returns to zero a couple seconds later once every sound has
  finished playing and freed itself — confirms no leak from firing this
  repeatedly.

### Vendor greeting line (`vendor_npc.gd`, `player3d.gd`)

- Requested in the same breath: have the vendor say something when the shop
  window opens, in the same "$NPC says, ..." style guard_npc.gd already uses
  for hail/engage lines. New `VendorNPC.greet_player(player_name)` — picks a
  random entry from the vendor's own `get_shop_stock()` and logs `"Welcome,
  <player>. I have a good <item name> you might be interested in."` Called
  from `player3d.gd`'s `_open_shop()` right after the window opens, passing
  the player's own `player_name`.
- Verified headlessly: opening the shop as "Zozuur" produced exactly one
  General-log line — `Aldric the Provisioner says, "Welcome, Zozuur. I have
  a good Trail Rations you might be interested in."`

---

## 2026-09-14 — Session 31: Character sheet redesign — tabbed Stats/Equipment/Inventory

Prompted by a screenshot: the character sheet was a single scrolling column
of ~25 raw stat Labels stacked above the equipment grid stacked above the
flat inventory grid — "cluttered and clunky." Compared against an EQ2
character-window screenshot the user shared; agreed a left-hand vertical tab
rail (Stats / Equipment / Inventory) was the right shape, mocked it up as an
HTML artifact first (approved: "that looks perfect!"), then built it.

### `Scripts/character_sheet.gd` / `Scenes/character_sheet.tscn` — rebuilt

- New structure: a persistent identity+vitals strip (name/race/class/level,
  plus HP/MP/STA/Food/Water bars — Food/Water are new here, sourced the same
  way `player_frame.gd` does via `player.satiety`/`player.thirst`) sits above
  a left tab rail and a content area that shows exactly one of three
  ScrollContainers at a time (`_select_tab()`). The `.tscn` only declares the
  structural containers; all actual content (stat tiles, kv rows, resistance
  badges, the paperdoll, inventory slots) is still built in `_ready()` via
  helper functions, matching the existing project convention (the old
  equipment paperdoll was already 100% code-built via
  `_init_equipment_slots()`) rather than hand-authoring dozens of `.tscn`
  node blocks.
- **Stats tab**: Attributes as a 4-column grid of bordered tiles (reusing the
  exact dark-panel/thin-border style already established by
  `corpse_loot_window.gd`/`shop_window.gd`'s rows, not a new palette);
  Combat (Armor Class/Attack/Crit/Spell Power/Carry Weight) as alternating-
  shade key/value rows; Resistances as small pill badges with a color dot per
  damage type; Experience as the existing purple XP bar plus numbers;
  Currency as four coin-colored dots (platinum/gold/silver/copper).
- **Equipment tab**: the paperdoll grid is functionally unchanged (same
  `_init_equipment_slots()` layout, same 25 `EQUIPMENT_SLOTS`, same
  `slot_button.gd` instances) — just moved into its own tab instead of being
  one more section in the long scroll.
- **Inventory tab** — the one genuinely new piece, and the one the user
  specifically weighed in on: "the bag slots (inventory) are the main
  player's inventory system until you find some real bags... a mix of what
  I've seen in other MMO games." The 12 `basic_inventory` root slots are
  shown as the actual inventory (not a pointer to a separate window), and
  any bag equipped into one of them (`small_bag`, `traveler_pack`, etc.) gets
  its own nested section directly below with its own full-capacity slot
  grid — `_rebuild_bag_sections()`, rebuilt on every
  `Inventory.inventory_changed`. Each bag's slots (filled AND empty) get the
  correct `bag_slot` index so dropping an item from the root grid straight
  into a bag works via the existing `slot_button.gd`/`move_item_between_slots()`
  drag-and-drop plumbing, unchanged. The separate Backpack window (`B` key)
  still exists and shows the same underlying data — this doesn't replace it,
  just stops the character sheet from hiding that data behind it.
- Draggable/resizable window behavior (`_on_panel_gui_input()`,
  `WindowPosition` save/load) is unchanged, just retargeted at the new
  container names.
- Verified in the real zone scene: opening the sheet populates every field
  correctly (name/level/subtitle, all 7 attributes, armor class, resistances,
  XP, coins, all 5 vitals); tab switching correctly shows exactly one panel;
  the Equipment tab has all 25 slots; the Inventory tab shows all 12 root
  slots plus one bag section for the starting Small Bag with the right
  tooltip; adding an item live-refreshes the bag section without error;
  drag/resize input handling doesn't crash.

---

## 2026-09-14 — Session 30: Loot silently vanishing, guard chatter range, zoom-to-first-person

Reported while testing a fresh character: looted "Bone Shield" logged as
received but never appeared anywhere in inventory. Also asked for guard/NPC
speech to only show in the log when the player's close enough to hear it
(~5m), and for zooming the camera all the way in to drop into first-person.

### Bug: loot could report success while silently failing to fit

**Root cause, `Scripts/inventory_autoload.gd`**: every "player receives an
item" call site (`corpse_loot_window.gd`'s Take/Loot All/preference
checkboxes, the manual loot-window drag-to-inventory path in
`slot_button.gd`, `monster3d.gd`'s auto-loot, `shop_window.gd`'s Buy) called
`Inventory.add_to_basic_inventory(item_id)` and then unconditionally logged
"You receive X" — the function's boolean return value (false when all 12
`basic_inventory` slots are full) was never checked at any of those sites.
A new character starts with `small_bag` + 6 pieces of unequipped starting
gear already occupying 7 of those 12 slots directly (not in a bag — they're
a staging area to be dragged onto equipment slots), so it doesn't take much
looting before `add_to_basic_inventory` starts silently failing while the
log keeps claiming success.
- A second, independent bug in the same code path: **every** call to
  `add_to_basic_inventory()`/`add_to_bag()` across the whole codebase passed
  no quantity argument, so `create_item_instance(item_id)` always defaulted
  to quantity 1 — a "3x Spider Silk" drop's log message correctly said
  "x3," but only 1 was ever actually added.
- Fixed both by adding a new `Inventory.add_item(item_id, quantity)` —
  merges onto an existing stack in any bag first (correctly applying
  `quantity`), then any bag with room (keeps loot from eating the scarce 12
  `basic_inventory` slots one-per-item when a bag has space), and only falls
  back to a bare `basic_inventory` slot if every bag is full. Returns false
  only when there is truly no room anywhere. `add_to_basic_inventory()`/
  `add_to_bag()` both gained an optional `quantity` parameter (default 1,
  so every existing call site is unaffected) so `add_item()` can thread a
  real quantity through instead of hardcoding 1.
- Every "player receives an item" call site now checks `add_item()`'s return
  value: `corpse_loot_window.gd`'s `_apply_drop()` now returns bool and only
  logs "You receive X" on success, logging "Your inventory is full — you
  leave X behind" otherwise; `_take()`/`_loot_all()`/the preference
  checkboxes only remove the drop from the corpse (`pending_loot`) when it
  was actually applied, so a full-inventory drop stays lootable instead of
  vanishing. Same fix applied to `monster3d.gd`'s auto-loot (falls back to
  the manual loot window instead of disappearing), `slot_button.gd`'s
  drag-to-inventory loot path, and `shop_window.gd`'s Buy.
- Verified headlessly: stacking 3 then 2 more of a stackable item onto the
  same bag slot correctly reaches quantity 5; filling a 4-slot bag correctly
  spills the next item into `basic_inventory`; filling every slot
  everywhere makes `add_item()` return false and `_apply_drop()` leaves the
  drop in `pending_loot` rather than discarding it.

### Guard/NPC speech now range-gated (`guard_npc.gd`)

- `say()` (the single chokepoint both `_say_flavor()`'s hail/engage lines
  and `npc_scripted_conversation.gd`'s day/night scripted guard exchange
  route through) previously logged unconditionally regardless of the
  player's distance from the speaking guard — the screenshot that prompted
  this showed Guard Corwin and Guard Reyna's day/night exchange appearing in
  the log while the player was at the vendor, nowhere near either guard.
  Added `HEAR_RANGE := 5.0` and a `_player_in_hear_range()` check at the top
  of `say()`; verified in the real zone scene (far away = silenced, within
  range = heard).
- `try_hail_nearby_npc()`'s own H-key hail already required proximity before
  calling `respond_to_hail()` at all, so this doesn't change hail behavior —
  only unprompted lines (engage callouts, scripted conversations) were ever
  broadcasting past hearing range.

### Camera: zooming all the way in drops into first-person (`camera_controller.gd`)

- `zoom_camera()`: from either third-person mode, scrolling in until
  `current_zoom` hits `MIN_ZOOM` now switches `current_mode` to
  `FIRST_PERSON` automatically instead of just stopping at the closest
  third-person distance. Scrolling out again while in first-person pops back
  to `THIRD_PERSON_BEHIND` at `MIN_ZOOM`, so the whole thing reads as one
  continuous zoom rather than a jump between unrelated modes. `Home`'s
  manual camera-mode cycle is unchanged.
- Verified headlessly: zooming in from just above `MIN_ZOOM` switches mode
  to `FIRST_PERSON`; zooming back out from `FIRST_PERSON` switches back to
  `THIRD_PERSON_BEHIND` with `current_zoom` reset to `MIN_ZOOM`.

---

## 2026-09-14 — Session 29: Satiety system made functional, general goods vendor + shop window

Requested after Session 28's overnight balance pass: "implement the satiety
system... they should be consumed to allow the player to restore health and
mana... add a well fed buff... when the player is out of food, health and
stamina don't regenerate... out of drink, mana doesn't regenerate" — plus a
vendor NPC "just past the two guards" the player can right-click to buy
food/drink/bags and sell exploring loot for coin.

### Satiety / Thirst — was decaying but non-functional, now a full loop

`player3d.gd` already had satiety/thirst decaying over real time (satiety:
100 real minutes to fully drain from 100; thirst ~50, roughly twice as fast)
and a `get_stat_penalty()` combat debuff below 25 — but nothing could ever
raise them back up (no eat/drink action existed anywhere), the "out of X"
behavior was a flat 20% regen reduction rather than a hard stop, running out
dealt small chip damage instead, and the two `print()` warnings never
actually reached the player (console-only, no GameLog call).

- **Hard regen gating**: satiety <= 0 now halts HP regen (`_process()`'s regen
  tick) AND movement stamina regen (`update_stamina()`) entirely — not a
  percentage reduction. thirst <= 0 halts mana regen entirely. The existing
  <25 soft penalty (0.8x regen, 5%/10% melee damage) still applies above 0 as
  a graduated warning zone.
- Removed `apply_starvation_damage()`/`apply_dehydration_damage()` (chip
  damage on empty) — replaced by the regen gating above, which is what was
  actually asked for. Added real, edge-triggered (fires once per threshold
  crossing, not every 60s tick) `GameLog` warnings instead of the old silent
  `print()`s: "Your stomach growls..." at <25, "You are famished..." at 0
  (and the thirst equivalents), so the player actually sees why regen
  stopped.
- **Consumption**: `consume_food_or_drink(item)` (player3d.gd) reads an
  item's `restores` ("satiety"/"thirst") and `restore_amount` fields, capped
  at 100. Triggered from a new "Eat"/"Drink" button in the inventory's
  right-click inspect popup (`slot_button.gd`, alongside the existing
  Equip/Learn buttons) for any item with `type` "food"/"drink". Consumes
  exactly one unit via a new `Inventory.consume_one()` (decrements a stack's
  quantity, or removes the slot/entry entirely at quantity 1 — the same
  addressing scheme `slot_button.gd`'s drag payload already uses).
  `iron_rations`/`water_flask` (`items.json`) already had unused "Restores 15
  Hunger/Thirst" flavor text — wired that exact number up rather than
  inventing a new one. Added two cheaper tiers, `trail_rations` (+8 satiety,
  2cp) and `spring_water` (+8 thirst, 1cp), for vendor variety.
- **Well Fed buff**: re-evaluated every vitals tick (not a fixed timer) —
  `combat_node.apply_effect("well_fed", INF, {hp_regen_bonus:2,
  mana_regen_bonus:2, stamina_regen_bonus:2})` while satiety AND thirst are
  both >= 75, removed the moment either drops below. Reuses the existing
  generic buff/debuff system (`combatnode.gd`'s `active_effects`, same one
  `campfire.gd`'s "Warmth of the Campfire" and stances use) — shows up on the
  buff bar automatically with no separate UI wiring, exactly the "give
  feedback to the player" the request asked for. Added its description to
  `buff_bar.gd`'s `ENVIRONMENTAL_EFFECT_DESCRIPTIONS`.
- **HUD**: `player_frame.tscn`/`.gd` gained Food/Water bars (orange/teal)
  below the existing HP/MP/STA rows — satiety/thirst had no player-visible
  readout at all before this, which would have made "why did my HP stop
  healing" undebuggable from in-game alone.

### General Goods Vendor — new NPC + shop window

- **`Scripts/vendor_npc.gd` + `Scenes/vendor_npc.tscn`** (new): stationary
  `CharacterBody3D`, group `"npc_vendor"`, reuses the player's own Mixamo
  model/idle animation for its visual (same convention as `monster3d.gd`'s
  humanoid mobs) — no combat, no navigation, just stands and has a name
  label like `guard_npc.gd`. Loads its stock list from `Data/vendor_shop.json`
  by `shop_id` (`get_shop_stock()`, `get_sell_price()`).
- **`Data/vendor_shop.json`** (new): per-vendor stock lists + buy/sell
  multipliers. Prices are NOT duplicated data — they derive from each
  item's own `items.json` `value`/`currency_type` via a new
  `Global.item_value_in_copper()` helper (handles the handful of
  silver-denominated trophy items correctly), scaled by the vendor's
  multiplier (buy 1.0x, sell 0.5x for the starting vendor). First vendor,
  "Aldric the Provisioner", stocks `trail_rations`, `iron_rations`,
  `spring_water`, `water_flask`, `small_bag`, a new `traveler_pack` (8-slot
  bag, up from `small_bag`'s 4), and `torch`.
- **Currency**: was 4 independent counters (copper/silver/gold/platinum)
  with no conversion helper anywhere — `grant_currency()` could only add to
  one denomination at a time, unusable for "pay 17 copper" style pricing.
  Added `Global.get_total_copper()`/`can_afford()`/`spend_currency_copper()`/
  `add_currency_copper()` — these collapse all 4 counters to one
  copper-equivalent pool (standard 10cp=1sp/10sp=1gp/10sp=1pp conversion),
  spend/add against that pool, then re-mint the result back into
  denominations greedily from platinum down. Verified: 5cp+2sp=25cp total;
  spending 8cp against that yields 7cp+1sp; adding 150cp yields 1gp+6sp+7cp.
- **`Scripts/shop_window.gd` + `Scenes/shop_window.tscn`** (new): draggable
  window (same `WindowPosition`/title-bar/close-button pattern as
  `corpse_loot_window.gd`/`backpack_ui.gd`) with a currency readout and two
  lists — "For Sale" (the vendor's stock, Buy buttons, disabled when
  unaffordable) and "Your Items" (everything in the player's basic inventory
  slots and every bag's contents except bags themselves — selling a bag
  would destroy its contents, so those are excluded — each with a Sell
  button). Buying calls `Inventory.add_to_basic_inventory()` +
  `Global.spend_currency_copper()`; selling calls `Inventory.consume_one()` +
  `Global.add_currency_copper()`. Both list rebuilds are wired to
  `Global.currency_changed`/`Inventory.inventory_changed` so afford-state and
  contents stay live without manual refresh calls scattered everywhere.
- **Interaction**: right-click now goes through
  `player3d.gd`'s `_try_open_shop_or_loot()` — checks for a `"npc_vendor"`
  within `SHOP_RANGE` (5m, same range-based pattern as `try_hail_nearby_npc()`)
  first, opens the shop if found, otherwise falls through to the existing
  `_try_loot_corpse()` unchanged. Deliberately range-based rather than a
  raycast under the cursor (like left-click targeting uses) — a raycast
  would behave inconsistently between mouselook (captured cursor, fixed
  screen position) and free-look (visible, cursor-following) mouse modes;
  range-based works identically in both, matching how hail/loot already work.
- **Placement**: initially placed as a temporary stand-in near the guards,
  since the user's own marker cube existed only in their unsaved editor
  session at the time. Once they saved it and named it ("Vendor_shape", a
  `CollisionShape3D` under `NavigationRegion3D/LumoraOutskirts_Collision`,
  origin (45.77, 5.0, -23.22)), moved the vendor to just in front of and to
  the right of that marker — (49.77, 0.6725, -27.22), offset along the
  scene's world +X/-Z axes (matching `player3d.gd`'s own `right_dir`/
  `forward_dir` convention), verified with a 0.0 unit navmesh snap distance.
  The user then fine-tuned the exact spot and facing themselves in the
  editor; final saved transform in `Scenes/lumora_outskirts3d.tscn` is
  origin (45.075, 0, -15.055) with a custom rotation, confirmed working
  ("it looks great!").
- Verified end-to-end with a full scene-load headless test: vendor spawns at
  the placed position with a 0.0 navmesh snap distance, the player's
  proximity check finds it, `_open_shop()` opens a real window instance with
  7 populated buy rows and 10 populated sell rows (drawn from the player's
  actual starting inventory).

---

## 2026-09-14 — Session 28: Monster balance pass — combat math, zone spawn density, loot expansion

Overnight autonomous session, triggered by: "monsters aren't doing much damage... I
can kill them with the siphon life spell and they're not hitting me for the most
part," plus a request to review the zone's spawn density against its area map
(mausoleum → undead, goblin/bandit camps → goblins/bandits, spider den → spiders)
and to compare loot density/variety to EverQuest starting zones ("way too sparse").

### Bug Fixes

**Monster combat stats were flatly zeroed (`monster3d.gd` `_configure_combat_node()`)**
- Every monster's base STR/CON/DEX/INT/WIS/CHA/LUCK was hardcoded to 0, with only
  flat, level-derived `gear_atk`/`gear_ac`/`gear_hp` offsets driving combat — a
  pattern intended to let the JSON's `armor_class`/`max_health` stay authoritative,
  but with an unintended side effect: melee damage (`weapon_damage + STR/2`), hit
  chance (`weapon_skill*2 + STR + DEX/2 + gear_atk`), and ALL magic resistance
  (`CON/2 + WIS/2`) all lost their stat contribution entirely — confirmed every
  monster had exactly 0% arcane/divine resist.
- This is why a single Life Siphon (~70 flat damage) could one-shot a rat (20 HP)
  five times over and take a bandit (80 HP) to 12% remaining, fully unresisted,
  while monsters barely hit the player back (e.g. a level-3 bandit: 41% hit chance
  for 6% of player HP per hit).
- Fixed by giving monsters real, level-scaled stats (`stat_scale = 8 + level*3` for
  STR/CON/DEX, flat 5 for the rest) while backing out the JSON's hand-tuned
  `max_health`/`armor_class` via `gear_hp`/`gear_ac` so per-mob HP/AC didn't
  silently inflate on top of already-balanced numbers.
- Verified via a headless before/after comparison script: monster hit chance vs.
  player went from 9–41% to 19–55%; damage per hit from 0.6–6.8% to 3.4–10.2% of
  player HP. Life Siphon vs. monsters went from 70–350% (always fully unresisted)
  to 60–65 flat/65–320% of HP with resistances now correctly applying 7–13%.

**Spell resistance had never actually applied, to anyone (`combatnode.gd`)**
- `get_resistance(resist_type)` appends `"_resist"` to its argument itself. Both
  `calculate_spell_damage()` and the (dead/unused, confirmed via grep)
  `calculate_resist_chance()` were calling it with an already-suffixed key
  (`"arcane_resist"`/`"divine_resist"`), producing a lookup for the nonexistent
  `"arcane_resist_resist"`/`"divine_resist_resist"` — always 0.
- This meant spell damage has never been resisted by any target, player or
  monster, in the entire game, until this fix. Fixed by passing the unsuffixed
  `"arcane"`/`"divine"` instead.

### Zone Spawns — density pass against the hand-drawn area map (`Data/lumora_outskirts_spawns.json`)

- Previously just 10 sparse entries, only 2 zones ("core"/"edge"), a single
  skeleton spawn in a disconnected far corner, a single bandit spawn, and **no
  goblin spawns at all** despite goblins being a defined humanoid mob type.
- Real-world coordinates for every named map area were extracted from the
  `Transform3D` origins of the level's own named `CollisionShape3D` nodes under
  `NavigationRegion3D/LumoraOutskirts_Collision` in `Scenes/lumora_outskirts3d.tscn`
  (auto-generated from the Blender-exported level geometry, so these are exact,
  current level positions, not guesses). No dedicated geometry exists for "Bandit
  Camp" or "Spider Den," so those two were placed by cross-referencing the
  schematic map's relative layout (bandit camps between the dune starter area and
  the goblin camps; spider den in its own southwest quadrant) against the real
  navmesh bounds — discovered mid-session to be far larger than assumed
  (`X[-1485.5,115.0] Z[-1167.5,1002.0]`, not the small ~-90..100 corner exercised
  by earlier chase-bug testing). Every candidate coordinate, old and new, was
  verified with `NavigationServer3D.map_get_closest_point()` before being written
  in — all 27 final entries resolve within 1.5 units of the real navmesh surface.
- New layout: Sand Dunes starter area (rat/snake/slime + new dune_scarab/bat, near
  the origin) → Mausoleum/Graveyard/2 Small Crypts undead cluster (4 skeleton
  spawns + a mummy + a mirage_phantom, southeast) → 2 Bandit Camps (4 spawns,
  between the dunes and the goblin camps) → 3 Goblin Camps (goblins, clustered
  around ~(-568,605), matching the map's "three circles") → a dedicated 4-spawn
  Spider Den (southwest), replacing the two disconnected scattered spider spawns.
- Added `goblin` and `bat` entries to `Data/monsters.json` (goblin didn't exist as
  a monster at all before this, despite already being in `monster3d.gd`'s
  `HUMANOID_MOB_TYPES`), and added `dune_scarab`/`mummy`/`mirage_phantom`/`goblin`
  to `mob_spawner3d.gd`'s `VALID_MOB_TYPES` allowlist (previously present in
  `monsters.json` but silently unspawnable, or vice versa).
- Verified with a full in-engine functional test: loaded the real zone scene,
  confirmed all 27 spawn positions resolve onto the live navmesh, and spawned one
  of every new mob type via `monster_template.tscn` to confirm correct stat
  loading from JSON.

### Loot — expanded and compared against EverQuest-style starting-zone density (`Data/solgrave_expanse_loot.json`, `Data/items.json`)

- `category_loot_tables` (a baseline roll that fires once per kill *in addition*
  to the mob's own species table, per `monster3d.gd roll_loot()`) were all
  completely empty — a large part of why loot felt sparse regardless of species.
  Populated with a small per-category floor: animal → occasional fur pelt;
  insect → occasional chitin; reptile → occasional scale; humanoid → occasional
  leather scrap + modest copper; undead → occasional bone fragment + modest
  copper. This gives every mob of a given category a guaranteed-nonzero loot
  floor even before its bespoke table is considered.
- Expanded per-species tables for every mob now spawning in the zone: rat/snake
  gained a coin chance and a second junk item; dune_scarab's 5-entry table (item
  names already present in the JSON, but referencing items that didn't exist in
  `items.json`) got real item definitions instead of silently failing to loot;
  bandit/goblin/skeleton (`edge_spawns` table) each grew from 3–4 entries to 5–6
  (added silver coin/note flavor to bandit, a dagger/charm to goblin, bone
  fragment/bandage to skeleton); spider grew from 2 sparse entries (one of which,
  `venom_sac`, had no item definition either) to 5; added an entirely new
  `mirage_phantom` table (previously nonexistent anywhere in the file, despite
  the monster existing) with spectral loot + a meaningful silver coin chance.
- Added 10 missing item definitions to `items.json` (`scarab_eye`, `scarab_shell`,
  `beetle_wing`, `sand_shimmer`, `shiny_pebble`, `venom_sac`, `spider_fang`,
  `embalming_salve`, `canopic_jar_fragment`, `rotten_linen`) — these item names
  were already referenced by loot tables (dune_scarab, spider, mummy) but had no
  entry in `items.json`, so `Inventory.get_item_definition()` returned empty and
  the drop couldn't be looted/displayed properly.
- Verified with a 300-trial-per-mob headless simulation of `roll_loot()`: dune
  trash mobs (rat/snake/slime/bat/dune_scarab) now have a 61–73% chance of
  *something* dropping (up from ~1 sparse entry each, often nothing); camp/undead
  mobs (goblin/bandit/skeleton/mummy/mirage_phantom) have an 89–100% chance of a
  drop and consistent coin (goblin ~10cp/kill avg, bandit ~15cp/kill avg, skeleton
  ~21cp/kill avg, mirage_phantom ~8cp/kill avg with silver mixed in) — a clear
  gradient from "light dune scavenging" to "worth farming the camps," matching
  typical EQ starting-zone loot pacing.

---

## 2026-09-14 — Session 27: The real monster-movement fix, guard's own animations, humanoid mobs, options menu

### Bug Fixes

**Monsters still didn't chase, even after Session 26's fixes — the actual root
cause** (`monster3d.gd`, `mob_spawner3d.gd`, `Scenes/lumora_outskirts3d.tscn`)
- Session 26 fixed two real bugs (add_threat() not forcing CHASE, and a too-
  short leash) but they weren't sufficient — a monster still stood
  completely frozen even under direct, sustained melee attack. Root-caused
  with an 8-second real-physics trace (not a single-frame snapshot, which is
  what let the Session 26 fix look "confirmed" when it wasn't): the zone's
  baked `NavigationMesh` had **zero vertices** — never actually baked,
  despite `NavigationRegion3D` being present and enabled. Every path query
  returned nothing, so `handle_movement()` (used for both PATROL and CHASE)
  always saw "no path" and did nothing at all — no movement, not even
  gravity. Re-baking directly failed at first too: the default cell_size of
  0.25m is sized for small indoor spaces, and tried to generate an
  impossibly large voxel grid for this ~180×120 unit outdoor zone ("Baking
  interrupted... would likely crash the engine"). Fixed by setting
  `geometry_parsed_geometry_type = STATIC_COLLIDERS` (bakes from the zone's
  own maintained collision shapes instead of GPU mesh data — faster, and
  what the bake warning itself recommended) and `cell_size`/`cell_height` to
  0.5, producing a real 466-vertex/391-polygon mesh, saved as
  `Data/lumora_outskirts_navmesh.tres` and wired into the scene as an
  external resource (replacing the broken empty embedded one).
- Second, deeper bug found once the navmesh was real: `mob_spawner3d.gd`
  hardcodes a flat Y per spawn entry (`lumora_outskirts_spawns.json`) that
  rarely matches the real terrain height at the randomized X/Z offset,
  leaving mobs floating above/below the ground. A floating mob's first path
  waypoint becomes a pure vertical correction (near-zero horizontal delta),
  and `handle_movement()`'s "basically no movement needed" early-return
  treated that as "arrived" — permanently frozen one layer deeper than the
  first bug. Fixed by snapping spawn position onto the navmesh via
  `NavigationServer3D.map_get_closest_point()` before the mob enters the tree.
- Third bug, found from the SAME 8-second trace after the above two were
  fixed: gravity itself was gated behind the identical "close enough, nothing
  to do" check that gated horizontal movement — so a monster whose
  navmesh-projected waypoint differed from its true resting height by more
  than a hair (the baked navmesh surface can legitimately sit above the
  actual collision floor by a bit) could never fall/settle to the height
  that check would consider "arrived": it needed gravity to reach that
  height, but gravity was gated behind reaching it. `handle_movement()`
  restructured so gravity/`move_and_slide()` run unconditionally every PATROL/
  CHASE tick, independent of whether there's horizontal nav movement to do.
- Fourth and final piece, from the SAME trace: even with all three fixed, a
  monster attacked from ~12m away would take one step then freeze again —
  `NavigationAgent3D.get_next_path_position()` doesn't advance to the next
  real waypoint until the agent is within `path_desired_distance` (was 0.5)
  of the current one, using full 3D (including Y) distance — and the
  navmesh-vs-floor height gap (measured up to ~0.7m on this terrain) alone
  already exceeded that, so the agent could wait forever to close a vertical
  gap it was never going to close. Bumped `path_desired_distance`/
  `target_desired_distance` to 1.5. Verified end-to-end with a real spawned
  bandit, hit from 6m, tracked for 8 full seconds of physics: closes
  distance, correctly switches to melee ATTACK on arrival, holds there.
- Also fixed while in here: leash removed entirely per explicit request (see
  New Features below), and monster chase speed doubled (also below) — both
  needed for the fix to actually feel complete once movement itself worked.

**Guard T-posed** (`guard_npc.gd`, `models/guard/guard_animations.res`)
- The FIRST guard-animation library build (this same session, superseded
  before ever reaching the user — see below) mixed two bone-naming
  conventions in one AnimationLibrary: idle/attack tracks pulled from the
  guard's own downloads use "mixamorig_<Bone>" (matching the Paladin model's
  actual skeleton), while walk/death tracks reused from the player's
  downloads use "mixamorig1_<Bone>". A single runtime skeleton-bone rename
  pass can only satisfy one convention, not both — so walk/death animated
  correctly while idle/attack silently found no matching bone and left the
  guard in bind pose (T-pose) whenever idle or mid-swing. Root-caused by
  inspecting the saved .res file's raw track path strings directly rather
  than re-guessing. Fixed at the build step instead of at runtime: walk/death
  tracks now have every track path rewritten from "mixamorig1_" to
  "mixamorig_" (the OPPOSITE direction — matching the guard's own unrenamed
  skeleton) before being saved into the library, so all four animations
  consistently target the guard's actual bone names and the runtime
  skeleton-rename function was removed entirely (no longer needed).

**Loot window's Loot/Ignore/Sell controls didn't render** (`corpse_loot_window.gd`)
- Confirmed from a screenshot: genuinely just bare text, no checkbox glyph
  at all, no theme override in the project to explain why. Replaced
  Godot's `CheckBox` with plain toggle-mode `Button`s carrying explicit
  stylebox overrides (dark/bordered when off, green/bordered when on) —
  self-drawn, not dependent on any theme icon resource. Same pattern reused
  for the new Options menu's Invert Look Y toggle.

**Currency drops were silent** (`monster3d.gd`)
- `_auto_process_loot()` (Session 26) auto-collects currency at death with
  no GameLog message at all — every other drop type logged something, coins
  logged nothing, which read as "almost no money dropping" even when it was
  working correctly. Fixed: now logs "You receive X Copper." (etc.) same as
  item pickups.

**Day/night transition had a bright flash at the horizon** (`day_night_cycle.gd`)
- At the exact instant phase flips between DAY and NIGHT, the sun/moon's
  rotation, color, AND energy all snapped simultaneously — the moon's fixed
  rotation/cool-blue color/dim energy vs. the sun's completely different
  "edge" angle/warm-orange color/energy, with no interpolation between them
  at the boundary itself (daylight-factor math was continuous, but sun vs.
  moon was a hard `if phase == DAY / else` switch). Rewrote `_apply_lighting()`
  so DAY-phase rotation/color/energy each crossfade toward the exact moon
  values as `daylight` approaches 0 (`moon_x.lerp(sun_x, daylight)`) instead
  of switching to a different fixed "sun edge" value — at daylight=0 this
  evaluates to precisely what NIGHT phase renders, on both sides of the
  boundary. Verified numerically at all 4 relevant timestamps (20:59, 21:00,
  05:59, 06:00): rotation/color/energy are now continuous to 3+ decimal
  places across both transitions, where they previously snapped.

### New Features

**Real per-mob-type 3D character models** (`monster3d.gd`, new
`_setup_humanoid_visual()`/`_update_animation()`/`_play_attack_animation()`)
- Bandit, skeleton, and goblin (`HUMANOID_MOB_TYPES` const) now use the same
  Mixamo model/animation system as the player instead of the shared
  placeholder red box every mob type previously rendered as
  (`monster_template.tscn`'s `BoxMesh`) — hides the box, swaps in
  `models/player/character.fbx`, resizes collision to a human-scale capsule
  (matching the player/guard fix), and reuses `player_animations.res` for
  idle/walk/run/attack/death, driven by the same velocity+state logic as
  guards. Other mob types (rat, spider, etc.) are unaffected — still the box.
  `Monster.die()`'s long-standing "TODO: AnimationPlayer death animation" is
  now implemented for these three (monsters generally still have none).

**Guard's own idle/attack animations** (`models/guard/`)
- User-downloaded Mixamo "Neutral Idle" and "Sword And Shield Attack",
  calibrated specifically to the Paladin model's own rest pose/proportions
  (reusing the player model's clips across models with different
  proportions doesn't reliably retarget — this was the actual reason the
  Paladin "didn't render properly" on the first pass with borrowed
  animations). `guard_animations.res` rebuilt from these two plus the
  existing walk/death (see Bug Fixes above for the track-naming fix).
  `_play_attack_animation()` now plays a single "attack" clip (was a random
  pick between two player-model swings that no longer apply here).

**Monster aggro announcement** (`monster3d.gd`, `change_state()`)
- The instant a monster enters CHASE targeting the player specifically
  (`get_current_target() == player` — not a pet or other aggro-table entry),
  logs e.g. "A Hooded Bandit attacks you!" using the existing
  `monster_description` field (already phrased naturally: "a hooded
  bandit"). Fires once per engagement (change_state()'s existing same-state
  early-return prevents re-firing on every subsequent hit while still in
  combat).

**No chase leash, by design** (`monster3d.gd`)
- Removed the distance-based give-up entirely from `state_chase()` per
  explicit request — EQ-style "once aggroed, chases until it or you die," so
  players can't train a mob through an area and shake it by outrunning a
  fixed leash radius. `MAX_CHASE_LEASH_DISTANCE` (added, then removed same
  session) is gone; `MAX_AGGRO_DISTANCE` (initial by-sight detection range)
  is untouched and still applies.

**Monster chase speed doubled while actively chasing** (`monster3d.gd`,
`CHASE_SPEED_MULTIPLIER`)
- Most base speeds (2.5–3.5 m/s) trailed the player's 5 m/s walk, so a
  "threatening" aggroed monster could be outrun at a stroll. `handle_movement()`
  now applies a 2x multiplier only in CHASE state (not PATROL, which stays
  at its normal ambient pace) — a bandit now closes at ~7 m/s.

**Auto-target the enemy that's hitting you** (`player3d.gd`, `monster3d.gd`)
- `take_damage()`/`apply_damage()` gained an optional `attacker` parameter;
  `monster3d.gd`'s attack resolution passes itself when the player is the
  target. Switches `current_target` (and announces it, same as Tab/click)
  whenever hit by someone you're not already targeting — skips re-announcing
  on repeated hits from the same attacker.

**Cooler level-up message** (`monster3d.gd`)
- "You have reached level X!" → "Fortune smiles upon you; your adventures
  have made you stronger! You are now level X."

**Options menu** (`pause_menu.gd` restructured, `global.gd`,
`camera_controller.gd`, new `default_bus_layout.tres`)
- Escape menu gained an "Options" button that swaps to a second panel
  (Back/Escape returns to the main pause menu rather than closing outright).
  Music Volume / Sound Volume sliders (0–100, live-applied on drag, saved on
  release), an Invert Look Y toggle (affects both mouselook and head-turn —
  one shared setting, not two), and a Loot Preferences list — every
  item_id → Loot/Ignore/Sell mapping with a per-item Reset button, so an
  accidental "Sell" doesn't require re-encountering the item to fix.
- New `Global.settings` (Dictionary: music_volume, sfx_volume,
  invert_look_y) persisted to `user://settings.json` — deliberately separate
  from `player_data`/character saves, since audio/control preferences are
  system-wide, not per-character, and need to exist even before any
  character is loaded.
- Real audio bus infrastructure added — the project had none before (single
  default Master bus). New `default_bus_layout.tres` (Master/Music/SFX,
  registered via `project.godot`'s `[audio]` section). Routed the only two
  LIVE sound sources — `torch.tscn` and `campfire.tscn`'s
  `AudioStreamPlayer3D`s — to "SFX", and `global_background_music.tscn` to
  "Music". (`magic_missile.tscn`, `ranged_attack.tscn`, `hand-torch.tscn` are
  all dead 2D-legacy code — confirmed via reference search before touching
  anything — so left unbused.)
- `Global.settings.get("invert_look_y")` now flips the vertical sign in both
  `camera_controller.gd`'s `_handle_free_look()` and `_handle_head_turn()`.

**Draggable + position-persistent windows, everywhere** (new
`Scripts/window_position.gd`, `player_frame.gd`, `target_frame.gd`,
`buff_bar.gd`, `corpse_loot_window.gd`, `character_sheet.gd`, `backpack_ui.gd`)
- `player_frame`/`target_frame` had no drag handler at all (the actual bug
  behind "I can't seem to move the player and target frames") — added,
